# Fork capability map

**What this is.** A complete, code-grounded map of everything this fork adds or changes
relative to upstream [Almamu/linux-wallpaperengine](https://github.com/Almamu/linux-wallpaperengine)
at base commit `b016d7d`. It exists so that upstream maintainers and other developers can
see every capability at a glance, find its code in under a minute, and judge what it would
take to cherry-pick.

**How it was made.** Every claim in this document was produced by reading the fork's actual
source and diffing it against the upstream base. Every claim carries a `file::token` anchor
into the publication tree.
Anything that could not be determined from code is explicitly marked **Uncertain**. Where a
code comment and the code disagreed, the code won and the disagreement is noted.

**Conventions.** All paths are relative to the repository root. Anchors (`File.ext::token`: a symbol, env var, or log tag in the named file) refer to this
repository's tree. "Upstream" means commit `b016d7d`. `src/External/` is vendored
third-party code and out of scope except where the build wires it in.

## The shape of the delta

- ~135 upstream files modified, plus two whole new engine directories
  (`src/WallpaperEngine/Api/`, `src/WallpaperEngine/WebHelper/`) and ~34 other new engine
  files (new render objects, mip residency, overlay label, animation timeline, instrument
  registry, localStorage, pointer gate, null audio driver, sha256, new binaries:
  `lwe-web-service`, `lwe-web-helper`, `lwe_bc7enc`, the web-frame probe tool).
- The entire `lwe-ui/` control panel is fork-new (upstream has no UI component).
- Vendored dependencies under `src/External/` (previously upstream submodules, plus the fork-added
  ISPCTextureCompressor) are third-party code and not counted.

## The one-paragraph version

The fork does four big things. (1) It turns the engine into a **daemon**: a same-uid-only
Unix socket speaks a validated 30-verb JSON protocol (`show`, `rotate-set`, `playlist-set`, `lanes-set`, `next/prev`,
`set-*`, `release/acquire-outputs`), the Wayland event loop is rebuilt around a bounded
`poll()` that services commands even when no frames render, output lifecycle is a
state machine driven by a deadman switch, fullscreen policy, a running-apps rule, and
explicit verbs, and the daemon persists its own state and restores it on boot so a
restart needs no client. (2) It
**removes CEF from the engine process**: web wallpapers run in a supervised
`lwe-web-service` binary that exists only while a web wallpaper does, with frames crossing
a versioned shared-memory ring. (3) It closes a large slice of **rendering parity**:
scene lighting with shadow mapping, 3D model objects, skeletal puppet animation, particle
child systems and events, perspective cameras, fog, HDR bloom - features
upstream stubs or drops. (4) It makes the engine **cheap to run all day**: FBO pooling,
coverage-sized composites, mip-residency capping, offline BC7 texture compression,
lazy audio, and a stop-the-engine policy for fullscreen or listed apps that hands back
all of it on the Wayland desktop driver (elsewhere stop pauses). On top of all of it sits `lwe-ui/`,
a control panel (a small resident tray process plus an on-demand window) that is the
daemon API's reference client.

1. [Daemon mode and the command API](#1-daemon-mode-and-the-command-api)

# 1. Daemon mode and the command API


### Unix-domain command socket (transport layer)
- **What it does**: A non-blocking AF_UNIX/SOCK_STREAM server that accepts line-framed requests and writes line-framed replies on the same connection. `listen()` doubles as a single-instance guard: an existing socket file is probed with `connect()` - a live listener means refusal to start (never unlinking a live instance's socket), only `ECONNREFUSED` is treated as a stale file and removed (CommandServer.cpp::listen). `drain()` accepts all pending connections and buffers partial lines per client until newline; CRLF tolerated, blank lines ignored (CommandServer.cpp::drain). `respond()` retries a wedged reader in 50 ms poll slices up to a 500 ms total budget, then drops the client so a slow reader can never hang the render loop (CommandServer.cpp::respond).
- **Where it lives**: fork-only new file `src/WallpaperEngine/Api/CommandServer.{h,cpp}`; destructor closes all fds and removes the socket file only if this instance created it and the path is still the socket it bound: an `lstat` must show a socket with the device and inode recorded right after `bind` (`m_ownsSocketFile`, CommandServer.cpp::m_ownsSocketFile, flag set at CommandServer.cpp::m_ownsSocketFile; identity in CommandServer.cpp::m_socketInode).
- **Surface**: path resolution `--socket` -> `$LWE_SOCKET` -> `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/engine.sock` -> `/tmp/lwe-<euid>/engine.sock`, resolved by Config at static init and again after the command line is parsed (Application/Config.cpp::LWE_SOCKET, Application/Config.cpp::setFlags) and returned by `defaultSocketPath` (CommandServer.cpp::defaultSocketPath). Limits: `MAX_LINE_BYTES = 64*1024`, `MAX_CLIENTS = 8` (CommandServer.h::MAX_LINE_BYTES). Backlog = MAX_CLIENTS (CommandServer.cpp::MAX_CLIENTS).
- **Coupling**: fully self-contained module (POSIX sockets + `Log.h`); nothing upstream touched. Lifts alone with its CMake entry (CMakeLists.txt::COMMON_SOURCES) plus the caller hooks in WallpaperApplication.
- **Tests**: `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Cases/CommandSocket.cpp` - 8 cases: 0600 socket / 0700 dir perms (CommandSocket.cpp::socketStat), live-instance refusal (CommandSocket.cpp::displace), stale-socket reclaim (CommandSocket.cpp::reclaims), line framing/split-write/CRLF/blank-line (CommandSocket.cpp::drainUntil), 64 KiB overflow drop (CommandSocket.cpp::waitForClients), respond round-trip (CommandSocket.cpp::responds), `fds()` growth (CommandSocket.cpp::waitForClients), `LWE_SOCKET`/`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` path resolution (CommandSocket.cpp::LWE_SOCKET).

### Socket security model
- **What it does**: Three concentric controls. (1) Directory created `0700` - but only if the server created it, so a custom path in a shared dir isn't rewritten (CommandServer.cpp::listen). (2) Socket bound under `umask(0177)` and then explicitly `chmod 0600` (CommandServer.cpp::listen). (3) Every accepted peer is verified with `SO_PEERCRED` and rejected unless `peer.uid == geteuid()` (CommandServer.cpp::SO_PEERCRED). The same credentials feed an audit trail: `peerDescription()` (CommandServer.cpp::SO_PEERCRED) names the requester (pid + /proc comm), and the engine logs it on every `release-outputs`/`acquire-outputs` - the verbs that blank or restore the desktop. A client that never sends a newline is dropped at 64 KiB buffered (CommandServer.cpp::drain); the 8-client cap refuses further connections (CommandServer.cpp::drain).
- **Where it lives**: same fork-only files as above (`authenticatePeer`, CommandServer.cpp::authenticatePeer; called from `drain` at CommandServer.cpp::authenticatePeer).
- **Surface**: no flag changes the security posture; it is hard-coded. `--socket` relocates the socket the same way `$LWE_SOCKET` does, and either can move it into a less-safe directory - the 0600 socket mode is then the only filesystem control, because the `0700` directory fix-up applies only to a directory the server created itself (CommandServer.cpp::listen). A file already at the socket path is removed only when it is a socket that nobody answers; any other file refuses the listen with `<path> exists and is not a socket; refusing to replace it` and nothing is removed (CommandServer.cpp::listen). The web helper's listener follows the same rule and sets `0700` only on a directory it created (MessageChannel.cpp::listen).
- **Coupling**: self-contained; same module as above.
- **Tests**: CommandSocket.cpp perms case (CommandSocket.cpp::socketStat) and overflow-drop case (CommandSocket.cpp::waitForClients); `Testing/Cases/SocketListeners.cpp` covers, for both listeners, anything at the path that is not a socket being refused and left, an entry saved over the socket surviving exit, the listener's own socket removed at exit, and another listener's socket at the path (bound after an unlink, or renamed over it) left in place and answering; for the web helper it also covers a stale socket replaced, a live one refused and its folder mode. CommandServer's stale and live cases are in CommandSocket.cpp (CommandSocket.cpp::reclaims, CommandSocket.cpp::displace). The uid check itself has no test (UNCERTAIN: exercised only implicitly since tests run same-uid).

### Wire protocol + dispatcher (validation layer)
- **What it does**: One JSON object per line: `{"id": int, "cmd": verb, "args": {...}}` (schema documented in CommandDispatcher.h::cmd). `parse()` rejects non-objects, missing/non-integer `id`, missing/non-string `cmd`, non-object `args`, and any verb not in a 30-entry whitelist (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::KNOWN_VERBS). Before JSON parsing it scans bracket depth and refuses nesting > 64 to stop stack exhaustion in nlohmann's recursive parser (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse); JSON is parsed with exceptions disabled (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). Background ids must match `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}` - no dots, no separators, so no wire input can be path-shaped (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validBackgroundId; the rejection deliberately does not echo the value back, Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). Response builders emit `accepted` / `done(result)` / `failure(id, message)` (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse).
- **Where it lives**: fork-only `src/WallpaperEngine/Api/CommandDispatcher.{h,cpp}`. Pure parsing/validation, no engine or socket dependency (by design, CommandDispatcher.h::WallpaperEngine::Api).
- **Surface** - the 30 verbs and their validated argument contracts (all bounds enforced in `parse`, `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp`):
  - `status`, `quit`, `list-objects`, `next`, `prev`, `ping`, `pause`, `resume`, `release-outputs`, `acquire-outputs` - no args.
  - `show` - requires string `args.id` (validBackgroundId); optional `cc` [b,c,s 0..4, hue +/-6.4] (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `speed` 0..20 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `properties` <=64 entries, keys `[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,64}`, string values <=256 chars, number/bool/string only (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `scaling` stretch/fit/fill/default (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `clamp` clamp/border/repeat (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `ssfactor` and `clampcomposites` finite numbers no greater than 4 (0 or below off; a show carrying `res` is refused), `texcomp` bool, `texdetail` auto/full - the quality switches read at scene load (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `volume` int 0..128 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), booleans `audio_processing`/`mouse`/`automute`/`fullscreen_pause` (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `fullscreen_behavior` off/pause/stop (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `skip_objects` <=256 ints 0..1e6 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `skip_effects` <=64 ints 0..1e7 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `ui_id` string <=128 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs), `fit` object with `zoom` 1..2 and `pan_x`/`pan_y` -1..1, all finite (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateFit).
  - `rotate-set` - `entries` <=512, each an object with valid `id` plus the full show vocabulary (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse); `interval_s` int 15..604800 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse); `order` sequential/shuffle/static, random accepted for one release (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse); booleans `avoid_repeat`/`enabled` (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse); `label` <=128 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse).
  - `set-skip` - `ids` <=256 ints 0..1e6 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-fps` - int 1..480 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-speed` - 0..20 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-tuning` - at least one of `classic_k`/`classic_exp`/`audio_gain`/`audio_smooth`, finite (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-volume` - int 0..128 (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-mouse`/`set-audio`/`set-parallax`/`set-particles` - bool `enabled` (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-instrument` - string `name` + bool `enabled` (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-overlay` - at least one of `text` (<=512 printable ASCII chars and newlines), `corner` (top-left/top-right/bottom-left/bottom-right), bool `visible` (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-fullscreen-ignore` - `app_ids` <=128 non-empty strings <=128 chars (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-fullscreen` - `behavior` in off/pause/stop, required because the handler indexes it directly (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-app-conditions` - required `behavior` off/pause/stop, optional `names` <=128 strings of 1..64 chars, matched literally against `/proc/PID/comm` (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse). `set-fit` - optional string `lane` <=64, at least one of `zoom` 1..2 / `pan_x` / `pan_y` -1..1, finite (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateFit).
- **Coupling**: self-contained (depends only on nlohmann/json). Lifts alone.
- **Tests**: `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp` - 13 cases covering valid parses (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), malformed-input rejections with parseable JSON error bodies (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), id echo/null (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), hostile path-shaped ids incl. `../../etc/passwd`, `$(rm -rf ~)`, 65-char overrun (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), cc/speed bounds (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), properties validation incl. the 64-entry cap (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), show-requires-id (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), set-skip/skip_effects (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), show vocabulary (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), fit window on show, set-fit and lanes-set (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), rotation/transport verbs incl. set-fps and set-fullscreen edge values (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp), and response-builder shapes (Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp).

### Accepted-then-done command handling
- **What it does**: Short commands reply once with `done`; long commands (`show`, `next`, `prev`) reply `accepted` immediately, then `done` or `failure` with the same id when the multi-second scene rebuild finishes, so clients can distinguish "never heard you" from "working on it" (schema comment, CommandDispatcher.h::show). The dispatch point is `processApiRequests()` -> `CommandDispatcher::parse` -> `handleApiCommand` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand); parse rejections get the errorResponse written straight back; in daemon mode, after every mutating verb the drain loop re-persists the runtime state (WallpaperApplication.cpp::processApiRequests). Every handler branch responds; a final catch-all answers "verb accepted but not implemented" if the whitelist and the handler chain ever drift (WallpaperApplication.cpp::accepted).
- **Where it lives**: modified upstream `WallpaperApplication.cpp`: `handleApiCommand` WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand; ack sites at WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand (next/prev) and WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiShow (show).
- **Coupling**: deeply woven into WallpaperApplication - every handler reads/writes `m_context.settings`, `m_renderContext`, `m_fullScreenDetector`, or the instrument registry. See per-verb notes below.

### `show` - all-outputs hot swap with rollback and history
- **What it does**: Resolves the id against `$XDG_DATA_HOME/lwe/wallpapers`, `$HOME/.local/share/lwe/wallpapers` (both requiring `<id>/project.json`), then Steam workshop app 431960 - never a raw path (`resolveLibraryBackground`, WallpaperApplication.cpp::resolveLibraryBackground). Preflights by parsing project.json leniently and requiring `type`+`file` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::preflightWallpaper), re-acquires outputs if released, makes any viewport current, acks, then `applyShowCore` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyShowCore): clears skip-sets, applies args (cc and speed, which reset to the launch values when omitted, properties, volume/audio/mouse/automute, fullscreen policy, scaling/clamp written into per-screen maps BEFORE rebuild because they key mirror groups, the wallpaper layer of the fit window into the lane's `Look::fit` (Lane.h::Look), identity when omitted, WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyShowCore), assigns the new path to every non-span screen, and rebuilds everything via `rebuildForCurrentBackgrounds` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::rebuildForCurrentBackgrounds: clearWallpapers -> clear projects -> evict sole-owner texture cache -> loadBackgrounds -> setupProperties -> ensure browser/audio per project -> buildWallpapers -> `malloc_trim`). On exception it restores 14 previous settings and rebuilds the old set (WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyShowCore). Success pushes the previous full show record (id + ui_id + args) onto a 100-deep history deque (WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyShowCore) and makes the new record, with the opaque `ui_id` echo, the lane's `current` (Api/Lane.cpp::recordShow). A manual show restarts the rotation countdown (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiShow).
- **Where it lives**: modified `WallpaperApplication.cpp`: `apiShow` WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiShow, `applyShowCore` WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyShowCore, `rebuildForCurrentBackgrounds` WallpaperApplication.cpp::rebuildForCurrentBackgrounds, `resolveLibraryBackground` WallpaperApplication.cpp::resolveLibraryBackground. Launch defaults snapshot `m_showDefaults` captured in ctor (WallpaperApplication.cpp::m_showDefaults); omitted args fall back to it, not to current values.
- **Coupling**: deeply woven - touches loadBackgrounds (now skips screens with empty path when no defaultBackground, WallpaperApplication.cpp::loadBackgrounds), preset/dependency resolution in `loadBackground` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::loadBackground, project.json without `type` but with `dependency` recursively loads the base, depth-capped at 4, then applies `preset` property overrides), `buildWallpapers` mirror-group keying (WallpaperApplication.cpp::buildWallpapers), `parseLenient` JSON (also in preflight and ApplicationContext config load), and `RenderContext::evictUnusedTextures`. A porter needs the whole rebuild chain, not just `apiShow`.
- **Tests**: none directly (dispatcher-side arg validation only).

### Lane model (`playlist-set`, `lanes-set`, `rotate-set`, `next`, `prev`, timed advance)
- **What it does**: `apiRotateSet` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiRotateSet) replaces the whole set; `enabled` requires entries non-empty (WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyRotateSet). Disabling freezes the countdown where it stands (`frozenRemainingMs`, the remainder at the pause to the millisecond, 0 when already overdue; the state file writes `frozen_remaining_ms` and still reads an older `frozen_remaining_s`); static has no countdown at all and leaving it starts a full interval (Api/Lane.cpp::nextInSeconds); a new wallpaper while paused, by `show`, `next` or `prev`, makes the frozen remainder the full interval (Api/Lane.cpp::restartCountdown); re-enabling the SAME set (same display-id list + interval) resumes the clock via a backdated `lastShow` rather than insta-rotating (WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyRotateSet). A push of the same set in the same enabled state is a re-push, not a new play: the clock runs on and the forward history stays (Api/Lane.cpp::applySet); a changed set or interval restarts it and drops forward targets; an enabled change keeps forward and follows the freeze and resume rules. A `show` of the wallpaper already on screen (an editor or popup re-show) leaves the clock and any frozen remainder alone; a show of another wallpaper restamps them (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiShow). Picking: the walk item after the cursor, wrapping, with `random` a synonym for `shuffle` for one release (Api/Lane.cpp::pickNext); `avoidRepeat` (default true, Api/Lane.cpp::applySet) skips the on-screen display id once, where display id = `ui_id` when present so presets sharing a base wallpaper still count as distinct (Api/Lane.cpp::displayId). `status.rotation.next_up` names the item forward would show: forward history first, else the walk's next (Api/Lane.cpp::aheadUp). `tickApiRotation` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::tickApiRotation) advances when the interval elapses (and in daemon mode persists the runtime state on success), skips entirely while outputs are released (clock keeps counting; overdue advance fires on first tick after acquire), and re-arms a full interval on failure. Advance tries each entry up to set-size attempts, skipping entries that fail resolve/preflight/apply (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiRotationAdvance). `next`/`prev` are manual transports: ack-then-done; `next` steps into forward history else advances the walk, `prev` steps back through history else to the previous walk item, both applied with `recordHistory=false` and booked afterwards (WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand); in static `prev` never reads history: it steps to the previous walk item and holds there (Api/Lane.cpp::backTarget), and back is available whenever the walk has more than one item (Api/Lane.cpp::backEnabled); a history entry that no longer resolves is dropped from the books (WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand).
- **Where it lives**: rotation state is data in `Api/Lane.h`: a `Playlist` (entries, order, interval), a `Group` (screens by make, model, serial and connector name) and a `Lane` that binds one group to one playlist and owns the walk, the bounded history (100), the timer, the fit and the look (Api/Lane.h::Lane). The pick, the same-set countdown rules and the status block are free functions (Api/Lane.cpp::pickNext, Api/Lane.cpp::applySet, Api/Lane.cpp::laneStatus). The application holds one lane bound to every screen (WallpaperApplication.h::m_lanes) and its playlists and groups beside it; `rotate-set` is a shim onto playlist `default` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyRotateSet). `playlist-set` stores a playlist by slug in numbered parts (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiPlaylistSet); `lanes-set` binds the lane, records the group and an optional fit (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiLanesSet); the schedule is engine state (Api/Lane.h::Schedule): `schedule-set` stores enabled plus entries in sent order, the first where day begins and the second where it ends (Api/Lane.cpp::scheduleSlugAt, Api/Lane.cpp::scheduleIsDay); `tickSchedule` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::tickSchedule) compares the local minute with the boundaries every loop pass (Api/Lane.cpp::scheduleTick), and a crossed boundary marks the switch PENDING: it applies when the countdown expires or on the next `next`/`prev`, and at once when the lane's playlist is static (no countdown to wait for); the switch binds the lane and shows the new playlist's next pick, or its FIRST scene when that playlist is static (WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyPendingSchedule), a new play (clock restarts, forward drops). While the schedule is on, `lanes-set` honours a playlist change only with `manual: true`, which holds until the next boundary of any entry; a policy push keeps the engine's binding (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiLanesSet). Status carries `schedule` {enabled, entries, active, is_day, next_boundary_s, held, pending}; the state file carries the block (Api/Lane.cpp::scheduleFromJson). `show`, `next` and `prev` take an optional `lane`. The lane walks a stored order by an identity-anchored cursor: the pick is the walk item after the cursor, wrapping, the on-screen id skipped once (Api/Lane.cpp::pickNext); a push re-seats the cursor by identity and never restarts a shuffle cycle (Api/Lane.cpp::applySet); shuffle draws the following cycle when the cursor lands on the last item so `next_up` stays known (Api/Lane.cpp::nextUp); in static a click seats the cursor on the clicked item (Api/Lane.cpp::seatCursor). History is browser-style per lane: `prev` steps into history, else to the previous walk item wrapping, and in static always steps to the previous walk item, never into history (Api/Lane.cpp::backTarget); `next` steps into forward history when any, else advances the walk (Api/Lane.cpp::forwardTarget); the timer moves a backed-up lane to its newest item before advancing (Api/Lane.cpp::jumpToEnd); a show of the id on screen adds no entry and any new show discards forward history (Api/Lane.cpp::recordShow); status `lanes[].previous/next/back_enabled` follow the same rules (Api/Lane.cpp::previousUp). Order `static` never advances on the timer (Api/Lane.cpp::dueForAdvance). Status carries `lanes[]` and `groups[]` beside the unchanged `rotation` block. Runtime state is version 2 (lanes, playlists, groups); a version 1 file loads as one lane (Api/Lane.cpp::fromLegacyState).
- **Coupling**: sits entirely on top of `applyShowCore`/`resolveLibraryBackground`; self-contained given those.
- **Tests**: the lane unit (Testing/Cases/LaneState.cpp) covers the walk, the countdown rules, the history bound, the version 1 upgrade and the status block; the schedule unit (Testing/Cases/Schedule.cpp) covers the entry in force across midnight, boundary crossing and wrap, the manual hold, and the state round trip; the transport unit (Testing/Cases/LaneHistory.cpp) covers back, forward, the detour, the re-show and the timer's jump; the dispatcher shapes of the verbs are covered in Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse.

### Output release/acquire, deadman switch, fullscreen-stop gate
- **What it does**: A `ReleaseReason { Live, Verb, Deadman, Fullscreen, AppCondition }` state machine (WallpaperApplication.h::ReleaseReason). `apiReleaseOutputs` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiReleaseOutputs) first refuses, with "driver does not support releasing outputs" and nothing torn down, on a driver that cannot release its output surfaces (`VideoDriver.h::canReleaseOutputSurfaces` is false by default and true only in `WaylandOpenGLDriver.h::canReleaseOutputSurfaces`); otherwise it tears down GL first while a surface still holds the context current (scenes, decoders, cached textures die -> VRAM freed), then asks the driver to destroy layer surfaces; failure rebuilds and reports. Idempotent, and a Verb hold cannot be downgraded to Deadman (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiReleaseOutputs). After a successful release, `evictResidentPages` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::evictResidentPages) trims the heap and `MADV_DONTNEED`s every fully-CLEAN read-only private file mapping, so a released engine's RSS drops to skeleton size (~60 MB) instead of parking hundreds of MB of resident-but-reclaimable library pages; only mappings with zero dirty bytes are dropped (RELRO segments are read-only yet hold relocated data - dropping one is a crash), and the pages refault from page cache on acquire. `apiAcquireOutputs` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiAcquireOutputs) re-creates surfaces then rebuilds wallpapers. `tickDeadman` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::tickDeadman): if a ping was ever seen, and BOTH no ping and no render for `m_deadmanSeconds`, it releases outputs (orphan reflex); a failed release re-arms by faking a ping. On a driver that cannot release its output surfaces it logs once per stall that it cannot free the screens and does nothing more; a recent ping or frame re-arms that line (WallpaperApplication.cpp::m_deadmanCannotReleaseLogged). A `ping` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand) refreshes the heartbeat AND re-acquires outputs if the hold is Deadman - explicitly why Verb holds can't be downgraded. `tickFullscreenGate` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::tickFullscreenGate): when the policy in effect is `FullscreenBehavior::Stop` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::fullscreenBehaviorInEffect; a live Stop on a driver that cannot release its output surfaces is in effect as Pause) and the detector reports fullscreen, shed outputs; re-acquire the moment it clears; never touches Verb/Deadman holds. `tickAppCondition` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::tickAppCondition): a 3-second `/proc/PID/comm` poll for the running-apps rule (`set-app-conditions`, names + off/pause/stop) - pause drives timescale 0 and restores the prior speed, stop releases with reason `app` and re-acquires when no listed process remains, and on a driver that cannot release its output surfaces stop pauses as pause does (the same rule, `Application/FullscreenPolicy.cpp::inEffect`); each mechanism only undoes what it engaged. `processApiRequests`, the four policy ticks (fullscreen gate, app condition, rotation, deadman), the property-reload check, the boot-survival mark, and the web-helper pump all run at the top of every main-loop pass in `show()` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::show), before `render()` - so commands are serviced even while paused or released.
- **Where it lives**: WallpaperApplication.cpp as cited; driver half below.
- **Surface**: `m_deadmanSeconds` default 300 (Application/Config.cpp::LWE_DEADMAN), overridden by `$LWE_DEADMAN` as Config reads it (0..86400; 0 disables, Application/Config.cpp::LWE_DEADMAN). Verbs `release-outputs`/`acquire-outputs` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand), `ping`, `set-fullscreen` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand, updates both live settings and `m_showDefaults` so later shows don't resurrect the old policy), `set-app-conditions` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand; conditions persist with the runtime state). `FullscreenBehavior` enum in ApplicationContext.h::Stop; default `Pause` (ApplicationContext.h::FullscreenBehavior in fork's designated init); `--no-fullscreen-pause` now also writes `fullscreenBehavior = Off` (ApplicationContext.cpp::FullscreenBehavior); `--daemon` defaults `Stop` - the engine owns the policy; persisted state and verbs override (ApplicationContext.cpp::Stop).
- **Coupling**: requires the three new `VideoDriver` virtuals (`canReleaseOutputSurfaces`/`releaseOutputSurfaces`/`acquireOutputSurfaces`, default `false` - VideoDriver.h fork addition) and the pause-policy rewiring in `render()` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::m_manualPauseRequested: pause decision now gates on the policy in effect being Pause (`fullscreenBehaviorInEffect`), live and per-frame, plus `m_manualPauseRequested`; upstream's `render()` had no policy gate, and `pauseOnFullscreen` only chose the driver once at startup).
- **Tests**: dispatcher-shape only (Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse) for release-outputs/acquire-outputs/ping/set-fullscreen; no behavioral coverage of the release, acquire, deadman or fullscreen-gate logic itself.

### Runtime state persistence + crash-loop guard
- **What it does**: In daemon mode, every mutating verb re-persists the durable runtime state - current show (id, ui_id, full args), the rotation set with per-entry args, pause, fps, volume, audio/mouse/parallax/particles toggles, speed, color correction, fullscreen policy + ignore list, tuning globals, and the app conditions - to `$XDG_STATE_HOME/lwe/engine/engine-state.json` via temp+rename (`persistRuntimeState`, WallpaperApplication.cpp::persistRuntimeState; the drain-loop hook WallpaperApplication.cpp::processApiRequests). Every successful SCHEDULED rotation advance persists too (`tickApiRotation`, WallpaperApplication.cpp::persistRuntimeState), so a crash or restart restores the wallpaper that was actually on screen, not the one at the last verb. An idle daemon boot (`--daemon`, no CLI backgrounds) restores it by replaying the settings writes a client makes, rebuilding the rotation from the file (a version 2 file's playlists, groups, lane and schedule; a version 1 file through `Api::fromLegacyState` and `Api::applySet` onto playlist `default`, Api/Lane.cpp::fromLegacyState), then resolve/preflight/`applyShowCore` for the lane's current show (`restoreRuntimeState`, WallpaperApplication.cpp::REFUSED). Boot survival is tracked in `boot-history.json`: each boot appends `survived:false` and flips it after 60 s of uptime, or at once on a stop the operator asked for (SIGTERM/SIGINT reach `signal`, which marks the boot clean before the loop unwinds), so a run of operator restarts never counts as a crash loop (`markBootSurvived`, WallpaperApplication.cpp::markBootSurvived); when the last two recorded boots died young, restore is REFUSED and the engine boots idle, naming the re-arm path (delete the history file) in its log, and `status` reports `restore_refused` true until the first wallpaper the engine shows after that, whether a show, next, prev, a rotation or schedule advance, or a legacy playlist advance put it there (`Application/BootGuard.cpp::evaluate`; cleared in WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyShowCore and WallpaperApplication.cpp::advancePlaylist). Each lane entry carries `remaining_s` and the file carries `saved_at` (unix seconds), so a restart resumes a running lane's countdown less the downtime, overdue lanes advancing on the first tick; a file without them restores the full interval (Api/Lane.cpp::resumeCountdown).
- **Where it lives**: fork-only additions to `WallpaperApplication.{h,cpp}`; state dir helper `runtimeStateDir` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::runtimeStateDir) honors `$XDG_STATE_HOME` and never touches `~/.config/lwe` (the panel owns it).
- **Surface**: the state file and `boot-history.json`; no new verbs or env vars. A boot with CLI-specified backgrounds never restores.
- **Coupling**: reads/writes the same members the verb handlers use; portable with the daemon layer as a unit.
- **Tests**: exercised live (restart restores in seconds with zero clients; triple-SIGKILL produces the refusal); no Catch2 case yet.

### Running-apps condition (engine-side)
- **What it does**: `set-app-conditions {names:[/proc comm strings], behavior: off|pause|stop}` arms a 3-second poll of `/proc/PID/comm` (`tickAppCondition`, WallpaperApplication.cpp::tickAppCondition). While a listed process exists: `pause` sets timescale 0 (the master-pause fact) and restores the prior speed on release; `stop` releases the outputs with `ReleaseReason::AppCondition` (status reason `app`) and re-acquires when no listed process remains; on a driver that cannot release its output surfaces `stop` pauses the same way `pause` does (Application/FullscreenPolicy.cpp::inEffect). Process existence is the only honest signal for windowless CLI processes (a local LLM holding the VRAM is the motivating case). Holds owned by a bench, the deadman, or the fullscreen gate are never stolen; each mechanism only undoes what it engaged. Conditions persist with the runtime state.
- **Where it lives**: handler WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand, tick as cited, validation `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`, status block `app_condition` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiStatus).
- **Surface**: the verb; `status.app_condition {behavior, count, engaged}`.
- **Coupling**: uses the release/acquire machinery and `setTimescale`; needs both.
- **Tests**: exercised live (pause and stop legs against a probe process); no Catch2 case yet. Names match EVERY process comm - clients should send specific names (the kernel caps comm at 15 chars).

### Wallpaper console rate-limit
- **What it does**: `console.log`/`console.error` from wallpaper scripts collapse identical messages to one line per 10 s window, with the suppressed count appended on the next emission (`ConsoleObject.cpp::CONSOLE_SUPPRESS_WINDOW`). A broken wallpaper logging per frame (~540 lines/s measured) previously blew past journald's rate limit and suppressed the engine's own diagnostics. The distinct-message map is capped at 512 and cleared wholesale so a message-varying wallpaper cannot grow it.
- **Where it lives**: `Scripting/ConsoleObject.cpp`, file-local.
- **Surface**: none; behavior only.
- **Coupling**: none. Lifts alone.
- **Tests**: none.

### Poll-based Wayland event loop + API wake-up
- **What it does**: Upstream's blocking `wl_display_dispatch` is replaced by a proper prepare_read/flush/poll/read_events/dispatch_pending cycle (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::POLLNVAL). The poll set is the Wayland display fd PLUS every API fd from `WallpaperApplication::getApiWakeFds()` (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::dispatchEventQueue; app side WallpaperApplication.cpp::getApiWakeFds) - so a socket command wakes the loop even with no frame callbacks arriving. Timeout is bounded always: 100 ms with the API up, 2000 ms without (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::dispatchEventQueue). `POLLERR|POLLHUP|POLLNVAL` on the display fd (dead compositor) requests exit instead of spinning (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::POLLNVAL). Frame callbacks no longer render inline from the listener: the callback just sets `framePending` (WaylandOutputViewport.cpp::framePending) and the dispatch loop renders each pending viewport ONCE per pass (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::dispatchEventQueue).
- **Where it lives**: modified upstream `src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Drivers/WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp` (`dispatchEventQueue` WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::dispatchEventQueue), `WaylandOutputViewport.{h,cpp}` (`framePending` flag WaylandOutputViewport.h::framePending, callback WaylandOutputViewport.cpp::surfaceFrameCallback). New virtual hooks on `VideoDriver.h` (VideoDriver.h::releaseOutputSurfaces in fork). Only the Wayland driver calls `getApiWakeFds` (grep-verified: single call site, WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::dispatchEventQueue).
- **Surface**: FPS cap read fresh each pass via `std::max(1, maximumFPS)` (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::dispatchEventQueue) - this is what makes `set-fps` live. The cap paces SCENE RENDERS, not loop passes: before updating a viewport the driver asks whether that update will run the scene (WallpaperApplication.cpp::willRenderScene, RenderContext.cpp::willRenderScene, CWallpaper.cpp::sceneRenderPending - the mirror-group owner, or a non-mirror wallpaper not yet rendered this pass) and sleeps once per pass, before the first such update, for the remainder of `1/cap` since the previous pass's first scene render (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::paceRender); the pacer keeps a steady-clock time point and measures the gap in double precision, so the cap does not drift with uptime the way float seconds since start did. Later scene updates in the same pass and viewports that merely present a shared frame never sleep. Upstream slept `1/cap - elapsed` on every pass, including passes that rendered nothing, which held a heavy scene on three outputs to two thirds of its cap. Two counters: the pass counter advances every loop pass and keys the once-per-pass scene render (VideoDriver.h::getPassCounter), the frame counter advances only when a scene rendered and is what `status` reports as `frames` (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::getFrameCounter), so a measured fps now counts scene renders rather than loop passes (the old counter read double on a three-output mirror group). The GLFW driver paces and counts per pass, since it renders every pass (GLFWOpenGLDriver.cpp::getPassCounter).
- **Coupling**: confined to the Wayland driver + viewport + the two VideoDriver base virtuals + `getApiWakeFds` on the app. A porter needs those four pieces; the loop is otherwise self-contained.
- **Uncertain**: on X11/GLFW no driver polls API fds, so command latency there depends on that driver's own loop cadence - `processApiRequests` is non-blocking and runs every pass (WallpaperApplication.cpp::processApiRequests), X11/GLFW loop timing is not characterized here.

### Render keepalive + DPMS safety
- **What it does**: `WallpaperApplication::update()` stamps an atomic `m_lastRender` tick on every serviced frame (WallpaperApplication.cpp::m_lastRender). If `secondsSinceLastRender() > 2.0` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::secondsSinceLastRender), the driver re-kicks EVERY viewport in the render set (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::secondsSinceLastRender) - this restarts the frame-callback chain after DPMS-on, hotplug, or a compositor that stopped scheduling callbacks; the always-bounded poll timeout guarantees the check runs (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::dispatchEventQueue). Separately, `makeCurrent` forces `eglSwapInterval(0)` once per surface (WaylandOutputViewport.cpp::makeCurrent) so `eglSwapBuffers` can never block on a DPMS-off output and wedge the single-threaded loop (which would take the command socket down with it - comment in code). Viewports without surfaces drop out of the render set because `WaylandOutput::updateViewports` skips `layerSurface == nullptr` (WaylandOutput.cpp::updateViewports).
- **Where it lives**: WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::dispatchEventQueue; WaylandOutputViewport.cpp::swapIntervalConfigured (`swapIntervalConfigured` flag, WaylandOutputViewport.h::swapIntervalConfigured); WallpaperApplication.cpp::secondsSinceLastRender.
- **Coupling**: small and separable - keepalive needs `m_lastRender` stamp + `secondsSinceLastRender`; swap-interval needs the flag + 3 lines in `makeCurrent`.
- **Tests**: none.

### Output hotplug / hot-unplug
- **What it does**: Add: upstream's `handleGlobalRemoved` TODO is implemented - registry removal matching a viewport's `waylandName` routes to `onLayerClose` (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::handleGlobalRemoved), which clears `viewportInFocus` if it names the dying viewport (dangling-deref fix for missing pointer-leave on unplug, WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::onLayerClose), runs the viewport's own surface teardown, frame callback and cursor objects included, then destroys the xdg output and `wl_output` (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::onLayerClose), erases the screen, resets the output set (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::onLayerClose). Remove->add: `wl_output.done` now calls `onOutputAnnounced` (WaylandOutputViewport.cpp::onOutputAnnounced), which - only after startup init (`m_outputsInitialized`, set at WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::m_outputsInitialized), only for configured screens without a layer surface - sets up xdg-output if needed, creates the layer surface, resets the output set, and primes the frame-callback chain with one `update()` (driver WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::onOutputAnnounced). `releaseOutputSurfaces`/`acquireOutputSurfaces` (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::releaseOutputSurfaces) reuse the same per-viewport `teardownSurfaces` (WaylandOutputViewport.cpp::teardownSurfaces, nulls everything and resets all flags) / `setupLS` (WaylandOutputViewport.cpp::setupLS) pair.
- **Where it lives**: WaylandOpenGLDriver.{cpp,h} (WaylandOpenGLDriver.h::onOutputAnnounced, WaylandOpenGLDriver.h::m_outputsInitialized) and WaylandOutputViewport.cpp as cited.
- **Coupling**: moderate; needs `shouldSetupScreen` (WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::shouldSetupScreen, shared with startup) and the viewport teardown. Independent of the socket/API except that release/acquire are the driver's half of the verbs.
- **Tests**: none.

### Daemon mode, CLI flags, signals, env dials
- **What it does**: `--daemon` sets `daemonMode` + `apiSocket` and fullscreen policy Stop unless `--fullscreen` is given, which is applied after parsing (ApplicationContext.cpp::daemonMode, ApplicationContext.cpp::fullscreenWord); it exempts the launch from the "at least one background" requirement (ApplicationContext.cpp::defaultBackground), and `loadBackgrounds` then skips every screen whose path is empty (WallpaperApplication.cpp::loadBackgrounds) - surfaces exist (screens registered by `--screen-root` with empty path, ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv) but no wallpaper until `show`. `--api-socket` alone enables the socket with a normal boot (ApplicationContext.cpp::apiSocket); `setupApi` is called from `setup()` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::setupApi) and fails LOUDLY via `sLog.exception` if the socket can't be bound (WallpaperApplication.cpp::setupApi). Unknown CLI args are now logged instead of silently ignored (ApplicationContext.cpp::parse_known_args). Signals: SIGUSR1 -> deferred property reload from `--properties-file` JSON ({screen:{prop:value}}), applied via `property->update` and pushed to the scene script engine or CWeb `notifyPropertyChanged` (main.cpp::appContext, WallpaperApplication.cpp::SIGUSR1, `checkPropertyReload` WallpaperApplication.cpp::SIGUSR1; flag ApplicationContext.cpp::SIGUSR1); SIGUSR2 toggles manual pause (WallpaperApplication.cpp::signal); SIGINT/SIGTERM stop. Handlers only record the signal in an atomic mask (main.cpp::signalhandler); the main loop dispatches it at the top of each pass (WallpaperApplication.cpp::dispatchSignals) and the shutdown flag itself is atomic. main.cpp also adds a crash backtrace handler for SEGV/ILL/BUS/FPE/ABRT that prints to stderr then re-raises for the core dump (main.cpp::crashHandler), `mallopt(M_ARENA_MAX,2)` + `M_MMAP_THRESHOLD=128K` under glibc (main.cpp::main), and drops upstream's uncatchable SIGKILL handler.
- **Surface** (env vars and flags): Config reads these when a static initializer first asks for it and again once the command line is parsed (Application/Config.cpp::setFlags, called at ApplicationContext.cpp::setFlags), and every reader takes its value from Config (Application/Config.h::Config); each of the thirteen knobs Config holds also has a launch flag that wins over its variable (chapter 8, the switch map): `$LWE_SOCKET` with `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (Application/Config.cpp::LWE_SOCKET); `$LWE_DEADMAN` (Application/Config.cpp::LWE_DEADMAN); `$LWE_CC` "b c s hue" (Application/Config.cpp::LWE_CC); `$LWE_TIMESCALE` (Application/Config.cpp::LWE_TIMESCALE); `$LWE_CLASSICK`/`$LWE_CLASSICEXP`/`$LWE_AUDIOGAIN`/`$LWE_AUDIOSMOOTH` seeding the tuning globals (defaults 16.0/2.0/1.0/90.0, Application/Config.cpp::LWE_CLASSICK), which Config sets again once the command line is parsed (Application/Config.cpp::applyConfigTuning, called by setFlags and clearFlags). Read where they are used: `$LWE_TIMESTATS` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::LWE_TIMESTATS); `$LWE_MOUSEDBG` (driver WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::LWE_MOUSEDBG); `$LWE_EGLDEBUG` (viewport WaylandOutputViewport.cpp::LWE_EGLDEBUG). Kill switches: no `--api-socket` = no server at all (setupApi early-return WallpaperApplication.cpp::setupApi); `LWE_DEADMAN=0` disables deadman.
- **Related but adjacent**: `set-tuning` writes globals `g_LweClassicDivisor`/`g_LweFalloffExp`/`g_LweAudioGain`/`g_LweAudioSmoothMs` clamped 0.01..1000 / 0.5..6 / 0.1..20 / 0..500 (WallpaperApplication.cpp::set-tuning); `set-instrument` round-trips through `Logging::instrumentKnown/instrumentSet` and refuses launch-time gates (WallpaperApplication.cpp::instrumentKnown; registry seeded from env at show() WallpaperApplication.cpp::instrumentSeedFromEnv).
- **Coupling**: CLI/flags are additive hunks in ApplicationContext; signals are small main.cpp changes; the env dials are read into Config when a static initializer first asks for it and again once the command line is parsed, a given flag's knob taking the place of its variable's (Application/Config.cpp::fromEnvironment). Each is individually portable, though `--daemon` is meaningless without the show/rebuild machinery.
- **Tests**: `Testing/Cases/ArgvFlags.cpp` parses the thirteen settings flags through `loadSettingsFromArgv` (a valid value gives its knob that value, the flag text as raw and source flag, a refused one stops the launch with the `--help` hint, and the four tuning flags reach their globals), each of the fifteen second names against the name it joins, the edge words through `--edge` and `--clamp`, `--fullscreen` (pause after and before `--daemon`, keep, stop, an empty word, and the refused pair with `--no-fullscreen-pause`), and `--debug` (a switch set, an inherited variable cleared, two in one argv, and an unknown switch refused with the `--help` hint); `Testing/Cases/FlagValues.cpp` covers each value check, `Testing/Cases/DebugSwitches.cpp` covers the switch table, each kind of value rule at its bounds, the refusal messages and the help text, and `Testing/Cases/HelpText.cpp` covers the engine help text (its content and each entry's spellings) and runs the engine binary with `-h` and with `--help`; none for the other flags and the signals. `Testing/Cases/Config.cpp` covers the env dials Config reads (value and source unset, set and empty, and one malformed or edge value each) and a flag over each variable; the socket path env test is at CommandSocket.cpp::LWE_SOCKET.

### Status introspection (`status` verb)
- **What it does**: `apiStatus` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiStatus) returns api version 1, the build's version stamp as `version` (the first line of `VERSION`), pid, uptime, per-screen wallpaper paths, current show id, ui_id and title (the title of the project loaded for the screen the id comes from, else of the window-mode project, else empty), manual_pause, `restore_refused` (true from a boot whose crash-loop guard refused the restore until the first wallpaper is shown, else false, WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiStatus), tuning globals, connected client count, cc, speed, outputs state/reason/deadman_s/ping_seen, full rotation block (enabled, interval, next_in_s - honoring frozen countdown, WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiStatus), volume/audio/mouse/automute/fps/frames/parallax/particles, overlay (visible, corner), fullscreen ignore list + behavior, live instruments, `config` (the thirteen knobs Config reads, Application/Config.h::Config, keyed by variable name, each with its value as used at launch and its source, env, default or flag; `LWE_CC` and `LWE_TIMESCALE` after their setters' clamps and `LWE_TEXDETAIL` as auto or full, WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiStatus), scaling/clamp of the first non-span screen, and the rotation countdown as `next_in_s` (whole seconds, floor of the milliseconds) and `next_in_ms` (the engine keeps the fraction of a second across a pause, so a deck anchored to the milliseconds never disagrees with it, WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiStatus; a `lanes-set` reply carries the same per lane), the fit window as `fit` (the wallpaper layer of the current show) and `fit_effective` (composed with the lane layer, WallpaperApplication.cpp::effectiveFit); each `lanes[]` entry carries its own `fit` (lane layer) and `fit_effective` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiStatus). `list-objects` (WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand) dumps scene object ids/names plus image-effect chains (ids usable with `set-skip`/`skip_effects`), first screen only since mirror groups share one scene.
- **Coupling**: read-only aggregation over the same state; portable with the state it reads.

### The `lwe` name: the engine's own shell verbs, its socket client and the panel handoff
- **What it does**: The binary answers to a second name. When the last component of `argv[0]` is exactly `lwe` (for example a symlink named lwe), `main` hands the run to name mode right after the crash handler, before `SpawnGate`, logging, `ApplicationContext` and the state-file migration; an `argc` of 0 or a null `argv[0]` stays engine mode (main.cpp::isLweName, Application/LweCommand.cpp::isLweName). Name mode never touches argparse, the state tree or a display, prints with `std::cout` and `std::cerr`, and returns its exit code from `main` (Application/LweCommand.cpp::run). It answers four forms itself. `lwe` and `lwe status` send `status` and print five lines (Application/LweCommand.cpp::statusText): on screen (title and id), playlist (label, order, then "next in 7m 02s", or "paused" when `rotation.enabled` is false, and no timer for static), screens (on, or off with the reason: lwe off, fullscreen app, watchdog, running-app rule), version, and settings (volume, speed, fps, mouse, parallax, particles); every control character (0x00 to 0x1F and 0x7F) in those lines prints as `?`, because a Workshop title can carry terminal escape sequences. `lwe off` sends `release-outputs` and `lwe on` sends `acquire-outputs`, each printing one line. `lwe --version` prints `LWE_VERSION` alone without asking the engine; a word after it exits 3 like a word after status, off or on. `-j` or `--json`, as exact words anywhere, prints the engine's result object on one line instead, unchanged, and an error of these verbs as `{"error":"<message>"}` on stderr, compact, where the message is the text line without its "lwe: " prefix; the version itself always prints as plain text. The version check: `status` compares the running engine's `version` with the installed `LWE_VERSION` (a status with no version field reads as older than 1.1.0); on a mismatch it prints "The running engine is <running> but <installed> is installed; run lwe service restart." on stderr and still prints the status, exit 0; `off` and `on` ask for `status` first and refuse a mismatch with exit 1 before sending anything (Application/LweCommand.cpp::versionMismatch). Exit codes: 0 done; 1 refused (the engine's error, a version mismatch or a failed handoff); 2 not running, no answer within 3 seconds, a connection closed without an answer, or a reply lwe cannot read; 3 a word after status, off, on or --version. Every other first word goes to the panel.
- **The client**: one blocking request per call (Api/CommandClient.cpp::request): connect to Config's socket path, send the one line `{"cmd":"<verb>","id":1}` and read one reply line, all inside a 3-second deadline (Api/CommandClient.h::DEADLINE_MS; the connect wait is bounded with `SO_SNDTIMEO` as well, so a wedged engine with a full backlog cannot hang it) and a 64 KiB line cap (Api/CommandClient.h::MAX_REPLY_BYTES). Outcomes: a reply (an object with a boolean `ok` and a `result` object or an `error` string), not running (ENOENT, ECONNREFUSED or any other failure to connect, a path too long for a socket included; the message names the path and the reason), no answer (the deadline passed), closed (the request could not be sent, or the engine closed the connection before any byte, as it does when its client table is full or the peer has another uid; printed as "lwe: the engine closed the connection without answering; it may have too many clients.") and a bad reply (anything else). It never sends `ping`, so a bar polling lwe cannot keep a dead panel's watchdog quiet. Name mode parses no flags, so the path comes from the environment only: `$LWE_SOCKET`, else `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/engine.sock`, else `/tmp/lwe-<euid>/engine.sock`.
- **The handoff**: for every other verb, Application/PanelHandoff.cpp::handOff replaces the process with the panel launcher: `execve(launcher, [launcher, "--lwe", LWE_VERSION, <cwd>, word1, word2, ...], <environment>)`, the user's words unchanged, `-j` included, and `<cwd>` empty when the working directory cannot be read; stdin, signals and the exit code pass through. The environment is the one the process started with, read from `/proc/self/environ` (`environ` when that cannot be read), because library initializers change the environment before `main`: sdl2-compat sets `SDL2_COMPAT`, `SDL3_VERSION` and `SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER`. The launcher is the first regular executable file of (Application/PanelHandoff.cpp::findPanelLauncher): `dirname(argv[0])/lwe-ui` when `argv[0]` contains a slash, as typed; each absolute `$PATH` entry plus `/lwe-ui`, empty and relative entries skipped; when `HOME` is absolute, `$HOME/.local/bin/lwe-ui` and `$HOME/.local/share/lwe-ui/venv/bin/lwe-ui`. None found: "lwe: <verb> needs the LWE panel, which is not installed; without it lwe does status, off, on and --version." and exit 1. An exec failure: "lwe: cannot run <path>: <reason>" and exit 1.
- **Where it lives**: fork-only new files `src/WallpaperEngine/Application/LweCommand.{h,cpp}`, `src/WallpaperEngine/Api/CommandClient.{h,cpp}` and `src/WallpaperEngine/Application/PanelHandoff.{h,cpp}`, listed in CMakeLists.txt::COMMON_SOURCES; the branch in main.cpp::main.
- **Coupling**: needs the reply shape the dispatcher writes (Api/CommandDispatcher.h::Replies), the `status` fields it prints and Config; nothing else in the engine. The handoff's argument list is the input contract of the panel's command entry.
- **Tests**: `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Cases/LweCommand.cpp`: the name check and the parse table, the status text for live, released, paused, static, no version field and control characters, the mismatch line, and the built engine run through a scratch symlink named lwe with an environment built from nothing (Cases/LweCommand.cpp::environment): `--version`, `--version now` (3, and its JSON error), `status` against a scratch server, `status` against an engine of another version (the warning, exit 0), `off` against it (1, nothing sent after status), `off` and `on` against a matching engine (status, then the verb), `-j` status, a silent engine (2), a reply lwe cannot read (2), an engine that closes the connection (2), a refusal (1), `off` with no engine (2), `off now` (3), a panel verb recorded by a fake `lwe-ui` on the scratch `PATH` (its argv and working directory, and its exit code passed through), the starting environment reaching the launcher, a directory named `lwe-ui` skipped, and no launcher (1). `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Cases/CommandClient.cpp`, against a scratch CommandServer in a thread: a reply and a refusal, a socket file with no listener, no socket file, the 3-second deadline, a reply of exactly 64 KiB and one byte longer, and replies that are not a reply object. `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Cases/PanelHandoff.cpp`: the search order through an injected predicate (a relative or empty `HOME` adds no place), and the built engine spawned with `HOME=rel` from a folder holding `rel/.local/bin/lwe-ui`, which prints the engine-only line and exits 1 without running that file.

---

## 5-line summary

The fork adds a daemon/command layer: a same-uid-only UNIX socket server (new `src/WallpaperEngine/Api/`, 0600 socket/0700 dir/SO_PEERCRED/single-instance guard) speaking one-JSON-per-line with a strictly validated 30-verb dispatcher (`show`, `rotate-set`, `next/prev`, `ping`, `pause/resume`, `set-*`, `release/acquire-outputs`, `set-app-conditions`) using an accepted-then-done reply pattern. `--daemon` boots the engine idle (surfaces up, no wallpaper), while `--api-socket` alone adds the socket to a normal boot that still needs a wallpaper; an idle daemon boot restores its own persisted state (in daemon mode every mutating verb re-writes `$XDG_STATE_HOME/lwe/engine/engine-state.json` atomically; a crash-loop guard refuses restore after two boots that died young) and otherwise awaits `show`, which hot-swaps all outputs with per-show args, rollback, and 100-deep history; a rotation engine (sequential, shuffle and static orders, random accepted for one release, avoid-repeat, freezable countdown) advances on a timer, except in static. Output lifecycle is managed by a release/acquire state machine driven by four sources - verb, a ping-fed deadman switch (`LWE_DEADMAN`, default 300 s), a fullscreen-stop gate, and a running-apps rule polling `/proc` - all serviced from the top of the main loop so commands work while parked. The Wayland driver's blocking dispatch is replaced by a bounded poll loop that includes the API fds, renders each callback-signaled viewport once per pass, forces `eglSwapInterval(0)` (DPMS safety), re-kicks rendering after 2 s of silence (keepalive), and implements output hotplug/hot-unplug. Verified by the Catch2 suites `CommandSocket.cpp` and `CommandDispatcher.cpp` and the lane, schedule and transport units (`LaneState.cpp`, `Schedule.cpp`, `LaneHistory.cpp`); the engine-side handlers (~1,700 new lines in `WallpaperApplication.cpp`) are deeply woven into settings/rebuild state and are the main cherry-pick cost.

---

# 2. The two-service web architecture


All paths are relative to the repository root unless prefixed `upstream:`. Every claim below is taken from the code rather than from documentation.

---

### 1. Three-binary split: engine (no CEF) / `lwe-web-service` (CEF browser process) / `lwe-web-helper` (CEF subprocess host)

- **What it does**: The fork takes CEF entirely out of the engine process. Upstream created `WebBrowserContext` inside `WallpaperApplication` (upstream:`WallpaperApplication.cpp::ensureBrowserForProject`) and linked libcef into `linux-wallpaperengine-lib` (upstream:`CMakeLists.txt::target_link_libraries`). The fork builds two new executables: `lwe-web-service` (`src/web-service-main.cpp`), which owns `CefInitialize`, the scheme handlers and the browser instances; and `lwe-web-helper` (`src/web-helper-main.cpp`), a 11-line subprocess binary set as `settings.browser_subprocess_path` (`WebBrowserContext.cpp::browser_subprocess_path`) so CEF's render/zygote children exec a tiny binary instead of the service. The engine lib no longer links or compiles any CEF code - fork `CMakeLists.txt::target_link_libraries` link list contains no cef (the lib target still carries CEF include paths and copies the CEF binaries into the output dir - build wiring, not compilation); all `WebBrowser/*` sources moved into the `lwe-web-service` target (`CMakeLists.txt::lwe-web-service`), `SubprocessApp` into `lwe-web-helper` (`CMakeLists.txt::lwe-web-helper`). The service is spawned only when a web wallpaper is actually created: `CWeb`'s constructor calls `HelperClient::create` (`src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Wallpapers/CWeb.cpp::create`), which calls `ensureHelper()` -> `spawnService()` (`src/WallpaperEngine/WebHelper/HelperClient.cpp::create`). `WallpaperApplication::setupBrowser` enumerates web wallpapers but deliberately does not start anything (`src/WallpaperEngine/Application/WallpaperApplication.cpp::setupBrowser`).
- **Where it lives**: New files `src/web-service-main.cpp`, `src/web-helper-main.cpp`, all of `src/WallpaperEngine/WebHelper/`. Heavily rewritten upstream files: `WebBrowserContext.{cpp,h}` (constructor now takes `CefMainArgs&`, `SpawnConfig&`, `MediaSource&` instead of `WallpaperApplication&` - `WebBrowserContext.h::WebBrowserContext`), `BrowserApp.{cpp,h}`, `SubprocessApp.{cpp,h}`, `BrowserClient.{cpp,h}`, `RenderHandler.{cpp,h}`, `WPSchemeHandlerFactory.{cpp,h}`. `WPSchemeHandler.{cpp,h}` are byte-identical to upstream (verified by diff).
- **Surface**: Service CLI switches parsed by `SpawnConfig::fromArguments` (`src/WallpaperEngine/WebHelper/SpawnConfig.cpp::fromArguments`): `--lwe-assets-dir=` (required), `--lwe-web-socket=` (required), `--lwe-max-fps=`, `--lwe-protocol=` (required; must equal `PROTOCOL_VERSION` = 1, `Protocol.h::PROTOCOL_VERSION`, mismatch is a hard refuse at `SpawnConfig.cpp::PROTOCOL_VERSION`), and one `--lwe-scheme=<workshopId>=<path>` per wallpaper (`SpawnConfig.cpp::fromArguments`). Helper-side switch: `--lwe-schemes=<id>,<id>,...`, appended by `BrowserApp::OnBeforeChildProcessLaunch` (`BrowserApp.cpp::OnBeforeChildProcessLaunch`) and parsed pre-CEF by `SubprocessApp::parseSchemeIds` (`SubprocessApp.cpp::parseSchemeIds`, used at `web-helper-main.cpp::parseSchemeIds`). If `lwe-web-helper` is absent next to the service binary, the code logs and leaves `browser_subprocess_path` unset (`WebBrowserContext.cpp::browser_subprocess_path`).
- **Coupling**: Touches upstream broadly but shallowly: `CWallpaper::fromWallpaper`'s `WebBrowser::WebBrowserContext*` parameter became a `WebHelper::HelperClient*` (`src/WallpaperEngine/Render/CWallpaper.h::fromWallpaper`, `CWallpaper.cpp::fromWallpaper`) with three call sites in `WallpaperApplication.cpp`; `WallpaperApplication` gained `m_webHelper`, `enumerateWebBackgrounds`, `ensureWebHelperClient`, `setupBrowser`, `ensureBrowserForProject` and one `pumpEvents()` call per frame (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::m_webHelper`); `main.cpp` gained one line (`main.cpp::main`). A porter must take: the whole `WebHelper/` tree, the two mains, the rewritten `WebBrowser/` CEF classes, `CWeb.{cpp,h}`, the `fromWallpaper` signature change, the WallpaperApplication wiring, and the CMake target changes. It is a coherent module but not liftable as a single directory - the factory signature change is the invasive part.
- **Tests**: `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Cases/WebHelperStartupCost.cpp::Idle` asserts a `HelperClient` never spawns a process across 240 `pumpEvents()` calls and that `allocateInstance()` alone stays `Idle`.
- **Uncertain**: The missing-helper fallback path is questionable from code: a re-exec'd CEF child of `lwe-web-service` would run `web-service-main.cpp`'s `main`, which parses the spawn config and calls `HelperServer::start()` (web-service-main.cpp::main) *before* `CefExecuteProcess` (invoked inside `WebBrowserContext`, WebBrowserContext.cpp::CefExecuteProcess) - and `start()` fails when the parent already holds the socket (`MessageChannel.cpp::listen`). Whether that actually breaks CEF children depends on which switches CEF propagates to re-exec'd children; CEF's switch propagation is not verified here, and the code's own log message only warns about process-name census miscounting (`WebBrowserContext.cpp::helperPath`).

### 2. Spawn hardening (`SpawnGate`)

- **What it does**: `SpawnGate::captureAtStartup()` (`src/WallpaperEngine/WebHelper/SpawnGate.cpp::captureAtStartup`) runs early in `main()` after the mallopt tuning and crash-handler install (`main.cpp::main`, also in the probe at `tools/web-frame-probe.cpp::captureAtStartup`), resolving the service binary via `/proc/self/exe` (`SpawnGate.cpp::resolveServiceBinary`), recording the startup thread count from `/proc/self/status` (`SpawnGate.cpp::readThreadCount`) and the process signal mask. `spawn()` (`SpawnGate.cpp::POSIX_SPAWN_SETSIGMASK`) uses `posix_spawn` with `POSIX_SPAWN_SETSIGMASK` restoring the startup mask (SpawnGate.cpp::POSIX_SPAWN_SETSIGMASK), and closes every fd above stderr in the child: `posix_spawn_file_actions_addclosefrom_np(3)` on glibc >= 2.34, else a `/proc/self/fd` enumeration fallback (SpawnGate.cpp::defined). Environment is inherited wholesale, which is the documented channel for `LWE_CEFLOG`/`LWE_CEFDEBUG`/`LWE_WEB_IDLE_EXIT_MS` (SpawnGate.cpp::LWE_CEFLOG).
- **Where it lives**: New files `src/WallpaperEngine/WebHelper/SpawnGate.{h,cpp}`. One-line hooks in `main.cpp::main` and `tools/web-frame-probe.cpp::captureAtStartup`.
- **Surface**: No flags or env of its own; exposes `serviceBinary()`, `threadsAtCapture()` diagnostics (`SpawnGate.h::serviceBinary`). Late capture degrades gracefully with a logged warning (`SpawnGate.cpp::spawn`).
- **Coupling**: Self-contained; a porter needs the two files plus the `main.cpp` line.
- **Tests**: Indirectly, via `WebHelperStartupCost.cpp` (spawn counters) and the probe's lifecycle scenario.

### 3. Message protocol and channel (socket verbs, caps, peer auth)

- **What it does**: Length-prefixed binary protocol over a Unix stream socket. 8-byte `MessageHeader {u32 length, u16 type, u16 flags}` (`src/WallpaperEngine/WebHelper/Protocol.h::MessageHeader`, static_assert 8 bytes). Commands (engine->helper, type < 0x8000): `Create, Resize, MouseMove, MouseClick, InjectProperties, SetProperty, AudioSpectrum, Destroy` (`Protocol.h::MessageType`); events (helper->engine): `FrameReady, PageLoaded, PageFailed` (`Protocol.h::FrameReady`). Encoders at `Protocol.cpp::AUDIO_BANDS`; every payload starts with the `InstanceId`. `PayloadReader` is bounds-checked with a sticky failure flag (`Protocol.cpp::PayloadReader`). Caps: `MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES` = 1 MiB enforced on receive (`Protocol.h::MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES`, `MessageChannel.cpp::MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES`); `MAX_QUEUED_BYTES` = 8 MiB outbound queue, trip closes the channel (`MessageChannel.h::MAX_QUEUED_BYTES`, `MessageChannel.cpp::MAX_QUEUED_BYTES`); create/resize dimensions capped at 16384x16384 and non-zero, enforced helper-side (`Service/HelperServer.cpp::handle`); `AUDIO_BANDS` = 64 fixed (`Protocol.h::AUDIO_BANDS`). Listener: 0700 on a directory it created, 0600 socket (umask 0177 + explicit chmod), refuses to steal a live socket (`MessageChannel.cpp::listen`), backlog 1 (`MessageChannel.cpp::listen`), and `accept()` authenticates the peer with `SO_PEERCRED` same-uid check (`MessageChannel.cpp::SO_PEERCRED`). Default socket path: `$LWE_WEB_SOCKET`, else `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/web-<pid>.sock`, else `/tmp/lwe-<uid>/web-<pid>.sock` (`SpawnConfig.cpp::LWE_WEB_SOCKET`). All fds non-blocking with `MSG_NOSIGNAL` (`MessageChannel.cpp::setNonBlocking`).
- **Where it lives**: New files `src/WallpaperEngine/WebHelper/Protocol.{h,cpp}`, `MessageChannel.{h,cpp}`, `SpawnConfig.{h,cpp}`.
- **Surface**: Socket path env `LWE_WEB_SOCKET` read at `SpawnConfig.cpp::LWE_WEB_SOCKET`. Protocol version kill switch: `--lwe-protocol` vs `PROTOCOL_VERSION` (`SpawnConfig.cpp::PROTOCOL_VERSION`).
- **Coupling**: Self-contained module (only dependency: `Logging/Log.h`); primary consumers are `HelperClient` (engine) and `HelperServer` (service), with its headers also included by `web-service-main`, `WebInstance`, and `PropertyClassifier`.
- **Tests**: None of the Catch2 cases exercise the wire format directly; the probe does end-to-end.

### 4. Shared-memory frame transport (double-slot seqlock ring)

- **What it does**: One POSIX shm object per instance per size, named `/lwe-web-<helperPid>-<instanceId>-<generation>` (`FrameContract.h::frameShmName`). Fixed layout: `FrameHeader` (magic `0x4645574C` "LWEF", version 1, geometry, generation, `std::atomic<uint32_t> sequence`) in the first 64 bytes, then 2 BGRA8 slots (`FrameContract.h::BGRA8`). Writer (`FrameWriter`, service side): `allocate()` creates with `O_EXCL`, 0600, bumps generation even on failure, and only releases the old mapping after the new one succeeds (`FrameContract.cpp::FrameHeader`); `publish()` memcpy's into the back slot then a release-store on sequence, and *drops* paints whose size doesn't match the allocation (FrameContract.cpp::FrameWriter). Reader (`FrameReader`, engine side): two-stage map (probe header at 64 bytes, validate magic/version/geometry, then map full size read-only) - all header fields from the other process are validated, never trusted (`FrameContract.cpp::FrameHeader`); `consume()` is latch -> copy live slot -> re-check, max `MAX_READ_ATTEMPTS` = 4, then abandons and keeps the old texture (`FrameContract.cpp::MAX_READ_ATTEMPTS`). The only per-lifecycle socket traffic is the `FrameReady` event announcing a new generation (`Service/HelperServer.cpp::hasUnannouncedGeneration`, emitted once per generation per `WebInstance::hasUnannouncedGeneration`, `Service/WebInstance.h::hasUnannouncedGeneration`); steady-state frames cross zero socket bytes. On the engine side `CWeb::syncFrameReader` maps `frameShmName(helperPid, id, generation)` from the event (`CWeb.cpp::syncFrameReader`).
- **Where it lives**: New files `src/WallpaperEngine/WebHelper/FrameContract.{h,cpp}`. Consumers: `Service/WebInstance` (writer, `WebInstance.h::m_frames`), `CWeb` (reader, `CWeb.h` member `m_frames`), probe.
- **Surface**: None (no env/flags); kill switch is `FRAME_VERSION` mismatch refuse (`FrameContract.cpp::FRAME_VERSION`).
- **Coupling**: Self-contained; liftable alone given `Logging/Log.h`.
- **Tests**: Probe phases [3]-[5] (`tools/web-frame-probe.cpp::runFrameTransportProof`) assert monotonic sequence, non-uniform content, and generation change across resize, and report retry/abandon counters without asserting on them. No Catch2 case.

### 5. `wp<id>` custom scheme and its containment

- **What it does**: Scheme name is `"wp" + workshopId` via `generateSchemeName` in the new header `src/WallpaperEngine/WebBrowser/CEF/SchemeName.h::generateSchemeName` (moved out of `WebBrowserContext.h`, where upstream had the bare `#define WPENGINE_SCHEME`). The complete scheme universe is the enumerated web library - `WallpaperApplication::enumerateWebBackgrounds` scans `$XDG_DATA_HOME/lwe/wallpapers`, `~/.local/share/lwe/wallpapers`, and Steam workshop roots for app 431960, keeping only `type=="web"` projects with a parseable `workshopid` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::XDG_DATA_HOME`) - and is baked into the spawn config (`buildWebHelperSpawnConfig`, WallpaperApplication.cpp::buildWebHelperSpawnConfig). Registered with `CEF_SCHEME_OPTION_STANDARD | SECURE | FETCH_ENABLED` in `SubprocessApp::OnRegisterCustomSchemes` (`SubprocessApp.cpp::OnRegisterCustomSchemes`), handler factories registered per-id in `BrowserApp::OnContextInitialized` (`BrowserApp.cpp::OnContextInitialized`). The helper parses `project.json` lazily on CEF's IO thread on first request, mutex-guarded, and a parse failure returns `nullptr` so CEF serves an error page while the helper survives (`WPSchemeHandlerFactory.cpp::Create`). The wallpaper URL is `<scheme>://root/<file>` built in `WebInstance::open` (`Service/WebInstance.cpp::generateSchemeName`). A wallpaper not in the scheme universe simply cannot be loaded by that helper (comment at `SpawnConfig.h::SpawnConfig`; behavior follows from the registration loop). Chromium hardening switches (`--disable-web-security`, `--disable-site-isolation-trials`, etc.) are byte-identical to upstream's list (`BrowserApp.cpp::OnBeforeCommandLineProcessing` vs upstream `BrowserApp.cpp::OnBeforeCommandLineProcessing`); what changed is child launch now passes only `--lwe-schemes` instead of copying the engine's entire argv (upstream `BrowserApp.cpp::OnBeforeChildProcessLaunch` copied the argv; the fork builds the list with the new `BrowserApp.cpp::collectWorkshopIds`).
- **Where it lives**: New `SchemeName.h`; rewritten `SubprocessApp.{cpp,h}`, `BrowserApp.{cpp,h}`, `WPSchemeHandlerFactory.{cpp,h}` (upstream factory took a `const Project&`; fork takes id+paths+a `MediaSource&` and parses lazily); unchanged `WPSchemeHandler.{cpp,h}`.
- **Surface**: `--lwe-scheme=<id>=<path>` per wallpaper (`SpawnConfig.cpp::fromArguments`); `--lwe-schemes=` child switch.
- **Coupling**: The lazy-parse factory depends on `Assets::setupAssetLocator`, `Data::Parsers::ProjectParser` and `Media::MediaSource` (`WPSchemeHandlerFactory.cpp::ProjectParser`) - service-side only, fed by the new `NullMediaSource` (`Service/NullMediaSource.{h,cpp}`, an inert `MediaSource` subclass with no-op `update`/`performUpdate`). Moderately self-contained.
- **Tests**: None direct; probe phase [2] (page-loaded within 30 s) covers the load path.

### 6. Load-state tracking, typed property injection, audio spectrum, audio-listener shim

- **What it does**: `BrowserClient` now implements `CefLoadHandler` and records the first main-frame verdict: `OnLoadError` (minus `ERR_ABORTED`) or an `OnLoadEnd` whose URL is a `chrome-error:` document or whose HTTP status is outside [200,400) (status 0 explicitly not a failure) marks the instance failed (`BrowserClient.cpp::OnLoadEnd`). The server polls these flags once per tick and emits `PageLoaded`/`PageFailed` exactly once (`Service/HelperServer.cpp::isPageLoaded`). Engine-side, `CWeb` gates its one-time property injection on `isPageLoaded` (`CWeb.cpp::isPageLoaded`), and a failed instance never reports itself loaded, so injection is never attempted (`HelperClient.cpp::PageFailed`). Properties cross the wire *typed* - Boolean/Number/String `PropertyValue` (`Protocol.h::PropertyValue`), classified once from the parsed model by `PropertyClassifier.cpp::PropertyValue` (color -> "r g b" string, text properties dropped entirely as display-only labels), and rendered into a JS literal calling `window.wallpaperPropertyListener.applyUserProperties` by `WebInstance::renderApplyUserProperties` (`Service/WebInstance.cpp::renderApplyUserProperties`). Audio: the engine sends 64 raw floats every other frame from `recorder.audio64` (`CWeb.cpp::audio64`; `audio64` at `src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/Drivers/Recorders/PlaybackRecorder.h::audio64`), and the helper builds a 128-element `Float32Array` (64 bands written twice) and calls `window.__lweAudioCallback` (`Service/WebInstance.cpp::Float32Array`). The callback exists because `SubprocessApp` injects an idempotent `wallpaperRegisterAudioListener` shim into every main-frame V8 context (`SubprocessApp.cpp::wallpaperRegisterAudioListener`), re-run as a backstop on each property injection (`WebInstance.cpp::SubprocessApp`).
- **Where it lives**: New `PropertyClassifier.{h,cpp}`; rewritten `BrowserClient.{cpp,h}` (upstream's was 6 lines, no load handling - verified above); modified `SubprocessApp` (shim); `Service/WebInstance.cpp`; engine-side hooks in `CWeb.cpp::notifyPropertyChanged` and `notifyPropertyChanged` (`CWeb.cpp::notifyPropertyChanged`), called from the property-reload path at `WallpaperApplication.cpp::notifyPropertyChanged`.
- **Surface**: No new flags; framerate for `windowless_frame_rate` is `max(60, settings.render.maximumFPS)` computed engine-side (`CWeb.cpp::framerate`) and trusted helper-side (`WebInstance.cpp::windowless_frame_rate`).
- **Coupling**: `PropertyClassifier` depends on the Data model and on `Protocol.h` for the `PropertyValue` wire type it returns - self-contained otherwise. The CWeb/WallpaperApplication hooks are a few call sites.

### 7. Idle teardown, crash supervision, respawn with state replay, crash-loop guard

- **What it does (service side)**: `HelperServer` exits when the last instance is destroyed and an idle grace elapses: default `DEFAULT_IDLE_EXIT_MS = 1000` ms, overridden by `LWE_WEB_IDLE_EXIT_MS` (read once, `Service/HelperServer.cpp::LWE_WEB_IDLE_EXIT_MS`), timer starts only after an engine has connected at least once (`HelperServer.cpp::updateIdleTimer`), canceled if a new instance arrives in the window (`HelperServer.cpp::updateIdleTimer`). It also exits immediately when the engine disconnects (`web-service-main.cpp::main`, `HelperServer.cpp::tick`, clearing all instances).
- **What it does (engine side)**: `HelperClient` runs a state machine `Idle/Starting/Connected/Draining/Backoff/Cooldown` (`HelperClient.h::Cooldown`). When the replay map empties while connected it enters `Draining` and the helper is expected to idle-exit; after `DRAIN_TIMEOUT_MS` = 8000 ms the client forces the socket closed and escalates `waitpid` to `SIGKILL` (`HelperClient.cpp::DRAIN_TIMEOUT_MS`). An unexpected disconnect while wallpapers are on screen SIGKILLs the child, unlinks orphaned `/dev/shm/lwe-web-<pid>-*` objects (`HelperClient.cpp::reapShmObjects`), and schedules a respawn with doubling backoff 250->5000 ms (`BACKOFF_BASE_MS`/`BACKOFF_MAX_MS`, `HelperClient.h::BACKOFF_MAX_MS`), reset after a 10 s healthy connection (`HEALTHY_CONNECTION_MS`, `HelperClient.cpp::HEALTHY_CONNECTION_MS`). Respawn is driven one attempt per `pumpEvents()` so a crashing helper cannot stall the render loop (`HelperClient.cpp::Cooldown`). On reconnect, `replayState()` re-sends `Create` for every recorded instance and defers properties until the replacement's `PageLoaded`, where the merged inject+set-property state is replayed (`HelperClient.cpp::PageLoaded`). Crash-loop guard: >=4 deaths in 60 s -> 30 s `Cooldown` with the death ring cleared (defaults `HelperClient.h::CrashGuardSettings`; logic `HelperClient.cpp::BACKOFF_MAX_MS`).
- **Where it lives**: `HelperClient.{h,cpp}` (engine), `Service/HelperServer.{h,cpp}` + `web-service-main.cpp` (service).
- **Surface**: `LWE_WEB_IDLE_EXIT_MS` (`HelperServer.cpp::LWE_WEB_IDLE_EXIT_MS`); `LWE_WEB_CRASHGUARD=<deaths>,<windowMs>,<cooldownMs>`, all three or ignored, values validated (`HelperClient.cpp::LWE_WEB_CRASHGUARD`). Connect patience: 200 attempts x 25 ms on first spawn only (`HelperClient.h::CONNECT_ATTEMPTS`, `HelperClient.cpp::ensureHelper`).
- **Coupling**: Self-contained within WebHelper + the one `pumpEvents()` call per frame in `WallpaperApplication.cpp::show`.
- **Tests**: Probe scenarios (a) teardown-to-zero, (b) SIGKILL respawn with property-replay fidelity, (c) crash-loop guard timing, plus orphan/shm hygiene sweeps (`tools/web-frame-probe.cpp::SIGKILL`); `WebHelperStartupCost.cpp` covers the never-spawn invariant.

### 8. CEF external-message-pump integration (wake-driven poll loop)

- **What it does**: The service sets `settings.external_message_pump = 1` (`WebBrowserContext.cpp::external_message_pump`) and drives CEF itself. `BrowserApp::OnScheduleMessagePumpWork` records the next due time (REPLACE semantics) and writes to a non-blocking eventfd (`BrowserApp.cpp::OnScheduleMessagePumpWork`); the eventfd is created before `CefInitialize` and registered via `setPumpWakeFd` (`web-service-main.cpp::setPumpWakeFd`).
