- **Uncertain**: whether every WE file with comments actually parses after stripping (the stripper is hand-rolled); not verifiable statically.

### JSON type-coercion hardening (numeric strings, exception-safe optionals)
- **What it does**: `JSON::coerceNumericString<T>` (src/WallpaperEngine/Data/JSON.h::coerceNumericString) converts string values like `"0.5"`, `"true"`/`"false"` to arithmetic T via `strtod`, rejecting non-finite parses (`inf`/`nan`) and clamping a finite value to the integral target's range, so no cast is undefined. A vector field that arrives as a bare number fills every component (JSON.h::is_number), matching content that writes `32` where `"32 32"` is expected. `require<T>` now tries coercion before the plain get (JSON.h::require). Both `optional<T>` overloads (JSON.h::coerceNumericString and JSON.h::value_or) lost their `noexcept`, coerce numeric strings, and catch conversion exceptions, logging `Ignoring optional value '<key>' of mismatched type` and returning nullopt/default instead of crashing the whole parse.
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/Data/JSON.h only.
- **Surface**: none; automatic for every `require`/`optional` call engine-wide.
- **Coupling**: header-only templates, additive; behavior change is global to all JSON consumers. Self-contained to lift, but it changes semantics everywhere it's included (silent defaulting instead of exceptions).
- **Tests**: none directly.

### CSS hex colour parsing
- **What it does**: the colour builder reads `#rgb`, `#rgba`, `#rrggbb` and `#rrggbbaa` through `stoul` with width-aware shifts (Data/Builders/ColorBuilder.cpp::hasAlpha); upstream overflowed `stoi` on any first channel of 0x80 or more and applied eight-digit shifts to six-digit input, so white threw and every six-digit colour lost its red channel.
- **Where it lives**: modified `Data/Builders/ColorBuilder.cpp`.
- **Coupling**: self-contained.
- **Tests**: `Testing/Cases/CssHexColors.cpp`.

### BinaryReader fail-fast reads + stream-size bounds
- **What it does**: New private `readExact` (src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Utils/BinaryReader.cpp::readExact) throws via `sLog.exception` when a short read occurs, instead of upstream's silent partially-uninitialized buffers. New `remaining()` (BinaryReader.cpp::remaining) reports bytes left (0 for non-seekable streams). `nextSizedString` rejects a declared length larger than `remaining()` before allocating (BinaryReader.cpp::nextSizedString). `nextNullTerminatedString` was rewritten to read byte-wise via `get()` so a missing terminator stops at EOF instead of spinning on an indeterminate byte (BinaryReader.cpp::nextNullTerminatedString). `nextInt` is now implemented as a cast of `nextUInt32` (BinaryReader.cpp::nextInt).
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Utils/BinaryReader.cpp/.h (new members declared at BinaryReader.h::BinaryReader).
- **Surface**: none.
- **Coupling**: same public API, strictly-additive members; all existing callers get hardening for free. Trivially liftable.
- **Tests**: BinaryParserHardening.cpp::truncated (truncated u32 throws, valid u32, unterminated string stops at EOF, oversized sized-string rejected pre-allocation).

### MemoryStream seek bounds
- **What it does**: `seekoff` now computes an absolute target and rejects (returns -1) any seek landing outside `[eback, egptr]` (src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Utils/MemoryStream.h::seekoff), where upstream would `gbump`/`setg` the get pointer out of bounds. Adds a `seekpos` override forwarding to `seekoff` (MemoryStream.h::seekpos).
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Utils/MemoryStream.h.
- **Surface**: none.
- **Coupling**: self-contained streambuf override; lift alone.
- **Tests**: BinaryParserHardening.cpp::MemoryStream (in-range seek succeeds, out-of-range seek sets failbit).

### Package (.pkg) parser hardening
- **What it does**: Two new checks in src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/PackageParser.cpp: after parsing the file list, every entry's `offset+length` must fit inside the remaining payload bytes (PackageParser.cpp::remaining); and the declared file count is capped at `remaining()/12` (minimum entry size) before `reserve()` (PackageParser.cpp::parseFileList), so a hostile header can't trigger multi-GB allocations or out-of-bounds reads.
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/PackageParser.cpp.
- **Surface**: none; throws `sLog.exception` on violation.
- **Coupling**: self-contained; depends on `BinaryReader::remaining()` above.
- **Tests**: BinaryParserHardening.cpp::PKGV0001 (well-formed empty package parses; 0xFFFFFFFF count rejected).

### Texture (.tex) parser hardening + raw-GL TEXB0004 variant + lenient flags
- **What it does**: (a) New `validateMipmapPayloadBounds` (src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/TextureParser.cpp::validateMipmapPayloadBounds) rejects negative sizes, `uncompressedSize > 1 GiB` (`MAX_MIPMAP_BYTES`, TextureParser.cpp::MAX_MIPMAP_BYTES), and payloads larger than `file.remaining()`; called for both the raw-GL path (TextureParser.cpp::validateMipmapPayloadBounds) and the classic path (TextureParser.cpp::validateMipmapPayloadBounds). (b) LZ4 decode must now produce exactly `uncompressedSize` bytes, not merely `>= 0` (TextureParser.cpp::validateMipmapPayloadBounds). (c) Animated textures with zero frames throw instead of dereferencing `frames.begin()` (TextureParser.cpp::begin). (e) A running budget across every image and mip of one texture (`MAX_TEXTURE_BYTES`, TextureParser.cpp::chargeTextureBudget) bounds the total decoded size, since the per-mip cap alone lets a small file expand many times over through LZ4; the mip count per image is capped at `MAX_MIPMAP_COUNT`. (f) Frame sizes and spritesheet grids computed from file floats are checked finite and in range before any unsigned conversion (TextureParser.cpp::gifWidth). (d) New format variant: TEXB0004 containers whose `freeImageFormat` is `FIF_UNKNOWN` are treated as raw-GL textures - header carries `rawGLMipLevels` (capped 1..16, TextureParser.cpp::FIF_UNKNOWN; new field src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Assets/Texture.h::rawGLMipLevels), the declared per-image mipmap count is overridden by `rawGLMipLevels` (TextureParser.cpp::rawGLMipLevels), and each mip reads optional per-mip width/height/flag plus LZ4-or-raw payload (TextureParser.cpp::validateMipmapPayloadBounds). Upstream cannot read these at all (it would force TEXB0003 semantics). (e) `parseTextureFlags` now masks unknown bits and logs once instead of throwing (TextureParser.cpp::bits) - wallpapers with unknown flag bits (comment cites 0x800000) no longer lose whole objects.
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/TextureParser.cpp/.h (`parseMipmap` signature gained `imageIndex, mipIndex`, TextureParser.h::parseMipmap) and Texture.h::rawGLMipLevels.
- **Surface**: none.
- **Coupling**: mostly self-contained in the parser; the raw-GL variant only becomes renderable through `CTexture`'s `FIF_UNKNOWN` upload path (src/WallpaperEngine/Render/CTexture.cpp::FIF_UNKNOWN - Render area), so a data-layer-only port gets parsing but not display. Depends on `BinaryReader::remaining()`.
- **Tests**: BinaryParserHardening.cpp::TEXS0001 (valid TEXV0005/TEXB0001 parses; negative mipmap size rejected; zero-frame TEXS0001 rejected). No test for the raw-GL variant or flag masking.

### Directory adapter path-containment fix
- **What it does**: Replaces the string-prefix check (`finalpath.find(basepath)==0`, which also matches sibling dirs like `1234-evil` for base `123`) with component-aware `within()` using `lexically_relative` and rejecting `..` (src/WallpaperEngine/FileSystem/Adapters/Directory.cpp::lexically_relative), applied in `open`, `exists`, `physicalPath` (Directory.cpp::open).
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/FileSystem/Adapters/Directory.cpp.
- **Surface**: none.
- **Coupling**: self-contained, lift alone.
- **Tests**: none.

### Case-insensitive package adapter index
- **What it does**: `PackageAdapter` now builds an `unordered_map` of lowercased filename -> `FileEntry*` at construction (Package.cpp::lowercase) and both `open` and `exists` look up lowercased paths (Package.cpp::open), so scenes authored on Windows resolve assets whose case differs inside the pkg. Also replaces the linear scans. `open` clears the shared stream's error state before seeking, checks the seek, and widens the offset sum past 32 bits (Package.cpp::streamoff).
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/FileSystem/Adapters/Package.cpp/.h (new `find` + `m_index`, FileSystem/Adapters/Package.h::PackageAdapter).
- **Surface**: none.
- **Coupling**: self-contained; behavior change confined to the adapter.
- **Tests**: none.

### Virtual adapter stream rewind
- **What it does**: `VirtualAdapter::open` now `clear()`s and `seekg(0)`s the stored shared stream before returning it (src/WallpaperEngine/FileSystem/Adapters/Virtual.cpp::clear), so re-reading the same virtual file doesn't return a stream stuck at EOF/fail state from a previous reader.
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/FileSystem/Adapters/Virtual.cpp.
- **Surface**: none.
- **Coupling**: 3 lines, lift alone. Note shared-stream design means concurrent readers still share one cursor.
- **Tests**: none.

### Centralized asset mounting + VFS-injected virtual assets (`Assets::setupAssetLocator`)
- **What it does**: The mounting logic moved out of `WallpaperApplication` into a new free function `Assets::setupAssetLocator` (src/WallpaperEngine/Assets/AssetLocator.cpp::setupAssetLocator, declared AssetLocator.h::setupAssetLocator). Changes vs the old upstream mounting code: it mounts **every** `.pkg` found in the wallpaper directory (AssetLocator.cpp::packageError; upstream mounted only `scene.pkg` and `gifscene.pkg` by name), swallows mount failures per-package, mounts the assets dir, every `assets/effects/*` subdirectory, and the process CWD (AssetLocator.cpp::AssetLoadException), then registers a set of synthesized virtual files into the VFS: a classic camera bloom effect (`effects/wpenginelinux/bloomeffect.json`, AssetLocator.cpp::AssetLoadException - carried over from upstream, as are the assets-dir/CWD mounts and the wpenginelinux/copy virtual files below), an HDR bloom material ladder built by string-patching `g_RenderVar0`/`g_TexelSize` uniform annotations into copies of `hdr_downsample`/`combine_hdr` shaders (AssetLocator.cpp::g_TexelSize; a missing `g_RenderVar0` declaration logs and skips the whole ladder, a missing `g_TexelSize` one is silently ignored), pass-through `models/wpenginelinux.json` + `materials/wpenginelinux.json` (AssetLocator.cpp::AssetLoadException), `shaders/commands/copy.{frag,vert}` (AssetLocator.cpp::AssetLoadException), and a transparent solid-layer shape model + `commands/transparent` shaders (AssetLocator.cpp::AssetLoadException - both new in the fork). New `AssetLocator::getVFS()` (AssetLocator.cpp::getVFS, AssetLocator.h::getVFS) exposes the VFS so `CScene` can add a scene-dependent HDR bloom effect file at construction (src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Wallpapers/CScene.cpp::getVFS).
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/Assets/AssetLocator.cpp/.h. Caller: src/WallpaperEngine/Application/WallpaperApplication.cpp::setupAssetLocator (thin wrapper) and WPSchemeHandlerFactory.cpp::setupAssetLocator.
- **Surface**: no flags in this file; the HDR-bloom virtual files are consumed by CScene, which is gated by `LWE_NOBLOOM` (CScene.cpp::LWE_NOBLOOM, Render area).
- **Coupling**: invasive in behavior but mechanically simple - it's a relocated/upgraded version of `WallpaperApplication::setupAssetLocator`; a porter would take AssetLocator.cpp/.h plus fix up the two call sites. It also depends on `MediaCoverFactory` and `Media::MediaSource` (both pre-existing upstream). The virtual-file payloads are plain data, no engine hooks.
- **Tests**: none.
- **Uncertain**: the comment at AssetLocator.cpp::bloomeffect says the classic bloom effect file is loaded by an in-scene image; the actual CScene wiring is covered in the render chapter.

### Steam workshop content-root discovery
- **What it does**: New `Steam::FileSystem::workshopContentRoots(appID)` (FileSystem.cpp::workshopContentRoots, declared FileSystem.h::workshopContentRoots) returns **all existing** `workshop/content/<appID>` roots across the candidate Steam library paths (a hardcoded set of `$HOME`-relative candidates, unchanged from upstream - there is no `libraryfolders.vdf` parsing, so custom library drives are not auto-detected), not just one. Used to enumerate wallpapers across every library folder when building the library list (src/WallpaperEngine/Application/WallpaperApplication.cpp::enumerateWebBackgrounds, app id 431960).
- **Where it lives**: modified src/Steam/FileSystem/FileSystem.cpp/.h.
- **Surface**: none; additive API.
- **Coupling**: self-contained (reuses existing `detectHomepath`/`workshopDirectoryPaths`); one call site. Trivially liftable.
- **Tests**: none.

### Keyframed property animations (AnimationTimeline)
- **What it does**: New data model for WE scene "animation" blocks on dynamic values. `AnimationTimeline` (new file src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Model/AnimationTimeline.h::AnimationTimeline) holds up to 4 channels of sorted keyframes with cubic-bezier tangents, `fps`, `length` (frames), `mode` (single/loop/mirror, AnimationTimeline.h::AnimationTimeline), `relative`, and `baseValue`. `evaluate(elapsedSeconds)` (new file AnimationTimeline.cpp::evaluate) converts time to frames, evaluates each channel with a Newton-solved bezier ease (x-only handles mapped to [0,1], AnimationTimeline.cpp::bezierEase), clamps outside keyframe range, and adds `baseValue` when `relative`. Note: `evaluate` itself ignores `mode` - loop/mirror time folding is done by the caller, `CScene::tickAnimations` (src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Wallpapers/CScene.cpp::tickAnimations); "single" just holds the last keyframe value by clamping. Parsed by `DynamicValueParser::parseAnimation` (src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/DynamicValueParser.cpp::parseAnimation) when a value object has an `animation` object (DynamicValueParser.cpp::setAnimation); channels `c0..c3`, options `fps/length/mode`, keys `frame/value/front/back`. Stored on `DynamicValue` via `setAnimation`/`getAnimation` (src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Model/DynamicValue.cpp::setAnimation) with new `UpdateSource::Animation = 3` (DynamicValue.h::Animation).
- **Where it lives**: new files AnimationTimeline.h/.cpp; modified DynamicValueParser.cpp/.h (new private method, DynamicValueParser.h::DynamicValueParser), DynamicValue.h/.cpp.
- **Surface**: driven purely by wallpaper scene JSON (`animation` blocks). Trace logging under `LWE_LIGHTDUMP` env in the consumer (CScene.cpp::tickAnimations, Render area).
- **Coupling**: the Data-layer portion (timeline struct + parser + DynamicValue storage) is self-contained and liftable; animation only visibly runs with the CScene ticker and per-object `queueAnimation` hookup (CScene.cpp::queueAnimation) plus `UpdateSource::Animation` handling wherever updates are dispatched.
- **Tests**: none.
- **Uncertain**: `AnimationTangent.y` is parsed (DynamicValueParser.cpp::parseTangent) but never used by the evaluator (bezier ease uses x components only) - non-unit-y curves therefore evaluate approximately.

### New scene object kinds: model objects (.mdl), lights, shapes
- **What it does**: `ObjectParser` now handles three previously-unsupported object kinds. (a) Objects with a string `model` key are parsed as `ModelObject` (new struct src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Model/Object.h::ModelObjectData): the parser reads the binary `.mdl` itself - verifies `MDLV` magic, reads submesh count at offset 17 (must be 1..16), walks null-terminated material paths and skips per-submesh bounds/vertex/index blocks with truncation checks (src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/ObjectParser.cpp::parseModelObject), loads each submesh's material via `MaterialParser`, and parses an optional keyframed `angles.animation` into `PropertyAnimation` (Object.h::PropertyAnimation; ObjectParser.cpp::MaterialParser). Parse failure falls back to a placeholder ObjectData named "model-parse-failed" (ObjectParser.cpp::parse). (b) Objects with a string `light` key parse as `Light` (Object.h::light; `parseLight` ObjectParser.cpp::parseLight) with type, color, intensity, radius/exponent, inner/outer cone, control point, cascade distances, castShadow; upstream only logged "not supported". (c) Objects with a `shape` key now render as an image using the synthesized transparent shape model `models/wpenginelinux_shape.json` (ObjectParser.cpp::LWE_SHAPES) instead of being dropped.
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/ObjectParser.cpp/.h (new private methods, ObjectParser.h::ObjectParser) and src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Model/Object.h, Data/Model/Types.h::ModelObject (new forward decls + ptr aliases).
- **Surface**: env var `LWE_SHAPES` read once at ObjectParser.cpp::LWE_SHAPES: shapes render **by default**; `LWE_SHAPES=0` disables.
- **Coupling**: parsing is self-contained in ObjectParser/Object.h, but the objects do nothing without their render counterparts (CModel/CLight render classes and the shape model VFS entry from `setupAssetLocator`). The .mdl walking is hand-rolled binary parsing independent of `BinaryReader`.
- **Tests**: none.
- **Uncertain**: the .mdl layout offsets (9-byte magic and version + two u32s, count at byte 17, records at 21) are asserted by comments for "MDLV00xx" files; only guarded by the truncation checks, not validated against a spec.

### Scene parsing additions: fog, HDR bloom, perspective camera, misc
- **What it does**: `WallpaperParser::parseScene` gains: distance and height fog blocks (12 `fogdistance*`/`fogheight*` keys, src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/WallpaperParser.cpp::parseScene; model src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Model/Wallpaper.h::Fog); HDR bloom parameters `hdr`, `bloomhdriterations` (default 8), `bloomhdrscatter` (1.619), `bloomhdrfeather` (0.1), `bloomhdrstrength` (2.0), `bloomhdrthreshold` (1.0), plus `bloomtint` (WallpaperParser.cpp::bloomtint; Wallpaper.h::hdr); perspective-scene support where `general.orthogonalprojection == null` sets `isPerspective` and skips required width/height (WallpaperParser.cpp::is_null; Wallpaper.h::isPerspective); `nearz`/`farz`/`fov` now also accepted from `general` in addition to `camera` (WallpaperParser.cpp::nearz); new `perspectiveoverridefov` and `zoom` user settings (WallpaperParser.cpp::perspectiveoverridefov); and `transparentsorting` flag (WallpaperParser.cpp::transparentsorting; Wallpaper.h::SceneData). Also ambient/skylight color defaults changed 0.0 -> 0.3 (WallpaperParser.cpp::parseScene).
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/WallpaperParser.cpp and src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Model/Wallpaper.h.
- **Surface**: all scene-JSON keys listed above; no env/CLI in the parser itself.
- **Coupling**: parser + POD fields are additive and liftable; fog/HDR-bloom/perspective rendering all live in Render. Changed default ambient (0.3) alters look of existing wallpapers if ported alone.
- **Tests**: none.

### Particle system parser rework
- **What it does**: `parseParticle` was refactored to extract `parseParticleCore(json, project, depth)` (src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/ObjectParser.cpp::parseParticleCore) so child particle files are now **fully parsed recursively** into `ParticleChild::definition` (Object.h::definition) with a recursion cap of depth 4 (ObjectParser.cpp::parseParticleCore); a child `name` ending in `.json` is treated as the file directly (ObjectParser.cpp::parseParticleChild). Other changes: new `parentcontrolpoint` key (default -1) on control points (ObjectParser.cpp::parentcontrolpoint; Object.h::ParticleControlPoint); `colornAuthored` tracks whether `colorn` was present (ObjectParser.cpp::colornAuthored; Object.h::colornAuthored) - consumed with `Project::fromPackage` in CParticle (src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Objects/CParticle.cpp::colornAuthored); "instant" emitters get different defaults (distanceMax 0, instantaneous from `count`, rate 0) (ObjectParser.cpp::parseParticleCore); ropetrail renderers get rope-like subdivision/length defaults (ObjectParser.cpp::parseParticleRenderer); a `sizechange` operator with only `starttime` grows from 0 to 0.5x scale over the first `1-starttime` of life and then holds (ObjectParser.cpp::starttime); child `maxcount` default changed 20 -> 10 (ObjectParser.cpp::maxcount); effect lists skip non-object entries and entries without `file`, and a failing effect is logged and skipped instead of aborting the image (ObjectParser.cpp::file); dependencies arrays accept either numbers or objects with `id` (ObjectParser.cpp::parseDependencies); particle files parse via `parseLenient` (ObjectParser.cpp::parseLenient). Workshop ids are read as unsigned 64-bit at the project parser, the preset dependency resolver and the show-time lookup, since an id above 2^31 went through an unchecked int conversion at all three (ProjectParser.cpp::workshopId).
- **Where it lives**: modified src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/ObjectParser.cpp/.h, src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Model/Object.h.
- **Surface**: scene/particle JSON keys only.
- **Coupling**: deeply woven into ObjectParser; a porter would take the whole ObjectParser.cpp + Object.h delta. `definition` and `colornAuthored`/`fromPackage` semantics only matter with the CParticle render changes.
- **Tests**: none.
- **Uncertain**: the depth-4 cap silently drops deeper children (only logs); whether real wallpapers nest deeper is not determinable from code.

### Misc data-layer additions
- **0..1 float color strings**: `ColorBuilder` now returns values as-is when all int components are <= 1, instead of dividing by 255 (ColorBuilder.cpp::parse) - supports scenes that author colors as float vectors.
- **Textinput property without value**: `parseTextInput` defaults a missing `value` to `""` instead of throwing (Parsers/PropertyParser.cpp::parseTextInput). Combo properties still require a value, same as upstream.
- **Sound volume**: sounds parse a `volume` user setting, default 1.0 (ObjectParser.cpp::volume; Object.h::volume); `parseSound` signature gained `const Project&` (ObjectParser.h::parseSound).
- **Text limits**: `limitwidth`/`maxwidth`/`limitrows`/`maxrows`/`limituseellipsis` parsed (ObjectParser.cpp::limitwidth; Object.h::limitwidth) - upstream had a TODO.
- **Image flags**: `copybackground` and `perspective` booleans (ObjectParser.cpp::copybackground; Object.h::ImageData).
- **Puppet crop offset**: model JSON `cropoffset` string vec2, can be negative (ModelParser.cpp::cropoffset; Model.h::ModelStruct); animation layers get `additive` default true (ObjectParser.cpp::additive; Object.h::additive).
- **`Project::fromPackage`**: set when `<bg>/scene.pkg` exists (src/WallpaperEngine/Application/WallpaperApplication.cpp::fromPackage; Project.h::fromPackage); consumed only by CParticle color random handling (CParticle.cpp::fromPackage).
- **Sha256 utility**: new header-only public-domain-style SHA-256 (src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Utils/Sha256.h::sha256_hex), stated to match `hashlib.sha256`; sole consumer is the BC7/BC4/BC5 texture disk cache key in src/WallpaperEngine/Render/CTexture.cpp::sha256_hex (cache at `$HOME/.local/state/lwe/engine/texcache`). Self-contained, liftable alone; no tests.
- Each is small and independently liftable except where noted; none have flags/env.

### Modified-in-name-only / unchanged notes
- src/WallpaperEngine/Data/Parsers/ProjectParser.cpp, ShaderConstantParser.cpp, UserSettingParser.cpp, DynamicValueParser.h changes are confined to what's listed above; ProjectParser and ShaderConstantParser are **byte-identical to upstream**. VectorBuilder, UserSettingBuilder, TypeCaster unchanged.
- src/WallpaperEngine/FileSystem/Container.cpp/.h and Adapters/MediaCover.* are **unchanged** vs upstream (`Container::getVFS` already existed upstream at Container.h::getVFS - the fork only exposes it through AssetLocator).

## 5-line summary

The fork's data layer is heavily hardened against malformed content: `BinaryReader` now fails on short reads and bounds-checks lengths against `remaining()`, `MemoryStream` rejects out-of-range seeks, and the package/texture parsers cap counts and sizes before allocating (all covered by the new `BinaryParserHardening.cpp` test). A lenient JSON pipeline (`parseLenient` + comment/trailing-comma stripping + numeric-string coercion + exception-safe `optional`) is wired through every scene/model/material/effect/particle parse path. New readable formats: raw-GL TEXB0004 textures, perspective-projection scenes, `.mdl` model objects, light/shape objects, keyframed `AnimationTimeline` property animations, recursive child particles, fog, and HDR-bloom scene keys - all unsupported upstream. Filesystem-wise, mounting was centralized into `Assets::setupAssetLocator` (mounts every `.pkg`, all effect dirs, and injects synthesized bloom/HDR/shape virtual files into the VFS), package lookup is case-insensitive, directory containment is fixed, and all candidate Steam library workshop roots are probed. Only env knob in this area: `LWE_SHAPES=0` (shapes render by default).

---

# 6. Script engine

- **What it does**: The fork fixes how native script modules publish their exports. Upstream called `JS_SetModuleExport()` in the module constructor and (incorrectly) `JS_AddModuleExport()` in the init callback; the fork flips this - exports are *declared* with `JS_AddModuleExport()` at construction and *valued* with `JS_SetModuleExport()` inside the init callback, which QuickJS runs at module instantiation. The init callbacks now also locate the module by matching `getDefinition()` against a global registry and throw a ReferenceError if unregistered (WEVector's own init does the same walk but silently returns instead of throwing). A new `WEVector` module adds `vectorAngle2(vec)` (atan2 in degrees) and `vectorAngle3(a, b)` (clamped-acos angle in degrees, zero-safe).
- **Where it lives**: modified `src/WallpaperEngine/Scripting/Modules/MathModule.cpp` (`wemath_init` MathModule.cpp::wemath_init, ctor MathModule.cpp::wemath_init), `Modules/ColorModule.cpp` (`wecolor_init` ColorModule.cpp::wecolor_init, ctor ColorModule.cpp::wecolor_init); new files `Modules/VectorModule.cpp` (`wevector_vectorangle2` VectorModule.cpp::wevector_vectorangle2, `wevector_vectorangle3` VectorModule.cpp::wevector_vectorangle3, `wevector_init` VectorModule.cpp::wevector_init, ctor VectorModule.cpp::wevector_init) and `Modules/VectorModule.h`. Module registered in `ScriptEngine.cpp::m_modules`. `Modules/ScriptModule.{h,cpp}` are unchanged.
- **Surface**: script-visible module names `WEMath` (`smoothStep`, `mix`, `deg2rad`, `rad2deg`), `WEColor` (`rgb2hsv`, `hsv2rgb`, `normalizeColor`, `expandColor`), `WEVector` (`vectorAngle2`, `vectorAngle3`). Unresolvable imports now throw `could not resolve module '%s'` (`ScriptEngine.cpp::JS_ThrowReferenceError`) instead of returning null silently.
- **Coupling**: self-contained. MathModule/ColorModule changes fix the upstream export-ordering defect; VectorModule is two new files plus three lines in `ScriptEngine.cpp` (include ScriptEngine.cpp::VectorModule, construct ScriptEngine.cpp::VectorModule, emplace ScriptEngine.cpp::emplace) and two lines in `CMakeLists.txt`. A porter could take VectorModule alone trivially; the init-callback restructuring is only needed if upstream's export timing actually misbehaves on their QuickJS version.
- **Tests**: none (no scripting cases exist under `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Cases/`).

### Script module load pipeline: compile-only eval, pending jobs, `init()` hook, error containment
- **What it does**: `queueScript()` was rewritten from a single `JS_Eval` into a staged pipeline: compile with `JS_EVAL_FLAG_COMPILE_ONLY`, report compile errors with module key, evaluate via `JS_EvalFunction`, drain up to 64 pending jobs, detect rejected evaluation promises, then capture the module namespace via `JS_GetModuleNamespace` and invoke a new `init(value)` export before the first `update(value)`. `logJSException` was hardened: detects value-less exceptions (host callback returned bare `JS_EXCEPTION`), and prints the JS `stack` property when present.
- **Where it lives**: modified `src/WallpaperEngine/Scripting/ScriptEngine.cpp` (`queueScript` ScriptEngine.cpp::queueScript, compile ScriptEngine.cpp::logJSException, job drain ScriptEngine.cpp::logJSException, namespace ScriptEngine.cpp::JS_GetModuleNamespace, init call ScriptEngine.cpp::logJSException; `logJSException` ScriptEngine.cpp::logJSException; `call` helper unchanged at ScriptEngine.cpp::call).
- **Surface**: script-facing contract gains an optional `export function init(value)`. Per-module `update()` exceptions are logged at most 3 times per module key then suppressed (`ScriptEngine.cpp::tick`); the write-back of the update result is wrapped in `try/catch (const std::exception&)` and swallowed (ScriptEngine.cpp::tick). Debug env var `LWE_SCRIPTDBG` (read at ScriptEngine.cpp::LWE_SCRIPTDBG) traces register/skip decisions.
- **Coupling**: contained to `ScriptEngine.cpp`/`ScriptEngine.h`. The error-shape fixes (replacing bare `JS_EXCEPTION` returns with `JS_ThrowTypeError`) span EngineObject/InputObject/SceneObject/ScriptPropertiesObject/VectorAdapter/ScriptableObjectAdapter but are mechanical and independently cherry-pickable per file.
- **Uncertain**: the 64-job drain cap silently drops further pending jobs; no code comment states whether that bound was measured.

### Degrees<->radians bridge for `angles_*` scripts
- **What it does**: WE script-space angles are degrees; the engine stores radians. For any script module whose key starts with `angles_` and whose value is a Vec3, the engine creates a persistent `degreesMirror` DynamicValue; each tick it refreshes the mirror from radians, hands the *mirror* to `init()`/`update()` (so linked vector args write through in degrees), and converts the result back with `glm::radians` before storing.
- **Where it lives**: `ScriptEngine.h` `LoadedModule::degreesMirror` ScriptEngine.h::degreesMirror; `ScriptEngine.cpp` mirror creation ScriptEngine.cpp::degreesMirror, queueScript store-back lambda ScriptEngine.cpp::degreesMirror, tick bridge ScriptEngine.cpp::degreesMirror.
- **Surface**: triggered purely by the property key prefix `angles_` (ScriptEngine.cpp::queueScript). Trace env var `LWE_LIGHTDUMP` dumps angles for keys containing `angles_112` every 90 ticks (ScriptEngine.cpp::tick).
- **Coupling**: self-contained in ScriptEngine, but depends on the upstream-unchanged script-key naming convention (`angles_<objectId>`); a porter must confirm key generation matches. Logic lives entirely in queueScript/tick.
- **Uncertain**: the `LWE_LIGHTDUMP` trace hardcodes the substring `angles_112`, so it traces one specific wallpaper's property keys rather than a general mechanism.

### Per-frame tick model
- **What it does**: `CScene::renderFrame` calls `getScriptEngine().tick()` once per rendered frame, before `tickAnimations()` (`src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Wallpapers/CScene.cpp::getScriptEngine`). Tick order: (1) `EngineObject::tick()` - refresh registered audio buffers, then fire due intervals/timeouts (`EngineObject.cpp::JS_Call`); (2) `dispatchCursorEvents()`; (3) per loaded script module, set `m_runningModule`, call `update(value)`, write result back. Interval/timeout callbacks are invoked with `JS_Call` and their results are **not** exception-checked (`EngineObject.cpp::JS_Call`).
- **Where it lives**: `ScriptEngine.cpp::LWE_LIGHTDUMP` (tick), `EngineObject.cpp::tick` (timer tick); call site `CScene.cpp::getScriptEngine`.
- **Surface**: none (no config). `g_Time - g_TimeLast` drives animation timing; `engine.frametime` now clamps non-positive deltas to 1/60 (`EngineObject.cpp::engine_get_frametime`).
- **Coupling**: tick itself is upstream structure; the fork inserts one line (`dispatchCursorEvents()` at ScriptEngine.cpp::dispatchCursorEvents) and the angles bridge. A porter wanting only cursor events can take dispatchCursorEvents + one call-site line.
- **Timer callbacks**: the value a `setInterval`/`setTimeout` callback returns is freed after every fire, and a throw is retrieved and logged, three times per timer and then discarded, so it can never surface in an unrelated later call (`EngineObject.cpp::runTimerCallback`).

### Audio response buffers (`engine.registerAudioBuffers`)
- **What it does**: Scripts call `engine.registerAudioBuffers(resolution)` and receive `{average, left, right}` JS arrays of length 16/32/64 (requested resolution rounds up to the next of 16, 32 or 64 and is capped there, with an error log). The engine keeps the returned arrays alive and rewrites every element from the playback recorder's spectrum bands at the top of every tick, before any script `update()` runs. Capture is mono, so `left`/`right` mirror `average`.
- **Where it lives**: modified `EngineObject.h` (`AudioBufferLink` struct EngineObject.h::AudioBufferLink, `m_audioBuffers` EngineObject.h::m_audioBuffers, `registerAudioBuffers` decl EngineObject.h::registerAudioBuffers, `updateAudioBuffers` decl EngineObject.h::updateAudioBuffers); `EngineObject.cpp` (JS binding `engine_register_audio_buffers` EngineObject.cpp::engine_register_audio_buffers, property registration EngineObject.cpp::engine_register_audio_buffers, impl `registerAudioBuffers` EngineObject.cpp::registerAudioBuffers, `updateAudioBuffers` EngineObject.cpp::updateAudioBuffers, dtor frees EngineObject.cpp::m_audioBuffers, tick call EngineObject.cpp::updateAudioBuffers). Data source: `src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/Drivers/Recorders/PlaybackRecorder.h::PlaybackRecorder` (`audio16/32/64` arrays - the `.h` differs from upstream; the fork also adds an `enabled` flag at PlaybackRecorder.h::PlaybackRecorder).
- **Surface**: script API `engine.registerAudioBuffers([resolution])`, default 16 (EngineObject.cpp::engine_register_audio_buffers), allowed {16,32,64} (EngineObject.cpp::requestedResolution). Debug env var `LWE_AUDIOSTATS` (EngineObject.cpp::LWE_AUDIOSTATS) prints buffer count and first bands every 30 ticks.
- **Coupling**: moderate. EngineObject side is self-contained, but it reads `scene.getAudioContext().getRecorder()` and the recorder's `audio16/32/64` arrays (declared upstream; the fork adds only the `enabled` flag) - a porter also needs whatever fills `audio16/32/64` in the audio driver (see chapter 7).
- **Tests**: none.

### Native persistent `localStorage`
- **What it does**: A new native `ILocalStorage` object replaces upstream's pure-JS in-memory shim. It persists per-wallpaper to `$HOME/.local/share/lwe/storage/<workshopId|filename>.json` (slashes sanitized, `default` fallback; temp-dir base if `$HOME` unset). Two API flavors: WE-style `get/set/remove` (values JSON-stringified on write, JSON-parsed on read with string fallback) and DOM-style `getItem/setItem/removeItem/key/clear/length` (raw strings). Saves are atomic: write `<file>.tmp`, verify stream health, rename over the target.
- **Where it lives**: new files `Scripting/LocalStorageObject.cpp` (ctor+path LocalStorageObject.cpp::load, WE-style LocalStorageObject.cpp::lsGet, DOM-style LocalStorageObject.cpp::lsGetItem, `load` LocalStorageObject.cpp::load, `save` LocalStorageObject.cpp::save) and `LocalStorageObject.h`. Wired in `ScriptEngine.h::LocalStorageObject` (include), `ScriptEngine.cpp::m_localStorageObject` (construct), `ScriptEngine.cpp::localStorage` (global define), `ScriptEngine.cpp::m_localStorageObject` (teardown); added to `CMakeLists.txt` sources.
- **Surface**: globals `localStorage`, constants `LOCATION_SCREEN`/`LOCATION_GLOBAL` exposed as string constants on the object (LocalStorageObject.cpp::LOCATION_GLOBAL) but **accepted nowhere** - no native function reads a location argument; the namespacing exists only in the builtins.js fallback. Env: `$HOME` read at LocalStorageObject.cpp::HOME. No opt-out/kill switch.
- **Coupling**: nearly self-contained - porter needs the two new files, the three wiring points in ScriptEngine, and CMake lines. Note ordering: `installBuiltins()` runs first (ScriptEngine.cpp::installBuiltins), so builtins' JS fallback installs first and the native define at ScriptEngine.cpp::localStorage overwrites it; keep that order.
- **Uncertain**: `LOCATION_*` constants suggest per-screen storage was planned; whether WE scripts passing a location get silent cross-screen leakage is untestable from code (the arg is ignored).

### Engine object: new properties + `setInterval`/`setTimeout` repair
- **What it does**: Adds `engine.screenResolution` (always `{x: sceneWidth, y: sceneHeight}`) and `engine.canvasSize` (same, but `undefined` unless the camera is orthogonal). Fixes upstream's broken timers: upstream stored `EngineObject&` in `engineInstances` but **never inserted into it** (only erased in the dtor) and registered handlers with `JS_CFUNC_generic` though the signatures expect magic - so every setInterval/setTimeout/clear* call hit the not-found path and returned `JS_EXCEPTION`. The fork inserts the instance in the ctor, switches the map to pointers, and uses `JS_CFUNC_generic_magic`.
- **Where it lives**: `EngineObject.cpp` (map EngineObject.cpp::engineInstances, ctor insert EngineObject.cpp::engineInstances, `engine_get_screenresolution` EngineObject.cpp::engine_get_screenresolution, `engine_get_canvassize` EngineObject.cpp::engine_get_canvassize, property registration EngineObject.cpp::engine_get_screenresolution, magic registrations EngineObject.cpp::engine_set_interval); upstream bug visible at upstream `EngineObject.cpp::engineInstances` + missing insert.
- **Surface**: script APIs `engine.screenResolution`, `engine.canvasSize`, `engine.frametime` (clamped), `engine.runtime`, `engine.setInterval/setTimeout` (now functional; cancellation uses the stop-closure they return - there are no `clearInterval`/`clearTimeout` JS bindings). Read-only setters throw `property is read-only` (EngineObject.cpp::engine_set_value). No env/config.
- **Coupling**: confined to EngineObject.{h,cpp}. The timer repair is a self-contained bug fix; screenResolution/canvasSize need `scene.getWidth/getHeight/getCamera().isOrthogonal()` (upstream APIs).
- **Uncertain**: `engine.openUserShortcut` remains a stub returning undefined (both trees).

### Camera transforms API (`thisScene.getCameraTransforms` / `setCameraTransforms`)
- **What it does**: Scripts can read the camera's eye/center as real builtins `Vec3` instances (constructed by calling the global `Vec3` constructor so methods like `.subtract()` exist) and write them back. The setter parses `eye`/`center` objects field-by-field with the current camera value as fallback, then calls `Camera::setScriptedView`, which rejects non-finite or degenerate (eye~center) input and stores a scripted view that recomputes the look-at matrix for perspective scenes.
- **Where it lives**: `SceneObject.cpp` (`makeVec3` SceneObject.cpp::makeVec3, `readVec3` SceneObject.cpp::readVec3, `scene_get_camera_transforms` SceneObject.cpp::scene_get_camera_transforms, `scene_set_camera_transforms` SceneObject.cpp::scene_set_camera_transforms, registration SceneObject.cpp::scene_get_camera_transforms); target method `src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Camera.cpp::setScriptedView` (`setScriptedView`), declared `Camera.h::setScriptedView`.
- **Surface**: script APIs only. Debug env var `LWE_CAMPROBE` (`SceneObject.cpp::LWE_CAMPROBE`, `Camera.cpp::LWE_CAMPROBE`) logs eye/center, capped at 10 (get) / 40 (set) lines.
- **Coupling**: SceneObject additions are self-contained; the setter requires the fork's `Camera::setScriptedView` + `m_hasScriptedView` state in Render/Camera (cross-area dependency - porter needs the Camera diff too).
- **Uncertain**: how `m_hasScriptedView` interacts with the per-frame view recomputation elsewhere in Camera is outside the files I diffed in depth.

### Cursor event system (`cursorEnter/Leave/Move/Down/Up/Click`)
- **What it does**: After a module is evaluated, the engine scans its namespace for any of the six cursor hook exports; if found, the module is flagged `cursorEvents`. Each tick (when any flagged module exists) the engine computes a world-space cursor position from the normalized mouse and scene dimensions, hit-tests each flagged module's object via `CImage::cursorLocalPosition` (which un-rotates the point into the image quad's frame and bounds-checks against half-extents), and fires edge/edge-exit/move/down/up/click hooks with an event object `{worldPosition, localPosition}` of temporal standalone vec3 adapters. Click requires the press to have started inside.
- **Where it lives**: `ScriptEngine.h` (`LoadedModule::{object,cursorEvents,cursorInside,cursorPressedInside}` ScriptEngine.h::ScriptEngine, decls ScriptEngine.h::dispatchCursorEvents); `ScriptEngine.cpp` (hook scan ScriptEngine.cpp::LWE_CURSORDBG, `makeCursorEvent` ScriptEngine.cpp::makeCursorEvent, `dispatchCursorEvents` ScriptEngine.cpp::dispatchCursorEvents, tick call ScriptEngine.cpp::dispatchCursorEvents); hit test `src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Objects/CImage.cpp::LWE_CURSORDBG`.
- **Surface**: script exports `cursorEnter, cursorLeave, cursorMove, cursorDown, cursorUp, cursorClick` (list at ScriptEngine.cpp::CURSOR_HOOKS). Debug env var `LWE_CURSORDBG` (CImage.cpp::cursorLocalPosition) traces exports, probes, edges, and fires. No config keys.
- **Coupling**: needs `LoadedModule` fields, `CImage::cursorLocalPosition` (Render area), `CScene::getMousePositionNormalized` and input context mouse state (both pre-existing). Non-CImage objects never report `inside`, so only image layers get events. Moderately portable: ScriptEngine side is additive; must take the CImage method too.
- **Uncertain**: movement detection uses first-frame `value_or(worldPosition + vec3(1))`, so the very first tick always counts as "moved" - deliberate or not is undeterminable.

### Layer adapter: texture animation control, `thisLayer.size`, and the exotic get_property fix
- **What it does**: Three things. (1) The fork assigns `m_exoticMethods.get_property` - upstream declared the handler but never wired it, so `thisLayer.<prop>` reads through the exotic path were dead. (2) New `thisLayer.size` returns `{x,y}` from `CImage::getSize()` for image layers (undefined otherwise). (3) New `thisLayer.getTextureAnimation()` returns a controller with `pause/play/stop/setFrame/getFrame/getFrameCount/isPlaying` delegating to `CRenderable` texture-animation methods (`stop` aliases pause).
- **Where it lives**: `Adapters/ScriptableObjectAdapter.cpp` (exotic wiring ScriptableObjectAdapter.cpp::scriptableobject_property_get, `textureanim_op` ScriptableObjectAdapter.cpp::textureanim_op, controller builder ScriptableObjectAdapter.cpp::scriptableobject_get_texture_animation, `size` ScriptableObjectAdapter.cpp::scriptableobject_property_get, `getTextureAnimation` ScriptableObjectAdapter.cpp::scriptableobject_property_get); target methods on `src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Objects/CRenderable` (declared via includes at ScriptableObjectAdapter.cpp::CRenderable; definitions in Render area).
- **Surface**: script APIs above. No env/config. Texture-animation methods are no-ops (undefined) on non-renderable layers (ScriptableObjectAdapter.cpp::textureanim_op).
- **Coupling**: controller needs the fork's `CRenderable::pauseTextureAnimation/play/set/get...` methods (Render area). The exotic wiring one-liner is independently cherry-pickable and arguably a pure upstream bug fix. The class also registers a finalizer that frees the per-instance opaque container upstream never released (ScriptableObjectAdapter.cpp::scriptableobject_finalizer).
- **Tests**: none.

### ScriptableObject lifecycle: property registration, animation queue, unregistration
- **What it does**: The base-class ctor no longer registers `origin/scale/angles/visible` from the *group* values (upstream did); per-subclass registrations (e.g. `CImage.cpp::registerProperty`, which existed upstream) now solely govern those names. `registerProperty` still queues the script but now also calls `scene.queueAnimation(value, object)`. A new destructor calls `scene.forgetObjectAnimations(*this)` and `scriptEngine.unregisterScriptable(this)`, which erases all script modules owned by the dying object (freeing their module namespace JSValues and clearing `m_runningModule` if it pointed at them).
- **Where it lives**: `ScriptableObject.cpp` (dtor ScriptableObject.cpp::forgetObjectAnimations, queueAnimation ScriptableObject.cpp::queueAnimation), `ScriptableObject.h::ScriptableObject`; `ScriptEngine.cpp` `unregisterScriptable` ScriptEngine.cpp::unregisterScriptable; scene-side `CScene::queueAnimation` CScene.cpp::queueAnimation / `forgetObjectAnimations` CScene.cpp::forgetObjectAnimations / animated list `CScene.h::m_animatedProperties`.
- **Surface**: none. (Note: `CScene::queueAnimation` logs an unconditional `LWE-TIMELINE` line via `sLog.out` at `CScene.cpp::TIMELINE` for every animated property - Render area, but visible to anyone tailing logs.)
- **Coupling**: the unregister half is self-contained (ScriptableObject + ScriptEngine); the animation-queue half requires the fork's `CScene::m_animatedProperties`/`tickAnimations` machinery (Render area). The dtor ordering matters: `CObject` base must still be alive, which it is (member-typed base).
- **Uncertain**: whether dropping the base-ctor group-value registrations changes behavior for object types whose subclass never re-registers those names - CModel/CLight/CParticle/CImage re-register their own, but `CText` registers `origin`/`scale`/`visible` without `angles`, so text-layer scripts can no longer read or write `thisLayer.angles`.

### Live user-property updates (`applyUserProperties`)
- **What it does**: When the application applies changed user properties (e.g. from the properties UI/socket), it calls `ScriptEngine::notifyUserPropertiesChanged(changed)`; the engine then, per loaded module, rebinds `thisLayer` to that module's object and calls its `applyUserProperties(props)` export with a plain JS object of changed property name -> value. Empty change sets short-circuit.
- **Where it lives**: `ScriptEngine.cpp` (`notifyUserPropertiesChanged` ScriptEngine.cpp::notifyUserPropertiesChanged, `buildUserPropertiesObject` ScriptEngine.cpp::buildUserPropertiesObject), decl `ScriptEngine.h::notifyUserPropertiesChanged`; sole caller `src/WallpaperEngine/Application/WallpaperApplication.cpp::notifyUserPropertiesChanged`.
- **Surface**: script export `applyUserProperties(properties)`. No env/config; driven entirely by the caller in WallpaperApplication (screen-override JSON path at WallpaperApplication.cpp::checkPropertyReload).
- **Coupling**: needs the `LoadedModule::object` pointer (shared with cursor events) and the application-side call site. Small, liftable as: the two ScriptEngine methods + one call site.

### ScriptPropertiesObject correctness fixes
- **What it does**: `add*()` on the script-properties creator now `JS_DupValue`s `this_val` instead of returning a borrowed reference (upstream leaked/mis-counted the ref). `finish()` null-guards the opaque. Property-set exotic now throws a real TypeError before returning -1 (upstream returned -1 with no pending exception, which printed as "[uninitialized]"). `createScriptProperties` is registered with `JS_CFUNC_generic_magic` so the instance-id magic actually arrives (upstream used `JS_CFUNC_generic`, same bug class as the engine timers). Property-get exceptions now carry the property name and message.
- **Where it lives**: `ScriptPropertiesObject.cpp` (`scriptproperties_property_get` ScriptPropertiesObject.cpp::scriptproperties_property_get, `_set` ScriptPropertiesObject.cpp::scriptproperties_property_set, `scriptpropertiescreator_add` ScriptPropertiesObject.cpp::scriptpropertiescreator_add area per diff, `finish` ScriptPropertiesObject.cpp::scriptpropertiescreator_finish, magic fix ScriptPropertiesObject.cpp::createScriptProperties).
- **Surface**: script API unchanged (`createScriptProperties().add*().finish()`); trace env var `LWE_LIGHTDUMP` (ScriptPropertiesObject.cpp::scriptproperties_property_get) logs property lookups, capped at 24 lines.
- **Coupling**: self-contained file; pure correctness fixes, individually cherry-pickable.

### VectorAdapter + vector write-back fixes
- **What it does**: (1) `jsToDynamicValue` fixes: upstream tested `JS_IsNumber(y)` without negation (so valid 2-component writes threw) and never converted z/w to doubles before use; the fork negates the y check and adds the missing `JS_ToFloat64` calls; it also no longer marks the source `Script`-updated when the script returns undefined. (2) `VectorOpaqueContainer` gains `adapterInstanceId`; the finalizer only frees the pooled DynamicValue if the owning adapter instance still exists - preventing frees into a destroyed adapter after engine teardown/hot-swap. (3) Error hygiene: bare `JS_EXCEPTION`/`-1` returns replaced with thrown TypeErrors. SUPERSEDED 2026-09-13: the earlier "unknown property reads return `undefined`" softening came from a `get_property` exotic hook that answered every name itself, which also hid the prototype, so no vector method ever resolved from a script; the hook is now `get_own_property` + `get_own_property_names` (x/y/z/w only, VectorAdapter.cpp::vector_get_own_property) and QuickJS walks the prototype for everything else, so unknown names behave like any JS object (`undefined`, `in` false, enumeration lists x/y/z/w). (4) Operand order: `subtract`/`divide`/`cross`/`mix` compute this-operand-first, `lengthSqr` squares (VectorAdapter.cpp::vector_length_sqr) and `dot` returns a number, matching the WE Vec reference (docs.wallpaperengine.io SceneScript Class Vec3); the earlier note that this matched the builtins classes was wrong: builtins lacked dot/cross/mix/equals/lengthSqr until 2026-09-13.skylightcolor` reads the skylight colour (SceneObject.cpp::get_skylightcolor).
- **Where it lives**: `Adapters/VectorAdapter.cpp` (field VectorAdapter.cpp::adapterInstanceId, finalizer guard VectorAdapter.cpp::vector_finalizer, instantiate sites VectorAdapter.cpp::vectorAdapterInstances, error fixes throughout); `ScriptEngine.cpp` `jsToDynamicValue` ScriptEngine.cpp::jsToDynamicValue.
- **Surface**: behavioral only; one softening - reading an unknown property off a linked vector yields `undefined` rather than an exception.
- **Coupling**: self-contained. The finalizer guard pairs with the fork's engine teardown ordering (adapters reset in `~ScriptEngine` ScriptEngine.cpp::~ScriptEngine).

### PBR mask component combos come from the texture header (2026-09-13)
- **What it does**: The lit image shaders (genericimage2/3/4, generic3/4, chroma4, foliage4, fur4) take a packed `components` mask in slot 2 whose channels are metallic (R), roughness (G), reflection (B) and emissive (A); each channel is gated by a `<COMPONENT>_MAP` combo. The editor marks a painted channel with texture header flag bit 20 + component index (`TextureFlags_ComponentMetallic..Emissive`, Texture.h). The runtime now enables a component combo only when the mask carries that bit (ShaderUnit.cpp::m_textureFlags); an explicit combo in the material or an override still wins. Previously `METALLIC_MAP`/`ROUGHNESS_MAP` were inferred from the absence of a slider constant and `EMISSIVE_MAP` was always on, so an RGB mask (alpha 1 everywhere after upload) with `emissivebrightness 10` lit the whole sprite ten times over (Tron 3427482597: the white blob). The parser keeps the four bits (`TextureFlags_All`, TextureParser.cpp::parseTextureFlags); it used to mask them off. CPass resolves the slot textures before assembling the shader units and hands their header flags in (CPass.cpp::collectFlags); the header is parsed from the `.tex` on every path, so the texcache cannot change the decision.
- **Where it lives**: `Data/Assets/Texture.h` (flags), `Render/Shaders/ShaderUnit.{h,cpp}` (`m_textureFlags`, component loop), `Render/Shaders/Shader.{h,cpp}` (parameter threading), `Render/Objects/Effects/CPass.cpp::setupShaders`.
- **Surface**: behavioral. `LWE_AUDIT` logs `PBRMASKS component <name> enable=<0|1> slot=<n> flags=<bits>` per unit. Library evidence (all 674 textures scanned 2026-09-13): only three masks carry any component bit; Tron's bike mask (bit 20, R painted) and both 3D Earth masks (bit 23, DXT5 alpha painted, `emissivebrightness` bound to a user slider); and the rule reproduces all three intents.
- **Coupling**: self-contained; `Testing/Cases/TextureComponentFlags.cpp` pins the parser side. Not changed: the `REFLECTION_MAP` block is still nested under `REFLECTION` in the shaders themselves.

### Script vectors expose the WE Vec2/Vec3/Vec4 method surface
- **What it does**: Native vectors handed to scripts (`value` in init/update for vector properties, `thisLayer.origin/scale/angles`, `event.worldPosition`, results of vector methods) resolve their prototype methods: copy, equals (epsilon), length, lengthSqr, normalize, add, subtract, multiply, divide (Number|smaller-or-equal vector operand, new vector returned, receiver untouched), dot, cross (Vec3), mix, min, max, abs, sign, round, floor, ceil, toString (space-separated, parseable) in C++ (VectorAdapter.cpp::vector_result); distance, distanceSqr, isFinite, negate, reflect, refract, project, angleBetween, toSpherical, clamp, fract, mod, step, smoothStep in JS, applied to BOTH the builtins classes and the native prototypes, which the engine publishes as `__lweNativeVec{2,3,4}Proto` (VectorAdapter.cpp::JS_SetClassProto, builtins.js `eachProto`). Argument conversion does not throw C++ exceptions across QuickJS frames: a bad operand raises a JS TypeError (VectorAdapter.cpp::vector_get). `jsToDynamicValue` likewise logs and keeps the old value instead of throwing when a script returns a non-vector object (ScriptEngine.cpp::jsToDynamicValue). The builtins `Vec2/3/4` constructors accept a vector argument (copy) as in WE.
- **Where it lives**: `Adapters/VectorAdapter.cpp` (exotic hooks VectorAdapter.cpp::vector_get_own_property, VectorAdapter.cpp::vector_get_own_property_names; argument parsing VectorAdapter.cpp::vector_get; result helper VectorAdapter.cpp::vector_result), `resources/builtins.js` (Vec constructors, `eachProto` blocks), `ScriptEngine.cpp::jsToDynamicValue`.
- **Surface**: behavioral. Library consumers: Tron 3427482597 (`value.copy` in init), 3D Earth 3437487219 (`value.add`, `initScale.multiply`, `thisLayer.origin.subtract`).
- **Coupling**: pairs with the builtins classes (three vector types coexist: native, builtins, and builtins returned by SceneObject.cpp::makeVec3); all accept any `{x,y,z}` operand so they interoperate. Known gaps, not addressed here: assignment to `thisLayer.<prop>` is still a silent no-op (ScriptableObjectAdapter.cpp set_property), `thisScene.getLayerIndex` is absent.
- **Tests**: probe scenes under the parity work dir (vecapi: every method against reference values derived from the WE doc; throw: bad operands must raise JS errors with the engine alive); no Catch2 case; ScriptEngine needs a live CScene.

### builtins.js expansion (Vec/Mat library + guarded fallbacks)
- **What it does**: The embedded preamble grows from ~16 lines to ~886. New: `Vec2/Vec3/Vec4` classes, with scalar-or-vector arithmetic (`add/subtract/multiply/divide/length/normalize`) inline on `Vec2`/`Vec3` only; `distance/negate/reflect/refract/project/angle*/rotate/clamp/fract/mod/step/smoothStep/toSpherical/fromSpherical` added via prototype only-if-missing), full column-major `Mat3`/`Mat4` (`lookAt`, `compose`, `decompose`, `inverse`, `transformPoint/Direction`, `normalMatrix`, Euler extract), JS fallbacks for `WEMath`/`WEVector`, and a namespaced in-memory `localStorage` fallback. Upstream's JS localStorage shim is replaced by this namespaced fallback (both are guarded). Global class bindings are `||`-guarded and the Vec extras are add-only-if-missing, which is defensive idempotency rather than deference to a native occupant (the Mat3/Mat4 prototype methods are unconditional assignments - harmless today since no native Mat3/Mat4 exists - and the internal `__intervals` hook is an unguarded leftover); the native `localStorage` define at `ScriptEngine.cpp::ScriptEngine` overwrites the fallback after install.
- **Where it lives**: `src/WallpaperEngine/Scripting/resources/builtins.js` (whole file is the diff); embedded via upstream's existing CMake machinery (`CMakeLists.txt::builtins` generates `Builtins.generated.h`; present in both trees) and installed by `installBuiltins()` (`ScriptEngine.cpp::installBuiltins`, called at ScriptEngine.cpp::JS_SetModuleLoaderFunc - both upstream).
- **Surface**: script globals `Vec2, Vec3, Vec4, Mat3, Mat4, WEMath, WEVector, localStorage, MediaPlaybackEvent` (the last is upstream). Angles in these helpers are degrees (DEG2RAD/RAD2DEG constants at builtins.js top of IIFE).
- **Coupling**: zero native coupling - drop-in file, build system untouched. The most easily cherry-picked item in this area.
- **Uncertain**: the C++ value bridge (`jsToDynamicValue`) reads plain x/y/z/w fields, so builtins Vecs and native adapter vectors are interchangeable by shape; but `instanceof` checks in scripts would distinguish them - whether any real script does is unknowable here.

---

### Summary (5 lines)

The fork turns the script engine from a skeleton into a working WE-compatible runtime: native script modules (`WEMath`/`WEColor` export timing fixed, new `WEVector`), a staged compile/eval pipeline with `init()`, per-module error suppression, and stack-trace logging. New script APIs: `engine.registerAudioBuffers` (16/32/64-band spectrum arrays refreshed each tick from the playback recorder), persistent per-wallpaper `localStorage` (atomic JSON file under `$HOME/.local/share/lwe/storage/`), `engine.screenResolution/canvasSize`, `thisScene.get/setCameraTransforms`, six cursor event hooks hit-tested against image quads, `thisLayer.size`, texture-animation control, and `applyUserProperties` live-update. Upstream bugs fixed en route: dead `setInterval/setTimeout` (never-inserted registry + wrong CFunc magic), the unwired layer exotic `get_property`, the inverted y-check and missing z/w conversions in vector write-back, a dangling album-art listener, and ref-count/exception-shape errors across adapters. Per-frame model: `CScene::renderFrame` -> `ScriptEngine::tick` -> audio buffers + timers -> cursor dispatch -> per-module `update()` with a degrees<->radians mirror for `angles_*` scripts. Debug surface is env vars only (`LWE_SCRIPTDBG`, `LWE_CURSORDBG`, `LWE_LIGHTDUMP`, `LWE_AUDIOSTATS`, `LWE_CAMPROBE`); no test cases cover any of this under `Testing/Cases/`.

---

# 7. Audio, input, video playback, fullscreen policy


- **What it does**: `AudioContext` no longer holds its driver by reference; it holds a pointer and exposes `setDriver()`, so the concrete driver can be swapped at runtime. A new `NullAudioDriver` (fork-only) satisfies the `AudioDriver` interface while opening no SDL device: `addStream()` returns -1, `getFormat/getSampleRate/getChannels` return fixed FLT/48000/2 (`src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/Drivers/NullAudioDriver.cpp::NullAudioDriver`). `WallpaperApplication::setupAudio()` builds the SDL driver only if some loaded scene actually contains a Sound object and audio is enabled, otherwise the Null driver (`src/WallpaperEngine/Application/WallpaperApplication.cpp::NullAudioDriver`). `ensureAudioForProject()` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::NullAudioDriver`, called from `WallpaperApplication.cpp::advancePlaylist` and `WallpaperApplication.cpp::applyShowCore`) lazily upgrades Null->SDL when a newly shown project has sound objects (never downgrades a live SDL driver, `WallpaperApplication.cpp::ensureAudioForProject`), and also upgrades the recorder to PulseAudio when a project sets `supportsaudioprocessing`, rebuilding the driver against the new recorder first (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::NullAudioDriver`).
- **Where it lives**: new files `src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/Drivers/NullAudioDriver.{h,cpp}`; modified `src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/AudioContext.h::setDriver` (`setDriver`), `AudioContext.cpp::setDriver` (member `m_driver` is now `AudioDriver*`, all accessors dereference it); call sites in `WallpaperApplication.cpp`.
- **Surface**: no flag/env of its own; driven by the existing `settings.audio.enabled` (`--no-audio`-style flags) and per-project sound/audio-processing metadata. Kill switch is implicit: with no sound objects the SDL device is never opened.
- **Coupling**: touches the upstream `AudioContext` class shape (reference->pointer - source-compatible for callers) plus two functions in `WallpaperApplication`. A porter needs: `AudioContext.{h,cpp}` diff, the two NullAudioDriver files, and the `setupAudio`/`ensureAudioForProject` logic. Self-contained otherwise; CMake lines `CMakeLists.txt::NullAudioDriver`.
- **Tests**: none directly.
- **Uncertain**: whether an `addStream()` failure (-1) on a Null driver that was never upgraded is ever user-visible; the header comment (`NullAudioDriver.h::load`) claims it is unreachable, which matches the `ensureAudioForProject` logic though not every `CSound::load` path is traced here.

### 2. Per-object (authored) sound volume in the SDL mixer

- **What it does**: `AudioStream` gains a per-stream linear gain `m_volume` (default 1.0) with `setVolume/getVolume` (`src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/AudioStream.cpp::m_volume`). `CSound::load()` reads the wallpaper-authored `sound.volume` property, clamps to 0..1, and sets it (`src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Objects/CSound.cpp::LWE_NOOBJVOL`). The SDL mix callback multiplies the global `state.audio.volume` by the stream's gain before `SDL_MixAudioFormat` (`src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/Drivers/SDLAudioDriver.cpp::SDL_MixAudioFormat`). Upstream mixed every stream at the single global volume.
- **Where it lives**: modified `AudioStream.h::AudioStream`, `AudioStream.cpp::setVolume`, `SDLAudioDriver.cpp::SDL_MixAudioFormat`, `CSound.cpp::LWE_NOOBJVOL`.
- **Surface**: env kill switch `LWE_NOOBJVOL` (any value forces per-object volume to 1.0), read once at `CSound.cpp::LWE_NOOBJVOL`.
- **Coupling**: small and linear - four files, one call chain. Self-contained cherry-pick; no other mixer exists (SDL is the only real driver).
- **Tests**: none.

### 3. AudioStream resample output-channel fix + per-stream packet cursor

- **What it does**: Two bug fixes in `AudioStream`. (a) In `resampleAudio()`'s modern-FFmpeg path, upstream allocated the resampled buffer using the **input** stream's channel count (`ch_layout.nb_channels`); the fork derives the output layout from the driver's requested channel count via `av_channel_layout_from_mask` (mono/stereo/surround), matching how the swr context was configured at init (`src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/AudioStream.cpp::av_channel_layout_from_mask`; init side at `AudioStream.cpp::av_channel_layout_from_mask` is upstream-identical). On mismatch the function now logs and returns -1 instead of proceeding. (b) The `static int audio_pkt_size` in `decodeFrame()` - shared across all streams - becomes per-instance member `m_audioPktSize` (`AudioStream.cpp::m_audioPktSize`; member declared `AudioStream.h::m_audioPktSize`).
- **Lifecycle and threads**: stream removal takes the list mutex the SDL callback iterates under and frees the mixing buffer (`SDLAudioDriver.cpp::removeStream`); the driver frees the remaining buffers after the device is closed. The reader's end-of-file flush and the playback decode share a codec mutex (`AudioStream.h::m_codecMutex`), and a reader that exits marks itself done and wakes the queue, so the decode side returns silence instead of waiting on a signal that would never come (`AudioStream.cpp::markReaderDone`). Teardown drains queued packets, destroys the queue primitives and closes the input with its custom io context; a clone that fails to queue is the one freed, not the reader's packet; the resampler returns the number of bytes it copied; the loop rewind seeks with plain flags.
- **Where it lives**: modified `src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/AudioStream.{h,cpp}` and `Drivers/SDLAudioDriver.cpp`.
- **Surface**: none.
- **Coupling**: fully self-contained; each hunk lifts independently.
- **Tests**: none.
- **Uncertain**: (a) is only reachable when `FF_API_OLD_CHANNEL_LAYOUT` is unset (newer FFmpeg); not runtime-verified here.

### 4. Audio-processing recorder runtime toggle (script-facing FFT freeze)

- **What it does**: `PlaybackRecorder` gains a public `bool enabled = true` (`src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/Drivers/Recorders/PlaybackRecorder.h::PlaybackRecorder`). Every frame, `AudioDriver::update()` copies `settings.audio.audioprocessing` into it before updating (`src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/Drivers/AudioDriver.cpp::update`). When disabled, `PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder::update()` still drains the pa mainloop but decays all three band arrays toward 0 (`movetowards(..., 0.0f, 0.3f)`), clears `fullFrameReady`, and returns early (`PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::fullFrameReady`) - so audio-reactive scripts smoothly settle to silence instead of freezing on the last frame. Band computations were also clamped with `fmax(0.0f, ...)` so bands can't go negative (`PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::update`).
- **Stream lifecycle**: `pa_stream_unref` alone leaves a capture stream connected on the server, which keeps its own reference until the stream is disconnected - so every re-take orphaned a live, callback-firing stream until the server ran out of descriptors and destroyed the client. `releaseCaptureStream` detaches both callbacks, disconnects a stream that is ready or still creating, then unrefs it and clears the cached sink name (`PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::releaseCaptureStream`). Re-takes are filtered against the live monitor name, so an unchanged default sink no longer replaces a working stream (`PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::currentSink`), and `default_sink_name` is null-checked before it reaches `std::string`. A stream that fails is flagged and retried with a bounded backoff (`PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::RETRY_DELAY_MAX`), and a feed that stops delivering decays the bands to silence instead of holding the last frame (`PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::STARVATION_TIMEOUT`).
- **Capture timing**: the buffer attributes are sized from named millisecond constants (`PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::CAPTURE_LATENCY_MS`, `PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::CAPTURE_BUFFER_MS`), replacing an upstream expression that divided by 100 where its own comment specified msecs - so fragsize was 100 ms and maxlength 7.6 s rather than the intended 10 ms and 750 ms. `update()` drains every pending fragment per frame instead of dispatching a single iteration (`PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::pa_mainloop_iterate`); the read callback already keeps only the newest window, so a queue would be stale by definition and the drain is what stops the shorter fragsize from raising latency instead of lowering it.
- **Where it lives**: modified `PlaybackRecorder.h`, `AudioDriver.cpp`, `PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp`; setting written by socket verb `set-audio` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand`), the `show` arg `audio_processing` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::audio_processing`), and CLI `--no-audio-processing` (upstream, `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv`).
- **Surface**: socket verb `set-audio {enabled:bool}` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand`); `show` arg `audio_processing`; status key `audio_processing` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::audio_processing`); CLI `--no-audio-processing`. Default on (`ApplicationContext.h::ApplicationContext`).
- **Coupling**: three small hunks plus whatever writes the setting. The recorder/decay part lifts alone; live control depends on the fork's command socket (covered elsewhere).
- **Tests**: none.

### 5. Live FFT band dials (`g_LweAudioGain`, `g_LweAudioSmoothMs`)

- **What it does**: Two process-global floats shape the FFT bands. The gain scales every computed band before the destination arrays are written (`PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::update`, `f1 *= g_LweAudioGain`); `set-tuning` clamps it to 0.1..20. The smoothing constant governs how the shader-visible arrays approach those destinations: upstream used `movetowards`, a slew limiter that returns the target outright whenever the change is under its cap, so small changes passed through unattenuated and read as flicker once capture was fast enough to resolve them. The live path instead applies an exponential filter whose coefficient is derived from the frame delta, leaving the response independent of frame rate (`PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::g_LweAudioSmoothMs`). The decay path still uses `movetowards` because it has to reach exactly zero. Both dials are env-seeded and live-settable.
- **Where it lives**: `extern float g_LweAudioGain` read at `PulseAudioPlaybackRecorder.cpp::g_LweAudioGain`; defined at `src/WallpaperEngine/Application/WallpaperApplication.cpp::g_LweAudioGain` and seeded from Config (`Application/Config.cpp::LWE_AUDIOGAIN`). `g_LweAudioSmoothMs` is defined alongside it (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::g_LweAudioSmoothMs`, seeded from `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_AUDIOSMOOTH`) and read in the same loop.
- **Surface**: env `LWE_AUDIOGAIN` (default 1.0, clamped 0.1..20 at read, read by Config, `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_AUDIOGAIN`) and `LWE_AUDIOSMOOTH` (default 90 ms, clamped 0..500 at read, 0 disables smoothing, `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_AUDIOSMOOTH`); socket verb `set-tuning {audio_gain}` / `set-tuning {audio_smooth}` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::audio_gain`, validated at `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::audio_smooth`); status keys `audio_gain` and `audio_smooth` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::audio_smooth`).
- **Coupling**: the gain is one line in the recorder plus its global; the smoothing is the band loop plus a frame-delta member on the recorder. Both are trivially portable but pointless without the socket unless env-seeding is enough.
- **Tests**: none.

### 6. Normalized pointer API on MouseInput (+ GLFW implementation)

- **What it does**: The `MouseInput` interface gains two pure virtuals: `normalized()` - pointer position in [0,1]^2 of the active output, y-down - and `hasPointer()` - whether a pointer position is known at all (`src/WallpaperEngine/Input/MouseInput.h::normalized`). GLFW's implementation (`src/WallpaperEngine/Input/Drivers/GLFWMouseInput.cpp::normalized`) undoes the y-flip its `update()` applies, divides by framebuffer size, clamps to [0,1], and returns nullopt (-> `hasPointer()==false`, `normalized()=={0.5,0.5}`) when mouse input is disabled or the framebuffer is degenerate.
- **Where it lives**: modified `MouseInput.h`, `GLFWMouseInput.{h,cpp}` (`resolveNormalized` helper, `GLFWMouseInput.h::resolveNormalized`); test double updated at `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Input/TestingMouseInput.{h,cpp}` (`setNormalized`, default `{0.5,0.5}`).
- **Surface**: none directly; consumers below. `settings.mouse.enabled` (upstream `--disable-mouse`/socket `set-mouse`, `WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand`) gates `hasPointer`.
- **Coupling**: adding pure virtuals to upstream `MouseInput` breaks every subclass - the fork updates all three (GLFW, Wayland, Testing). Consumers are `CScene::updateMouse` (`src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Wallpapers/CScene.cpp::updateMouse`) and `CWeb::updateMouse` (`src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Wallpapers/CWeb.cpp::hasPointer`). Moderate: an upstream porter must take the interface change plus one implementation per driver they keep.

### 7. Wayland pointer: global-layout coordinates + frozen-pointer semantics

- **What it does**: Rewrites `WaylandMouseInput` coordinate math. `update()` now stores positions in **global scaled layout coordinates** (surface-local `mousePos` + `globalPosition * scale`) instead of surface-local, fixing non-origin monitors (`src/WallpaperEngine/Input/Drivers/WaylandMouseInput.cpp::mousePos`); the Hyprland-IPC fallback path likewise adds `globalPosition` and drops the old y-flip (`WaylandMouseInput.cpp::globalPosition`). `position()` (`WaylandMouseInput.cpp::mousePos`) returns global coords for the focused viewport, falls back to the last IPC-tracked position, then to the viewport's last hover, then to the viewport **center** when the mouse is disabled (previously `{0,0}`). `resolveNormalized()` (`WaylandMouseInput.cpp::mousePos`) implements the [0,1]^2 contract from either the focused viewport or the IPC-tracked global position; mouse disabled -> nullopt. Click getters now read the focused viewport instead of "active output viewport" (`leftClick`/`rightClick`, fork `WaylandMouseInput.cpp::leftClick`).
- **Where it lives**: modified `src/WallpaperEngine/Input/Drivers/WaylandMouseInput.{h,cpp}`. Uses the pre-existing upstream `OutputViewport::globalPosition` field (`src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Drivers/Output/OutputViewport.h::globalPosition` - present at b016d7d, verified).
- **Surface**: none new; same `settings.mouse.enabled` gate. (Upstream already had the Hyprland IPC query; fork reuses it.)
- **Coupling**: contained to the Wayland driver files, but behaviorally entangled with capability 6 (same interface) and with `CScene::updateMouse` consuming normalized coords. The global-coords fix alone would lift as ~3 hunks if upstream's consumers still subtract origins - note the fork comment at `WaylandMouseInput.cpp::updateMouse` says `CScene::updateMouse` subtracts the rendered output's layout origin, position() has no live caller in the shipped fork, so a porter takes it only for interface completeness.
- **Tests**: `Testing/Cases/MouseCoordinates.cpp` is upstream's and its fork diff is cosmetic only (one comment ASCII-fied, 4 diff lines). The behavioral coverage is in `PointerMoveGate.cpp` below.

### 8. PointerMoveGate - dedup/unknown-pointer gate for web wallpapers

- **What it does**: New per-`CWeb` class (`src/WallpaperEngine/Input/PointerMoveGate.{h,cpp}`). `update(optional<normalized>, w, h)` scales to pixel coords and returns `send=false` when the pointer is unknown or size is degenerate - crucially without touching `m_lastSent`, so an unknown stretch can't poison dedup (`Input/PointerMoveGate.cpp::m_movesUnknown`). Identical successive positions are suppressed (`Input/PointerMoveGate.cpp::m_movesSuppressed`); the first known sample always sends, even at (0,0). `CWeb::updateMouse` (`src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Wallpapers/CWeb.cpp::updateMouse`) sends `mouseMove` only when the gate says so, and aims edge-triggered clicks at the last-sent position rather than a fabricated center (`CWeb.cpp::updateMouse`). Counters (`movesSent/Suppressed/Unknown`) feed a 1s-interval debug log.
- **Where it lives**: new `PointerMoveGate.{h,cpp}`; consumer `CWeb.cpp::updateMouse` + member `CWeb.h::m_moveGate`. CMake `CMakeLists.txt::PointerMoveGate`.
- **Surface**: env `LWE_MOUSEDBG` (read once, `CWeb.cpp::LWE_MOUSEDBG`; also gates compositor-side pointer logging in `src/WallpaperEngine/Render/Drivers/WaylandOpenGLDriver.cpp::LWE_MOUSEDBG`).
- **Coupling**: the gate class is fully self-contained; wiring it needs capability 6's interface plus the `CWeb::updateMouse` rewrite.
- **Tests**: `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Cases/PointerMoveGate.cpp` - eight cases: first-sample send, origin send, motion tracking, unknown sends nothing, unknown doesn't poison dedup, degenerate size treated as unknown, the MouseInput normalized/hasPointer contract, counter reset semantics (CMake `CMakeLists.txt::PointerMoveGate`).

### 9. MPV playback: hwdec default off, resource caps, speed, GL state hygiene

- **What it does**: Four changes in `GLPlayer`. (a) `hwdec` default changed from upstream `"auto"` to `"no"`, env-overridable (`src/WallpaperEngine/VideoPlayback/MPV/GLPlayer.cpp::hwdec`); `hwdec-extra-frames` capped at 2 (libmpv default 6) to cut NVDEC VRAM (`GLPlayer.cpp::hwdec`). (b) Demuxer cache capped: `demuxer-max-bytes` 48MiB, `demuxer-max-back-bytes` 16MiB (`GLPlayer.cpp::LWE_MPV_DEMUX_MB`); `vd-lavc-threads` capped at min(4, cores) (`GLPlayer.cpp::LWE_MPV_THREADS`). (c) New `setSpeed(double)` clamps to mpv's 0.01..100 and applies pre-init or live (`GLPlayer.cpp::m_speed`; member `m_speed`, `GLPlayer.h::m_speed`); reached via new fork-only virtual chain `WallpaperApplication::setTimescale` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::setTimescale`) -> `RenderContext::setPlaybackSpeed` (`RenderContext.cpp::setPlaybackSpeed`) -> `CVideo::setPlaybackSpeed` (`CVideo.cpp::setPlaybackSpeed`). (d) GL state fixes: `glColorMask(true,true,true,true)` before mpv render and before clear, and clear-color save/restore around a transparent-black clear (`GLPlayer.cpp::glColorMask`).
- **Where it lives**: modified `src/WallpaperEngine/VideoPlayback/MPV/GLPlayer.{h,cpp}`; speed call chain in `CWallpaper.h::setPlaybackSpeed`, `RenderContext.{h,cpp}`, `CVideo.{h,cpp}`, `WallpaperApplication.cpp`.
- **Surface**: env `LWE_HWDEC` (any mpv hwdec string; default "no", read by Config, `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_HWDEC`), `LWE_MPV_EXTRA_FRAMES` (`GLPlayer.cpp::LWE_MPV_EXTRA_FRAMES`), `LWE_MPV_DEMUX_MB` (positive int, default 48, `GLPlayer.cpp::LWE_MPV_DEMUX_MB`), `LWE_MPV_THREADS` (`GLPlayer.cpp::LWE_MPV_THREADS`); socket verb `set-speed {speed:0..20}` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand`) and env `LWE_TIMESCALE` (`Application/Config.cpp::LWE_TIMESCALE`).
- **Coupling**: (a), (b), (d) are self-contained inside GLPlayer - clean cherry-picks. (c) requires the four-class `setPlaybackSpeed` virtual chain plus the timescale machinery (timescale itself is covered elsewhere).
- **Tests**: none for GLPlayer.

### 10. Fullscreen policy model: Off / Pause / Stop + live ignore-list

- **What it does**: Upstream's boolean `pauseOnFullscreen` is superseded at runtime by a new tri-state enum `FullscreenBehavior { Off, Pause, Stop }` (`ApplicationContext.h::FullscreenBehavior`; settings field `ApplicationContext.h::ApplicationContext`, default Pause `ApplicationContext.h::ApplicationContext`). Pause = freeze + `setPause(true)`; the pause edges are evaluated in `render()` at `WallpaperApplication.cpp::render` and `WallpaperApplication.cpp::render`, now keyed on the policy in effect being Pause (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::fullscreenBehaviorInEffect`) instead of upstream's bare `anythingFullscreen()`. Stop = release the outputs entirely (surfaces torn down) via the output release/acquire machinery, once per edge, in `tickFullscreenGate()` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::tickFullscreenGate`). Only a driver that can release its output surfaces does that (`VideoDriver.h::canReleaseOutputSurfaces`, true only in the Wayland desktop driver); on any other driver Stop is in effect as Pause, so a fullscreen app pauses the wallpaper exactly as Pause does, while status and the saved state keep reporting stop (`Application/FullscreenPolicy.cpp::inEffect`). The Wayland detector gains `recomputeRelevance()`: a registry of all live toplevels (`WaylandFullScreenDetector.cpp::g_liveToplevels`, insert/erase at `WaylandFullScreenDetector.cpp::erase,WaylandFullScreenDetector.cpp::insert`) is re-counted on demand after a roundtrip (`WaylandFullScreenDetector.cpp::recomputeRelevance`), so live-editing the app-id ignore list takes effect immediately instead of on the next window event; base-class virtual added at `FullScreenDetector.h::recomputeRelevance` / stub `FullScreenDetector.cpp::recomputeRelevance`.
- **Where it lives**: modified `ApplicationContext.{h,cpp}`, `Render/Drivers/Detectors/FullScreenDetector.{h,cpp}`, `WaylandFullScreenDetector.{h,cpp}`, `WallpaperApplication.cpp` (policy application, verbs, show args). The relevance predicate itself (`isRelevant` with `pauseOnFullscreenOnlyWhenActive` + `fullscreenPauseIgnoreAppIds`) is upstream-unchanged.
