- **Surface**: CLI `--fullscreen keep|pause|stop` sets the policy after the arguments are parsed, so `--daemon`'s Stop never overrides it in either order; keep also clears `pauseOnFullscreen` as `--no-fullscreen-pause` does, and giving both refuses the launch (`ApplicationContext.cpp::fullscreenWord`). Either of them builds the stub detector only while the command socket is off: with `--listen`, `--api-socket` or `--daemon` the session's real detector is built, so a later `set-fullscreen` or a show's fullscreen policy takes effect (`Application/FullscreenPolicy.cpp::wantsRealDetector`, used by `VideoFactories.cpp::createFullscreenDetector`); with automute on, the default, that detector also mutes the wallpaper's sound while an app is fullscreen, as it does under `--daemon`, and `--no-automute` turns this off (`PulseAudioPlayingDetector.cpp::anythingFullscreen`). `--no-fullscreen-pause` now also writes `fullscreenBehavior=Off` (`ApplicationContext.cpp::noFullscreenPause`); `--daemon` defaults Stop, overridden by `--fullscreen`, persisted state and verbs (`ApplicationContext.cpp::daemonMode`); upstream `--fullscreen-pause-only-active` and `--fullscreen-pause-ignore-appid` unchanged, also spelled `--fullscreen-active-only` and `--fullscreen-ignore`. Socket verbs: `set-fullscreen {behavior:"off"|"pause"|"stop"}` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::fullscreen_behavior`; validated in `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp`), `set-fullscreen-ignore {app_ids:[...<=128]}` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand`; triggers `recomputeRelevance()` at `WallpaperApplication.cpp::recomputeRelevance`); `show` args `fullscreen_behavior` / legacy bool `fullscreen_pause` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::fullscreen_pause`); status keys `fullscreen_behavior`, `fullscreen_pause` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::fullscreen_pause`). Signal SIGUSR2 and verbs `pause`/`resume` share the same pause path via `m_manualPauseRequested` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::resume`).
- **Coupling**: deeply woven into `WallpaperApplication` (render loop, output release machinery, show-args, status). The detector-side `recomputeRelevance` (two detector files + one call site) lifts alone. The Pause/Stop split cannot be lifted without the fork's release plumbing (`apiReleaseOutputs`/`ReleaseReason` - owned by the socket/output-lifecycle area).
- **Tests**: `Testing/Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp` (valid forms) and `Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp` (rejection forms) cover the two verbs' wire validation, not the runtime behavior. `Testing/Cases/FullscreenPolicy.cpp` tests the two decisions as pure functions: the policy in effect for a policy and a driver capability, and the detector kind for parsed command lines.

### 11. Automute detector that never blocks the render loop

- **What it does**: Upstream's `PulseAudioPlayingDetector` asked the server for its sink inputs every frame and spun on the operation until it completed, so a server that stopped answering hung the engine, and a context that had left the ready state handed back a null operation that the loop dereferenced. The fork issues the query without waiting and commits the answer from the end-of-list callback (`PulseAudioPlayingDetector.cpp::sinkInputInfoCallback`), so no frame blocks on the server; the unused server-info round trip is gone (`PulseAudioPlayingDetector.cpp::pa_context_get_sink_input_info_list`). The context carries a state callback (`PulseAudioPlayingDetector.cpp::contextStateCallback`), the constructor waits at most `CONNECT_TIMEOUT` for ready (`PulseAudioPlayingDetector.cpp::pa_mainloop_prepare`), and a lost connection, or one that never reaches ready, is recycled with a doubling backoff capped at `RETRY_DELAY_MAX` (`PulseAudioPlayingDetector.cpp::maintainConnection`). The mainloop is drained at the top of every update regardless of settings. While the server is unreachable the detector reports nothing playing, so losing audio costs automute, not the wallpaper.
- **Where it lives**: modified `src/WallpaperEngine/Audio/Drivers/Detectors/PulseAudioPlayingDetector.{h,cpp}` only.
- **Surface**: none of its own; driven by the existing `--noautomute` flag and the `automute` show arg. Failures log as `Audio detection will ...` lines.
- **Coupling**: self-contained, two files. The reconnect shape mirrors the recorder in entry 4.
- **Tests**: none.
- **Uncertain**: "playing" is still any other process holding an unmuted sink input; the detector does not measure level, so an open but silent stream keeps automute asserted.

### 12. Media source that polls without blocking

- **What it does**: Upstream's `MediaSource::update` compared the clock against a next-update stamp that nothing ever set, so the two second interval handed to `DBusMediaSource` was dead and `performUpdate` ran every frame. Each run was a synchronous `Position` round trip to the current MPRIS player with the library default timeout, so a player that had gone away logged an error per frame and a player that stopped answering could hold the render thread for the full default wait. The fork arms the stamp after each poll (`MediaSource.cpp::m_nextUpdate`), sends the `Position` query as a pending call and collects the reply on a later frame (`DBusMediaSource.cpp::collectPositionReply`), bounds the startup calls with `REPLY_TIMEOUT_MS` (`DBusMediaSource.cpp::REPLY_TIMEOUT_MS`), and forgets a player whose call fails with anything but a timeout, marking playback stopped until a player announces itself again through `PropertiesChanged`. Errors log a few times and then stop (`DBusMediaSource.cpp::ERROR_LOG_LIMIT`).
- **Where it lives**: modified `src/WallpaperEngine/Media/MediaSource.cpp`, `DBusMediaSource.{h,cpp}` only.
- **Surface**: none. The interval is still the constructor argument in `WallpaperApplication::initializeSubsystems`.
- **Coupling**: self-contained. Position now updates at the polling interval rather than per frame, which is what the interval argument always claimed.
- **Tests**: none.

---

**Area summary (5 lines):**
The fork makes audio lazy: a new NullAudioDriver plus a re-bindable `AudioContext::setDriver` means no SDL device is opened until a shown wallpaper actually has sound, and the PulseAudio recorder is likewise upgraded only for audio-reactive projects (`AudioContext.cpp::setDriver`, `NullAudioDriver.cpp`, `WallpaperApplication.cpp::setDriver`). Audio correctness fixes include per-object authored volume mixed in SDL (`SDLAudioDriver.cpp::SDL_MixAudioFormat`, kill switch `LWE_NOOBJVOL`), an output-channel resample fix and a static->per-instance packet cursor in `AudioStream.cpp::resampleAudio`, plus a capture-stream lifecycle that disconnects before releasing so re-takes cannot strand connected streams, a runtime audio-processing toggle that decays FFT bands to zero, a live `audio_gain` dial (`LWE_AUDIOGAIN` / `set-tuning`), a capture path sized in real milliseconds and drained every frame, and band smoothing by an exponential filter rather than a slew limiter (`LWE_AUDIOSMOOTH` / `set-tuning`). Input gains a normalized, presence-aware pointer API (`MouseInput.h::normalized`) with a Wayland global-layout coordinate fix for multi-monitor, and a new self-contained `PointerMoveGate` dedups/suppresses move events to web wallpapers (7 test cases). MPV playback defaults to software decode with capped demuxer/thread/VRAM footprint (env-tunable: `LWE_HWDEC`, `LWE_MPV_DEMUX_MB`, `LWE_MPV_THREADS`, `LWE_MPV_EXTRA_FRAMES`) and supports live playback speed through a new `setPlaybackSpeed` virtual chain. Fullscreen handling becomes a tri-state Off/Pause/Stop policy (`ApplicationContext.h::FullscreenBehavior`): Pause freezes and optionally evicts VRAM, Stop releases outputs entirely where the driver can release its output surfaces and pauses like Pause elsewhere, keep and `--no-fullscreen-pause` still build the real detector while the command socket is on, and the ignore list is live-editable via `set-fullscreen-ignore` with detector recount (`WaylandFullScreenDetector.cpp::recomputeRelevance`) - but the policy is deeply woven into `WallpaperApplication`'s render loop and output-release machinery, so only the detector recount and the env-tunable MPV/audio/input pieces are clean cherry-picks.

---

# 8. The complete switch and surface map


Diffing both files against upstream shows exactly **twenty new flags**, fifteen second names on existing flags (fourteen on upstream flags, `--listen` on the fork's `--api-socket`), three changed flag actions (`--no-fullscreen-pause`; `--clamp`, which takes three more words; and `-h`/`--help`, which print a fixed text), and changed parse/signal behavior. Everything else in the arg parser (`--render-debug`, `--layer`, `--screen-span`, `--fullscreen-pause-only-active`, `--fullscreen-pause-ignore-appid`, `--playlist`, `--scaling`, `--clamp`, ...) already exists upstream - verified by greping the upstream file.

| Flag | Read/defined at | Effect (from code) | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--api-socket` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | `.flag()`; sets `settings.general.apiSocket = true` (field default `false`, `ApplicationContext.h::ApplicationContext`). Gate checked at `WallpaperApplication.cpp::setupApi`. With the socket on, `--fullscreen keep` and `--no-fullscreen-pause` still build the session's real fullscreen detector (`Application/FullscreenPolicy.cpp::wantsRealDetector`). | off |
| `--daemon` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Sets `daemonMode = true`, implies `apiSocket = true`, sets `fullscreenBehavior = Stop` unless `--fullscreen` is given, which is applied after parsing; Stop is in effect as Pause on a driver that cannot release its output surfaces (`Application/FullscreenPolicy.cpp::inEffect`). Also relaxes the "at least one background required" throw at `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv`. | off |
| `--properties-file <path>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Stores path in `settings.general.propertiesFile` (declared `ApplicationContext.h::ApplicationContext`; default `""` via `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv`). Re-read on SIGUSR1 by `WallpaperApplication::checkPropertyReload` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::checkPropertyReload`). | `""` (disabled) |
| `--version` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | `.flag()`; prints `LWE_VERSION`, the first line of `VERSION` read at configure time, alone on one line to stdout and exits 0 while the arguments are parsed. No short form: `-v` is volume. | off |
| `--resclamp <number>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Engine settings group. Config's `ssfactor` knob with source flag, in place of `LWE_SSFACTOR` (`Application/Config.cpp::setFlags`): a number up to 4, 0 or below is no cap (`Application/FlagValues.cpp::clampFactor`). Like every flag of the group it takes one value, is not repeatable, and refuses the launch on any other text with a message that names the flag and ends with the `--help` hint. | `LWE_SSFACTOR`, else 1 |
| `--effectclamp <number>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `clampComposites` knob, in place of `LWE_CLAMPCOMPOSITES`: a number up to 4, 0 or below is no cap (`Application/FlagValues.cpp::clampFactor`). | `LWE_CLAMPCOMPOSITES`, else 1 |
| `--texturecache <on or off>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `texcomp` knob, in place of `LWE_TEXCOMP`: on or off (`Application/FlagValues.cpp::onOff`). | `LWE_TEXCOMP`, else on |
| `--texturedetail <auto or full>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `texdetailAuto` knob, in place of `LWE_TEXDETAIL`: auto or full (`Application/FlagValues.cpp::autoFull`). | `LWE_TEXDETAIL`, else auto |
| `--videodecode <software or auto>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `hwdec` knob, in place of `LWE_HWDEC`: software gives the decoder mode `no`, auto gives `auto` (`Application/FlagValues.cpp::videoDecode`). | `LWE_HWDEC`, else `no` |
| `--color "<b> <c> <s> <hue>"` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `cc` knob, in place of `LWE_CC`: four numbers separated by spaces, brightness, contrast and saturation each 0 to 4 and the hue -180 to 180 degrees, kept in radians (`Application/FlagValues.cpp::color`). | `LWE_CC`, else 1 1 1 0 |
| `--speed <number>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `timescale` knob, in place of `LWE_TIMESCALE`: a number from 0 to 10 (`Application/FlagValues.cpp::decimalInRange`). | `LWE_TIMESCALE`, else 1 |
| `--watchdog <time>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `deadman` knob, in place of `LWE_DEADMAN`: whole seconds, or a whole number followed by s, m or h, 0 to 86400 seconds in all; 0 is off (`Application/FlagValues.cpp::watchdogSeconds`). | `LWE_DEADMAN`, else 300 |
| `--lightdimming <number>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `classicK` knob, in place of `LWE_CLASSICK`: a number from 0.01 to 1000; Config copies it into `g_LweClassicDivisor` once the command line is parsed (`Application/Config.cpp::applyConfigTuning`). | `LWE_CLASSICK`, else 16 |
| `--lightfalloff <number>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `classicExp` knob, in place of `LWE_CLASSICEXP`: a number from 0.5 to 6, copied into `g_LweFalloffExp` the same way. | `LWE_CLASSICEXP`, else 2 |
| `--audiogain <number>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `audioGain` knob, in place of `LWE_AUDIOGAIN`: a number from 0.1 to 20, copied into `g_LweAudioGain` the same way. | `LWE_AUDIOGAIN`, else 1 |
| `--audiosmoothing <ms>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `audioSmooth` knob, in place of `LWE_AUDIOSMOOTH`: a number from 0 to 500, optionally followed by ms (`Application/FlagValues.cpp::milliseconds`), copied into `g_LweAudioSmoothMs` the same way. | `LWE_AUDIOSMOOTH`, else 90 |
| `--socket <path>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Config's `socket` knob, in place of `LWE_SOCKET`: any text that is not empty (`Application/FlagValues.cpp::path`). | `LWE_SOCKET`, else `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/engine.sock`, else `/tmp/lwe-<euid>/engine.sock` |
| `--fullscreen <keep, pause or stop>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Records the word while the arguments are parsed and applies it after `Config::setFlags` (`ApplicationContext.cpp::fullscreenWord`): keep sets `pauseOnFullscreen = false` and `fullscreenBehavior = Off`, pause sets Pause, stop sets Stop. Stop frees the screens only on a driver that can release its output surfaces (the Wayland desktop driver) and pauses as pause does on any other (`Application/FullscreenPolicy.cpp::inEffect`); keep builds the stub detector only while the command socket is off, and with `--listen`, `--api-socket` or `--daemon` the real detector is built so a later policy change takes effect (`Application/FullscreenPolicy.cpp::wantsRealDetector`). Because it runs after the parse, `--daemon`'s Stop never overrides it in either order. Given with `--no-fullscreen-pause`, it refuses the launch with "--fullscreen and --no-fullscreen-pause cannot be used together". | not given: Pause, Stop with `--daemon`, Off with `--no-fullscreen-pause` |
| `--debug <switch>=<value>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Debugging options group; one value per use, repeatable. Looks the plain switch name up in the table of 81 debugging switches and checks the value against that switch's rule (`Application/DebugSwitches.cpp::resolve`), then, while the arguments are parsed, sets the switch's variable with `setenv`, or clears it with `unsetenv` when the value means as normal, which also clears a variable inherited from the environment. A malformed token (no `=`, nothing after it or nothing before it), an unknown name or a value outside the rule refuses the launch with a message that ends with the `--help` hint. | not given: the variables as inherited |
| `--help-debug` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | `.flag()`; prints the list of debugging switches, each with the values it takes (`Application/DebugSwitches.cpp::helpText`), to stdout and exits 0 while the arguments are parsed. | off |
| `--edge <word>` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Second name of `--clamp`. Both take clamp, border and repeat, and the edge words extend, blank and tile, which give the same texture flags as clamp, border and repeat (`ApplicationContext.cpp::extend`); per-screen and window handling are those of `--clamp`. | clamp |
| `--screen`, `--span`, `--wallpaper`, `--steamplaylist`, `--fullscreen-active-only`, `--fullscreen-ignore`, `--mute`, `--no-automute`, `--no-audioreactive`, `--listen`, `--assetsfolder`, `--no-particles`, `--no-mouse`, `--no-parallax` | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Second names appended to existing add_argument calls, so each pair is one argument and giving both spellings of a non-repeatable flag is a duplicate: `--screen` for `-r`/`--screen-root`, `--span` for `--screen-span`, `--wallpaper` for `-b`/`--bg`, `--steamplaylist` for `--playlist`, `--fullscreen-active-only` for `--fullscreen-pause-only-active`, `--fullscreen-ignore` for `--fullscreen-pause-ignore-appid`, `--mute` for `-s`/`--silent`, `--no-automute` for `--noautomute`, `--no-audioreactive` for `--no-audio-processing`, `--listen` for `--api-socket`, `--assetsfolder` for `--assets-dir`, `--no-particles` for `--disable-particles`, `--no-mouse` for `--disable-mouse`, `--no-parallax` for `--disable-parallax`. | as the flag each joins |
| `--no-fullscreen-pause` (modified) | `ApplicationContext.cpp::pauseOnFullscreen` | Upstream only cleared `pauseOnFullscreen`; fork additionally sets `fullscreenBehavior = FullscreenBehavior::Off`. Without the command socket it gives the stub detector; with `--listen`, `--api-socket` or `--daemon` the real detector is still built (`Application/FullscreenPolicy.cpp::wantsRealDetector`). | behavior default `Pause` (`ApplicationContext.h::Pause`) |
| `-h`, `--help` (modified) | `ApplicationContext.cpp::loadSettingsFromArgv` | Upstream printed the help argparse generates from each flag's help string. The fork builds the parser with `default_arguments::none` and registers `-h`/`--help` first, as a `.flag()` that prints the fixed text in `Application/HelpText.cpp::engineHelpText` to stdout and exits 0 while the arguments are parsed, like `--version`. | off |
| `lwe` (the binary run under the name lwe) | `main.cpp::isLweName`, `Application/LweCommand.cpp::isLweName` | Not a flag. When the last component of `argv[0]` is exactly `lwe`, `main` runs name mode right after the crash handler and never reaches the parser: `lwe` and `lwe status`, `lwe off` and `lwe on` go to the command socket (`Api/CommandClient.cpp::request`), `lwe --version` prints the stamp, and every other verb execs the panel launcher as `lwe-ui --lwe <version> <working directory> <words>` (`Application/PanelHandoff.cpp::handOff`). Chapter 1, "The `lwe` name", has the output, the exit codes and the launcher search. | engine mode under any other name |

Other entry-point changes (no new flags, but surface-relevant):

- `ApplicationContext.cpp::parse_known_args`: unknown arguments are now collected from `parse_known_args` and each logged with `sLog.error ("Ignoring unrecognized command line argument: ...")`; upstream discarded them silently.
- `main.cpp::crashHandler`: fork installs a `sigaction` crash handler for SIGSEGV/SIGILL/SIGBUS/SIGFPE/SIGABRT that writes a backtrace to stderr, then re-raises with the default handler. Installed before anything else in this process; `lwe-web-service` and `lwe-web-helper` are separate binaries and install their own handling.
- `main.cpp::main`: under glibc, `mallopt(M_ARENA_MAX, 2)` and `mallopt(M_MMAP_THRESHOLD, 128*1024)` at startup.
- `main.cpp::main`: `SpawnGate::captureAtStartup()` runs before `initLogging`.
- `main.cpp::main` vs upstream: upstream suppressed logging for `--type=zygote`/`--type=utility` CEF subprocesses; the fork deleted that scan and always calls `initLogging()`.
- `main.cpp::appContext`: fork registers SIGUSR1/SIGUSR2 to `app->signal()`; upstream registered SIGKILL (a no-op, uncatchable) - removed. Handlers: SIGUSR1 -> property reload (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::SIGUSR1`), SIGUSR2 -> manual pause toggle (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::SIGUSR2`).

**Coupling**: confined to `main.cpp` and `ApplicationContext.{h,cpp}` (new fields `apiSocket`, `daemonMode`, `propertiesFile`, `fullscreenBehavior` in `ApplicationContext.h::propertiesFile`); a porter needs the two files plus the `FullscreenBehavior` enum, and every consumer of those fields across the engine.

---

### 2. Environment variables (all reads in `src/`, excluding `src/External`)

Upstream read only `HOME`, `XDG_SESSION_TYPE`, `HYPRLAND_INSTANCE_SIGNATURE`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `XCURSOR_SIZE`, `XCURSOR_THEME` (verified by greping upstream). **Every `LWE_*` variable below is fork-introduced**, and the tables below omit reads in `Render/CTexture.cpp` and `Render/CWallpaper.cpp`, as are the new `XDG_DATA_HOME`/`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` read sites noted. Presence-checks mean "any value, even empty, enables" unless noted.

Config (`Application/Config.h::Config`) is the one reader of the thirteen supported knobs: `LWE_SOCKET`, `LWE_SSFACTOR`, `LWE_CLAMPCOMPOSITES`, `LWE_TEXCOMP`, `LWE_TEXDETAIL`, `LWE_HWDEC`, `LWE_CC`, `LWE_TIMESCALE`, `LWE_DEADMAN`, `LWE_CLASSICK`, `LWE_CLASSICEXP`, `LWE_AUDIOGAIN` and `LWE_AUDIOSMOOTH`. It reads them when a static initializer first asks for it (`Application/Config.cpp::fromEnvironment`) and again once the command line is parsed (`Application/Config.cpp::setFlags`), and keeps for each the value as its reader uses it, the text as read, and the source: flag when the knob's launch flag was given (its text checked strictly, `Application/FlagValues.h::FlagValues`), env when the parse used the variable's text, default when the variable is unset or the parse fell back. `status` reports them as `config` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiStatus`).

Each of the 81 debugging variables, the ones `--help-debug` lists by plain name (`Application/DebugSwitches.cpp::helpText`), can also be set at launch with `--debug <plain>=<value>`: the name and the value are checked against the switch table, and the variable is set, or cleared when the value means as normal, while the arguments are parsed (`Application/DebugSwitches.cpp::resolve`).

**API / daemon / web-helper surface**

| Variable | Read at | Gates | Default when unset |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LWE_SOCKET` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_SOCKET` | Overrides the command-socket path | `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/engine.sock` (`Application/Config.cpp::XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`), else `/tmp/lwe-<euid>/engine.sock` (`Application/Config.cpp::fromEnvironment`) |
| `LWE_WEB_SOCKET` | `WebHelper/SpawnConfig.cpp::LWE_WEB_SOCKET` | Overrides the web-helper socket path | `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/web-<pid>.sock` (`SpawnConfig.cpp::XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`), else `/tmp/lwe-<euid>/...` (`SpawnConfig.cpp::defaultSocketPath`) |
| `LWE_WEB_CRASHGUARD` | `WebHelper/HelperClient.cpp::LWE_WEB_CRASHGUARD` (parse `HelperClient.cpp::fromEnvironment`) | Helper crash-loop guard `<deaths>,<windowMs>,<cooldownMs>`; all three or ignored with error | `deaths=4, windowMs=60000, cooldownMs=30000` (`HelperClient.h::CrashGuardSettings`) |
| `LWE_WEB_IDLE_EXIT_MS` | `WebHelper/Service/HelperServer.cpp::LWE_WEB_IDLE_EXIT_MS` | Helper self-exit grace after last instance; read once | `DEFAULT_IDLE_EXIT_MS = 1000` (`HelperServer.h::DEFAULT_IDLE_EXIT_MS`) |
| `LWE_CEFLOG` | `WebBrowser/WebBrowserContext.cpp::LWE_CEFLOG` | CEF log severity: `verbose`/`info`/`error` | `LOGSEVERITY_WARNING` (`WebBrowserContext.cpp::LOGSEVERITY_WARNING`) |
| `LWE_CEFDEBUG` | `WebBrowser/WebBrowserContext.cpp::LWE_CEFDEBUG` | CEF remote-debugging port (1-65535) | off |
| `LWE_DEADMAN` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_DEADMAN` | Dead-man seconds (0-86400) before outputs release with no ping/frames (`tickDeadman`, `WallpaperApplication.cpp::tickDeadman`); on a driver that cannot release the outputs the watchdog logs once per stall and frees nothing | `m_deadmanSeconds = 300` (`WallpaperApplication.h::WallpaperApplication`) |

These three helper/CEF vars reach the helper via inherited `environ` in `posix_spawn` (`WebHelper/SpawnGate.cpp::environ`); no switch encodes them.

**Live tuning dials** (all read by Config at static init and again once the command line is parsed, into globals / the constructor)

| Variable | Read at | Gates | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LWE_CLASSICK` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_CLASSICK` | Classic-light divisor `g_LweClassicDivisor`, clamp 0.01-1000 | 16.0 |
| `LWE_CLASSICEXP` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_CLASSICEXP` | Falloff exponent `g_LweFalloffExp`, clamp 0.5-6 | 2.0 |
| `LWE_AUDIOGAIN` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_AUDIOGAIN` | `g_LweAudioGain`, clamp 0.1-20 | 1.0 |
| `LWE_AUDIOSMOOTH` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_AUDIOSMOOTH` | Band smoothing time constant in msecs `g_LweAudioSmoothMs`, clamp 0-500, 0 disables | 90.0 |
| `LWE_CC` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_CC` | `"b c s h"` floats -> `setColorCorrection` (clamped 0-4/+/-6.4, `WallpaperApplication.cpp::setColorCorrection`) | `{1,1,1,0}` (`WallpaperApplication.h::WallpaperApplication`) |
| `LWE_TIMESCALE` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_TIMESCALE` | Playback timescale >=0 -> `setTimescale` (clamp 0-20, `WallpaperApplication.cpp::setTimescale`) | 1.0 (`WallpaperApplication.h::WallpaperApplication`) |

**Behavior kill switches / escape hatches** (change what gets built; launch-scoped)

| Variable | Read at | Gates | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LWE_SHAPES` | `Data/Parsers/ObjectParser.cpp::LWE_SHAPES` | `"0"` skips shape objects; **default renders them** (`env == nullptr \|\| env != "0"`) | shapes on |
| `LWE_SKIPGATE` | `Render/Wallpapers/CScene.cpp::LWE_SKIPGATE` | `"0"` disables the render-debug skip-object list; **default honors it** | gate on |
| `LWE_KILLLIGHT` | `CScene.cpp::LWE_KILLLIGHT` | Integer light id to drop in `updateLights` | -1 (none) |
| `LWE_NOBLOOM` | `CScene.cpp::LWE_NOBLOOM` | Skips bloom object construction | bloom on |
| `LWE_NOPARTICLES` | `CScene.cpp::LWE_NOPARTICLES` | Skips every particle object | particles on |
| `LWE_NOPARSIM` | `CScene.cpp::LWE_NOPARSIM` | Runs every particle simulation inline in render order; the worker pool is never used | pool when its cost pays |
| `LWE_NOROPEUVFLIP` | `Render/Shaders/ShaderUnit.cpp::LWE_NOROPEUVFLIP` | Disables the genericropeparticle V-flip source patch | flip applied |
| `LWE_FORCECOMBO` | `ShaderUnit.cpp::LWE_FORCECOMBO` | `NAME=value` injected as a combo define into all shader units | off |
| `LWE_BILLBOARD` | `Render/Objects/CParticle.cpp::LWE_BILLBOARD` | Collapses short ropetrails (length < 0.35) to the spritetrail path | off |
| `LWE_NOPREWARM` | `CParticle.cpp::LWE_NOPREWARM` | Disables the 60 s prewarm sim for `startTime > 0` systems | prewarm on |
| `LWE_ANIMFRACTION` | `CParticle.cpp::LWE_ANIMFRACTION` | `"0"` selects the legacy spritesheet anim clock; any other/unset = new fraction clock | new clock |
| `LWE_NOFOLLOWALPHA` | `CParticle.cpp::LWE_NOFOLLOWALPHA` | Disables child-particle alpha-follow | follow on |
| `LWE_NOSPRITEVFLIP` | `CParticle.cpp::LWE_NOSPRITEVFLIP` | Sets sprite orientation-up to +Y (disables V flip) | flip (up = -Y) |
| `LWE_NOCHILDRIDE` | `CParticle.cpp::LWE_NOCHILDRIDE` | Treats all child systems as anchored for axis compensation | off |
| `LWE_HIDESTPARENT` | `CParticle.cpp::LWE_HIDESTPARENT` | Skips rendering spritetrail parents that have children | off |
| `LWE_TRAILMODE` | `CParticle.cpp::LWE_TRAILMODE` | `"exact"` = tau-sampled trail nodes; else span sampling | span sampling |
| `LWE_TINTFIX` | `Render/Objects/CModel.cpp::LWE_TINTFIX` | `"1"` forces combo `TINTMASKALPHA=0`; `"2"` forces a blue `color` constant | off |
| `LWE_SPECFIX` | `CModel.cpp::LWE_SPECFIX` | Forces `roughness=1.0` constant on model pass | off |
| `LWE_FRONTFACE` | `CModel.cpp::LWE_FRONTFACE` | `"ccw"` -> `glFrontFace(GL_CCW)` | `GL_CW` |
| `LWE_CROPOFF` | `Render/Objects/CImage.cpp::LWE_CROPOFF` | `"1"`/`"2"` apply +/-cropOffset to image origin (puppet excluded) | off |
| `LWE_NOFBOCOVERAGE` | `CImage.cpp::LWE_NOFBOCOVERAGE` | Presence **disables** coverage-based FBO sizing | coverage on |
| `LWE_NOSCREEN` | `Render/Objects/Effects/CPass.cpp::LWE_NOSCREEN` | Skips registering the `g_Screen` uniform | registered |
| `LWE_SSFACTOR` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_SSFACTOR` | Supersample factor for the resolution cap (`CScene.h::LWE_SSFACTOR`); above 4 reads as 4 | 1.0 |
| `LWE_CLAMPCOMPOSITES` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_CLAMPCOMPOSITES` | Effect factor: layer composites and effect targets clamp to the largest output times this; 0 or below leaves them uncapped; above 4 reads as 4 | 1 |
| `LWE_CLAMPOUTPUT` | `CScene.cpp::LWE_CLAMPOUTPUT` | Test override of the cap's screen size, `WxH` | largest screen (`CScene.cpp::largestOutputSize`) |
| `LWE_TEXDETAIL` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_TEXDETAIL` | Any value other than `"auto"`, empty included, disables mip-residency capping | on (`auto`) |
| `LWE_TEXCAP` | `MipResidency.cpp::LWE_TEXCAP` | Test override of cap dimension (>=256) | live output query, fallback 4096 (`MipResidency.cpp::capDimension`) |
| `LWE_MPV_DEMUX_MB` | `VideoPlayback/MPV/GLPlayer.cpp::LWE_MPV_DEMUX_MB` | mpv `demuxer-max-bytes` in MiB | 48 |
| `LWE_MPV_THREADS` | `GLPlayer.cpp::LWE_MPV_THREADS` | mpv `vd-lavc-threads` | `min(4, cores)` |
| `LWE_HWDEC` | `Application/Config.cpp::LWE_HWDEC` | mpv `hwdec` property string | `"no"` |
| `LWE_MPV_EXTRA_FRAMES` | `GLPlayer.cpp::LWE_MPV_EXTRA_FRAMES` | mpv `hwdec-extra-frames` | `"2"` |
| `LWE_MPV_REALSYNC` | `GLPlayer.cpp::LWE_MPV_REALSYNC` | Restore mpv real ARB_sync entry points + direct rendering (fence stubs otherwise) | off |
| `LWE_WINTITLE` | `Render/Drivers/GLFWOpenGLDriver.cpp::LWE_WINTITLE` | Window title for EXPLICIT_WINDOW mode | `"wallpaperengine"` |
| `LWE_OVERLAY_TEXT` | `Render/OverlayLabel.cpp::LWE_OVERLAY_TEXT` | Non-empty string seeds the GL text overlay drawn each frame | off |
| `LWE_OVERLAY_FONT` | `Render/OverlayLabel.cpp::LWE_OVERLAY_FONT` | Path of the overlay's font face, tried before the built-in DejaVu/Liberation list | built-in list |
| `LWE_OVERLAY_SIZE` | `Render/OverlayLabel.cpp::LWE_OVERLAY_SIZE` | Heading pixel size at 2160 px viewport height, 8..200; body lines draw at two thirds | 40 |
| `LWE_MOUSE_POS` | `CScene.cpp::LWE_MOUSE_POS` | `"fx,fy"` pins a synthetic normalized mouse position | off |
| `LWE_NOOBJVOL` | `Render/Objects/CSound.cpp::LWE_NOOBJVOL` | Ignores authored per-sound volume, plays at 1.0 | authored volume |

| Variable | Read at | Output |
|---|---|---|
| `LWE_TIMESTATS` | `WallpaperApplication.cpp::LWE_TIMESTATS` | g_Time vs wall-clock drift stats every 5 s |
| `LWE_AUDIT` | `Render/TextureCache.cpp::LWE_AUDIT`; `ShaderUnit.cpp::LWE_AUDIT`; `CModel.cpp::LWE_AUDIT` | Texture-resolve, PBR-mask combo, model GL-state audits |
| `LWE_TEXCACHEDUMP` | `TextureCache.cpp::LWE_TEXCACHEDUMP` | Eviction-survivor list |

| `LWE_CURSORDBG` | `Scripting/ScriptEngine.cpp::LWE_CURSORDBG`; `CImage.cpp::LWE_CURSORDBG` | Cursor-hook export and hit-quad logging |

**Non-`LWE_` reads, fork-new sites**: `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` at `Application/Config.cpp::XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (the command socket) and `SpawnConfig.cpp::XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`; `XDG_DATA_HOME` at `WallpaperApplication.cpp::XDG_DATA_HOME` (lwe wallpaper library roots); `XDG_STATE_HOME` and, as its fallback, `HOME` at `StatePaths.h::root` (the one resolver for every state path: the engine's files, its log, the CEF log, the probe dumps; `WallpaperApplication.cpp::runtimeStateDir` delegates to it); `HOME` at `ShaderUnit.cpp::HOME` (the dump gate), `WallpaperApplication.cpp::HOME`, `Scripting/LocalStorageObject.cpp::HOME`. **Preserved upstream reads**: `HOME` (`Steam/FileSystem/FileSystem.cpp::HOME`), `XDG_SESSION_TYPE` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::XDG_SESSION_TYPE`), `HYPRLAND_INSTANCE_SIGNATURE`+`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (`Input/Drivers/WaylandMouseInput.cpp::HYPRLAND_INSTANCE_SIGNATURE`), `XCURSOR_SIZE`/`XCURSOR_THEME` (`WaylandOutputViewport.cpp::XCURSOR_SIZE`).

**Coupling**: each var is read at 1-4 sites, almost all behind `static const` one-shot gates. Individually trivial to cherry-pick or drop; no shared infrastructure except the InstrumentRegistry (below) and Config, the one reader of the thirteen supported knobs (`Application/Config.cpp::fromEnvironment`, with its `lweEnvFloat` helper).

**Tests**: `Testing/Cases/InstrumentRegistry.cpp` covers the three registry-backed vars, `Testing/Cases/Config.cpp` covers the thirteen knobs Config reads (value and source unset, set and empty, and one malformed or edge value each), and `Testing/Cases/CommandSocket.cpp` covers `LWE_SOCKET` through `defaultSocketPath`; no other env var has direct test coverage.

---

### 3. Command API verb & argument vocabulary (`src/WallpaperEngine/Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp` - fork-only file)

`KNOWN_VERBS` at `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::KNOWN_VERBS` (30 verbs):

`status`, `show`, `quit`, `set-skip`, `list-objects`, `rotate-set`, `playlist-set`, `lanes-set`, `next`, `prev`, `ping`, `pause`, `resume`, `release-outputs`, `acquire-outputs`, `set-fullscreen`, `set-fullscreen-ignore`, `set-fps`, `set-speed`, `set-volume`, `set-mouse`, `set-audio`, `set-parallax`, `set-particles`, `set-instrument`, `set-tuning`, `set-app-conditions`, `set-overlay`, `set-fit`, `schedule-set`.

Request envelope: integer `id` required (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`), string `cmd` (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`), optional object `args` (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); nesting capped at depth 64 pre-parse (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); unknown verbs rejected (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`).

**`show` args** (validated in `validateShowArgs`, `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs`; `id` itself at `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse` matching `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`, `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validBackgroundId`):

| Arg | Type / range | Cited at |
|---|---|---|
| `cc` | `[b,c,s,h]` floats; b/c/s 0-4, hue +/-6.4 | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `speed` | number 0-20 | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `properties` | object <=64 entries; keys `[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,64}`; values string <=256 chars / number / bool | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `scaling` | `stretch\|fit\|fill\|default` | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `clamp` | `clamp\|border\|repeat` | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `volume` | int 0-128 | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `audio_processing`, `mouse`, `automute`, `fullscreen_pause` | booleans | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `fullscreen_behavior` | `off\|pause\|stop` | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `skip_objects` | <=256 ints, 0-1 000 000 | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `skip_effects` | <=64 ints, 0-10 000 000 | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `ssfactor`, `clampcomposites`, `texcomp`, `texdetail` | finite numbers no greater than 4 (0 or below off), bool, `auto\|full`; the quality switches read at scene load, omitted = the launch value (the flag, else the environment) | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `ui_id` | string <=128, opaque echo | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs` |
| `fit` | object: `zoom` 1-2, `pan_x`/`pan_y` -1..1, finite | `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateFit` |

**`rotate-set`** (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`): `entries` array <=512 (each = show-args + `id`, re-validated via `validateShowArgs` `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`), `interval_s` int 15-604800, `order` = `sequential|shuffle|static` (`random` accepted for one release), booleans `avoid_repeat`/`enabled`, `label` <=128 chars.

**Other verbs' args**: `set-skip` `ids` <=256 ints 0-1e6 (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); `set-fps` int 1-480 (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); `set-speed` 0-20 (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); `set-tuning` >=1 of `classic_k`/`classic_exp`/`audio_gain`/`audio_smooth`, finite numbers (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); `set-volume` int 0-128 (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); `set-mouse`/`set-audio`/`set-parallax`/`set-particles` `enabled` bool (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); `set-instrument` `name` string + `enabled` bool (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); `set-fullscreen-ignore` `app_ids` <=128 non-empty strings <=128 chars (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); `set-fullscreen` `behavior` = `off|pause|stop` (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`). `set-app-conditions` `behavior` = `off|pause|stop`, optional `names` <=128 non-empty strings (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`). `set-fit` optional `lane` <=64 chars, optional `layer` = `lane|wallpaper` (default lane), optional `id` (the wallpaper a wallpaper-layer push is for; refused when another is on screen), >=1 of `zoom` 1-2 / `pan_x` / `pan_y` -1..1 (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateFit`). `schedule-set` `enabled` bool + `entries` <=8 of {`at` HH:MM 24-hour, `playlist` [A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}}, >=2 entries when enabled, every playlist previously sent (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::parse`); `lanes-set` lane entries take an optional `manual` bool. Replies: `accepted`/`done`/`failure` builders `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::accepted`, `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::done`, `Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::failure`.

**Coupling**: `CommandDispatcher.{h,cpp}` + `CommandServer.{h,cpp}` are fork-only and self-contained; execution side lives in `WallpaperApplication.cpp` (`apiShow` `WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiShow`, `apiRotateSet` `WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiRotateSet`, `instrumentSet` call `WallpaperApplication.cpp::instrumentSet`, status `WallpaperApplication.cpp::apiStatus`) - that handler layer is woven into the app class, so a porter takes the Api/ directory plus the handler block.

**Tests**: `Testing/Cases/CommandDispatcher.cpp`, `Testing/Cases/CommandSocket.cpp` (incl. the `LWE_SOCKET` override case at `CommandSocket.cpp::LWE_SOCKET`).

---

### 4. Instrument registry (`src/WallpaperEngine/Logging/InstrumentRegistry.{h,cpp}` - fork-only)

- **What it is**: a fixed 3-entry table of runtime-toggleable log gates (`Logging/InstrumentRegistry.cpp::WallpaperEngine::Logging`): `LWE_PARTSTATS`, `LWE_TWINKLEPROBE`, `LWE_ROPETRAILPROBE`. Each entry is `{name, atomic<bool> on, atomic<uint32> epoch}`; lookup is a lock-free `strcmp` scan (`Logging/InstrumentRegistry.cpp::g_entries`).
- **Semantics**: `instrumentSet` bumps the epoch only on an off->on edge (`Logging/InstrumentRegistry.cpp::instrumentSet`); `instrumentSeedFromEnv` seeds initial state from getenv and sets epoch 1 for launch-enabled entries (`Logging/InstrumentRegistry.cpp::instrumentSeedFromEnv`), called once at the top of `WallpaperApplication::show()` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::instrumentSeedFromEnv`). Consumers poll `instrumentOn`/`instrumentEpoch` per frame and reset latched accumulators on epoch change (`CParticle.cpp::instrumentOn`).
- **Surface**: toggled live via the `set-instrument` socket verb (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::handleApiCommand`); `instrumentsEnabled()` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::instrumentsEnabled`) feeds status output. Launch-time env vars of the same names still work via seeding. Build-affecting switches (LWE_TEXCOMP and friends) stay refused here; their per-show door is `show`. The header comment (`Logging/InstrumentRegistry.cpp::WallpaperEngine::Logging`) and test (`Testing/Cases/InstrumentRegistry.cpp::LWE_TEXCOMP`) document that build-affecting switches (e.g. `LWE_TEXCOMP`) are deliberately *refused* - `instrumentKnown`/`instrumentSet` return false for unregistered names.
- **Coupling**: fully self-contained module; consumers opt in per call site. Trivially liftable alone.
- **Tests**: `Testing/Cases/InstrumentRegistry.cpp` covers known/unknown names, epoch edges, re-enable no-op, and `instrumentsEnabled` formatting.

---

### 5. Config-file keys

- **`config.json` (Wallpaper Engine's own)**: the fork consumes the **same keys as upstream** - `steamuser` section and playlist entries (`name`, `settings.delay` default 60, `settings.mode` default `"timer"`, `settings.order` default `"sequential"`, `settings.updateonpause`, `settings.videosequence`, `items`) at `ApplicationContext.cpp::parsePlaylistSettings`. The only fork change is in the parse call itself: `JSON::parse(configFile)` -> `Data::JSON::parseLenient(contents)` with error logged instead of thrown (`ApplicationContext.cpp::parseConfigJson`). **No new config.json keys.**
- **`--properties-file`** (fork-new config surface): JSON of shape `{screen: {property: value}}`, re-read on SIGUSR1; per-screen keys are matched against each project's properties, string values used raw, non-strings `dump()`ed, changes pushed via `Property::update(..., User)` and script/web notification (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::checkPropertyReload`).
- **lwe wallpaper library** (fork-new lookup, not a keys file): `$XDG_DATA_HOME/lwe/wallpapers/<id>/project.json` and `~/.local/share/lwe/wallpapers/<id>/project.json`, consulted before the Steam workshop roots (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::XDG_DATA_HOME` resolve, `WallpaperApplication.cpp::XDG_DATA_HOME` enumerate). `project.json` itself is parsed with `parseLenient` (`WallpaperApplication.cpp::loadBackground`).

**Uncertain**: whether `parseLenient` accepts a formally different grammar than upstream's strict `JSON::parse` (e.g. comments/trailing commas) is defined in `src/WallpaperEngine/Data/JSON.cpp`, which is not examined here - only its call sites. The fork introduces no new *keys*, but the accepted syntax may be broader.

---

### Area summary (5 lines)

The fork adds exactly twenty CLI flags - `--api-socket`, `--daemon`, `--properties-file`, `--version` (`ApplicationContext.cpp::apiSocket`), the thirteen Engine settings flags, one per supported knob (`ApplicationContext.cpp::setFlags`), `--fullscreen` (`ApplicationContext.cpp::fullscreenWord`), `--debug` (`Application/DebugSwitches.cpp::resolve`) and `--help-debug` (`Application/DebugSwitches.cpp::helpText`) - and fifteen second names on existing flags, plus startup crash-handler/mallopt changes and SIGUSR1/SIGUSR2 handlers in `main.cpp`. It introduces 96 `LWE_*` environment variables (upstream had none): the 81 debugging switches `--help-debug` lists, the thirteen supported settings Config reads, `LWE_DAEMON` and `LWE_NOPARTICLES`. 86 are enumerated in the tables above; the remaining ten are the `LWE_TEXCOMP`/`LWE_SRGBALL`/`LWE_SRGBALBEDO` texture gates and the `LWE_PRESENTTRACE`/`LWE_BASELEVEL_PROBE`/`LWE_MASKAUDIT` probes, covered in chapter 3, the three instrument names (section 4), and `LWE_DAEMON` (chapter 2). Config reads the thirteen supported knobs at static init and again once the command line is parsed, the tuning dials via its `lweEnvFloat` clamp helper (`Application/Config.cpp::fromEnvironment`), and the three instrument names are read via the registry's seed loop; the rest at cited one-shot `getenv` sites. A fork-only unix-socket command API (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::KNOWN_VERBS`) exposes 30 verbs with a strictly validated per-`show` property vocabulary (`Api/CommandDispatcher.cpp::validateShowArgs`). A 3-entry runtime InstrumentRegistry (`Logging/InstrumentRegistry.cpp::WallpaperEngine::Logging`) makes `LWE_PARTSTATS`/`LWE_TWINKLEPROBE`/`LWE_ROPETRAILPROBE` toggleable via the `set-instrument` verb, seeded from env at startup. Config-file consumption is upstream-identical in keys but parsed leniently, with the fork adding the SIGUSR1-reloaded properties file and the `~/.local/share/lwe/wallpapers` library root.

---

# 9. The control panel (lwe-ui)


**Tree status: `lwe-ui/` is 100% fork-only.** Upstream commit b016d7d has no `lwe-ui` directory (the upstream tree contains no such directory). Nothing upstream was modified by this area; it is a standalone PySide6+QML Python package (`pyproject.toml::lwe-discover` installs the `lwe-ui` and `lwe-discover` console scripts; requires Python >=3.11, PySide6 >=6.6, Pillow). Its coupling to the fork is entirely through the fork's *engine-side* additions - the daemon Unix-socket API, the fork-new `--daemon` flag, the pre-existing `--layer`/`--clamp` flags it also drives, and `LWE_*` env vars - never through shared source.

---

### Engine daemon API client (`api_client`)
- **What it does**: Stdlib-only Unix-socket client for the engine's command API: one JSON object per line, replies correlated by id, on the socket the engine uses, found as the engine finds it: `LWE_SOCKET` when set and not empty, used as written, else `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/engine.sock` when that variable is set and not empty, else `/tmp/lwe-<euid>/engine.sock` (`api_client.py::socket_path`). Short verbs answer once with `status="done"`; `show` answers `accepted` first, then `done` after the scene load. Every call is one bounded connect-send-read (3 s ack timeout, 30 s for `show` done, 64 KiB reply cap); a dead or absent engine returns `None`, never raises (`api_client.py::request`). Each request records how it ended, which `last_class` returns for the calling thread: `ok` (a done reply with ok true), `refused` (ok false), `uncertain` (it may have run: something failed after the connect, or the reply was neither) or `away` (nothing ran) (`api_client.py::last_class`), and `acked` whether it read an accepted reply while it waited for the final one (`api_client.py::acked`).
- **Where it lives**: fork-only `lwe-ui/src/lwe_ui/api_client.py`.
- **Surface**: env override `LWE_SOCKET` (`api_client.py::LWE_SOCKET`); default socket path (`api_client.py::XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`). Full verb surface the panel can drive, each a thin function:
  - `show` with per-wallpaper resolved args: `id`, `cc` [b,c,s,hue_rad], `speed`, `properties` (PROP_ strings), `scaling`, `clamp`, `volume` 0..128, `audio_processing`, `mouse`, `automute`, `fullscreen_pause`, `fullscreen_behavior`, `skip_objects`, `ui_id` identity echo, `fit` {zoom, pan_x, pan_y}, `ssfactor` and `clampcomposites` (numbers at most 4, 0 or below off) / `texcomp` / `texdetail` only when the wallpaper set them (`api_client.py::PROP_`)
  - `status` (`api_client.py::status`), `playlist-set` (slug, entries = complete show-args in stored order, numbered parts, interval clamped 15..604800 s, order, avoid_repeat, label clipped to 128 bytes) (`api_client.py::playlist_set`), `lanes-set` (lane id, playlist slug, enabled, optional group and fit) (`api_client.py::lanes_set`), `rotate-set` kept as a client function the panel no longer sends (`api_client.py::rotate_set`), `next`/`prev` ack-only (`api_client.py::next_wallpaper`), `ping` dead-man heartbeat (`api_client.py::ping`)
  - `list-objects` (`api_client.py::list_objects`), `set-skip` (`api_client.py::set_skip`), `set-fps` 1..480 (`api_client.py::set_fps`), `set-speed` 0..20 (`api_client.py::set_speed`), `set-volume` (`api_client.py::set_volume`), `set-app-conditions` (`api_client.py::set_app_conditions`), `set-mouse` (`api_client.py::set_mouse`), `set-audio` (`api_client.py::set_audio`), `set-tuning` partial-update (`api_client.py::set_tuning`), `set-fit` partial-update of one layer, `layer` = lane or wallpaper (`api_client.py::set_fit`), `set-parallax` (`api_client.py::set_parallax`), `set-particles` (rebuilds scene) (`api_client.py::set_particles`), `set-fullscreen-ignore` substring app_ids (`api_client.py::set_fullscreen_ignore`), `set-instrument` (`api_client.py::set_instrument`), `set-fullscreen` off|pause|stop (`api_client.py::set_fullscreen`)
  - Raw `release-outputs` / `acquire-outputs` via `request()` in `bench_courier.py::resume`.
- **Coupling**: Self-contained module; depends only on the fork's engine daemon API existing. Lift-alone = this file plus an engine that speaks the verbs.
- **Tests**: `lwe-ui/tests/test_api_client.py`, `test_api_shownow.py`.
- **Uncertain**: none.

### App shell: entry point, tray, single instance
- **What it does**: `python -m lwe_ui` and the `lwe-ui` script both run `launch.main` (`launch.py::main`). A first argument `--lwe` is a command handed over by the engine run as `lwe` (`--lwe <version> <folder> <words>...`): it enters the folder when it exists and runs the words through the command entry (`cli/__init__.py::main`) without importing Qt. The service verbs (`cli/verbs/service.py::run`) read systemd's state, rebuild engine-env and the unit and run daemon-reload before each start and restart, hold the sync lock from recording BUNDLE in the sync marker through the launch, the wait of up to 20 s for the engine and their own sync_all, so no other drain runs in between and a failed or interrupted launch leaves an ordinary BUNDLE for the next drain (`cli/verbs/service.py::_launch`), take as the engine only one whose status pid is a new, non-zero MainPID, a restart reading the old MainPID once it holds the lock (`push.py::wait_ready`), and use both only for timing: a sync that reached the old engine records that pid as served, and the new engine is bundled by the first delivery that sees it (`push.py::_unserved`), never enable with --now, and call systemctl only through `engine/daemon_unit.py::RUNNER`. The command entry writes every line as UTF-8 through one cleaner (`cli/__init__.py::clean`), so a byte that is not UTF-8 prints as U+FFFD, as in the engine's output, and it stops with exit 141, printing nothing, when its output pipe closes. The command entry's verbs are the modules in `cli/verbs/` (`cli/registry.py::discover`); `help` (`cli/verbs/help.py::run`) prints the fixed help screens held in `cli/help_text.py` and, for `help --debug`, runs the engine's `--help-debug` and prints its switch list under `cli/help_text.py::DEBUG_HEADER` in place of the engine's own header. `debug` (`cli/verbs/debug.py::run`) saves a debugging switch for the next service start as its engine variable, one line in engine-env written through `daemon_unit.py::write_env`, and sends the engine nothing; with no screen found it saves nothing. It checks the switch and its value as the engine does, against a copy of the engine's switch table that `test_cli_debug_table.py` keeps equal to it (`debug_table.py::resolve`). `get` and `config` (`cli/verbs/settings.py::_get`, `cli/verbs/settings.py::_config`) read saved values through `settings_table.py::read`, each with where it comes from (settings.conf, the default, the active playlist's file, engine-env or the running engine); they take no lock and write nothing, and a running engine from another build is named on stderr while the read goes on. A setting command (`cli/verbs/settings.py::_set`) saves a panel setting as one settings.conf line and sends the engine nothing; a restart setting (`cli/verbs/settings.py::_restart_write`) saves its line and, still holding the settings lock, rewrites engine-env through `daemon_unit.py::write_env`, never the unit; watchdog and color are engine-env lines with no settings.conf key. `order` and `interval` (`cli/verbs/playlist.py::_change`) change the MODE or INTERVAL line of the derived active playlist's file (`settings_table.py::derived_active_playlist`), the playlist the engine is bound to while its schedule is on, else the saved active playlist, and never write ACTIVE_PLAYLIST; the change runner then sends that playlist's transfer and the lane's enabled state (`push.py::run_change`), and `order load` and `interval load` (`cli/verbs/playlist.py::_load`) send the playlist again under the sync lock and write nothing. `schedule` (`cli/verbs/schedule.py::run`) reads day's and night's rows of the SCHEDULE line through `storage/schedule.py::parse_rows` in stored order and edits one field through `storage/schedule.py::set_field`, never sorting, dropping or cutting a row; bare `schedule` takes no lock and writes nothing, and `schedule on|off` and the time and playlist edits save under the settings lock through `push.py::save_change` with the schedule row, which sends every scheduled playlist and then schedule-set, never lanes-set or a manual switch. `show` (`cli/verbs/running.py::_show`) prints the pick line of the wallpaper it picked, then, once status answers, shows it through `push.py::show_final` under the sync lock until the load finishes, set-tuning only after a done ok; it never starts the service and writes nothing. `next` and `prev` (`cli/verbs/running.py::_step`) read status, then send the engine's next or prev under the sync lock and wait for its final reply (`api_client.py::next_wallpaper`, `api_client.py::prev_wallpaper`), writing nothing and leaving the playlist timer as it is; `pause` (`cli/verbs/running.py::_pause`) saves ROTATION_ENABLED through the change runner (`push.py::save_change`), whose own push is lanes-set with the lane's enabled state and never set-speed. `playlist` lists the playlists numbered by name and marks the one playing; `playlist <p>` (`cli/verbs/playlist.py::_switch`) saves ACTIVE_PLAYLIST through the change runner, which binds p, manual while the engine's schedule is on, and with the schedule on and the service not answering it refuses with exit 2 before anything is saved, since the engine takes a hand switch only while it runs (`push.py::SwitchRefused`). A live or next-wallpaper setting (`cli/verbs/settings.py::_live_write`) saves its line and sends the contract's targeted push through `push.py::run_change`, the runner's outcome as its receipt; speed 0 and audiosmoothing go to the engine under sync and are not saved (`cli/verbs/settings.py::_engine_only`). `reload` (`cli/verbs/settings.py::_reload_run`) checks every file lwe reads from its raw text through `cli/validate.py::check_all`, refuses with every error named by file and line and nothing written, prints what changed since its own snapshot under the panel state folder (`cli/validate.py::changes`), then makes the playlist cleanup, runs sync_all with the re-show of the wallpaper on screen, rebuilds engine-env and names the settings that wait for a service restart (`cli/verbs/backup.py::restart_line`). Before any verb but `help` runs, the command entry refuses with exit 1 a sender whose version stamp differs from the panel's (`version.py::sender_refusal`) and a panel whose own stamp file is missing, unreadable or blank (`version.py::StampError`). The panel's stamp is the first line of `lwe_ui/VERSION` (`version.py::panel_stamp`), a link to the repo's `VERSION` that a built wheel carries as a regular file; `version.py::running_refusal` words the matching refusal for a running engine from another build. Any other start runs `app.main()`. Two entry paths, one codebase: `--tray` dispatches to `tray.main` BEFORE any heavy import (`app.py::spawn_tray_if_needed`) - the resident tray is its own QML-free, theme-stripped process (`tray.py::QApplication`, menu = pause/resume, next, stop/restore outputs, open panel, exit) that opens the full window as a child process and re-raises a live one over the window's single-instance socket (`tray.py::open_window`). The bare window process ensures config/state dirs, builds a QApplication, then takes the single-instance guard on `ui.sock` (a second launch asks the running window to present itself and exits 0, `app.py::main`), builds a `QApplication`, registers theme tokens + ten bridge objects (`backend`, `themeBridge`, `importBridge`, `settingsBridge`, `editor`, `bench`, `deckPopup`, `dev`, `workshop`, `wizardBridge`) as QML context properties (`app.py::themeBridge`), loads `qml/Main.qml` (`app.py::main_qml`), and EXITS when the window closes - the window's memory returns to zero while the tray stays. On startup it rewrites the engine env file alone (`app.py::write_env`) and reconciles the autostart entry (autostart launches the tray only, `app.py::reconcileAutostart`, `models.py::reconcileAutostart`). Both processes name themselves via `PR_SET_NAME` (`proctitle.py`, stdlib ctypes) - `lwe-ui` for the window, `lwe-ui-tray` for the tray - because otherwise both report as `python3` and neither can be found by name in a process monitor; the COMMAND column instead follows argv[0], which a pip install gets right from its console script.
- **Where it lives**: fork-only `launch.py`, `cli/` (the command entry and its verb registry; verb files go in `cli/verbs/`; `cli/vocabulary.py` holds the approved command, setting and per-wallpaper rows as data, `cli/vocabulary.py::SETTINGS` among them, and `cli/settings_table.py` takes each setting's scope from them), `version.py` with `VERSION` beside it (a link to the repo's `VERSION`), `app.py`, `tray.py`, `single_instance.py` (QLocalServer on `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/ui.sock`, `single_instance.py::XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`).
- **Surface**: CLI flags `--tray` (`app.py::spawn_tray_if_needed`) and `--lwe <version> <folder> <words>...`, the engine's command handoff (`launch.py::main`); setting `CLOSE_TO_TRAY` default True (`constants.py::CLOSE_TO_TRAY`); XDG autostart file `~/.config/autostart/lwe-ui.desktop` (launches the tray only) written/reconciled by `Backend` (`models.py::XDG_CONFIG_HOME`). With the setting on, the tray is up for as long as the app runs: a window launched with no tray alive spawns one at startup (`app.py::spawn_tray_if_needed`, called at start and at exit), and the tray's Exit ends the window too, over a second verb on the window's socket (`single_instance.py::request_quit`, `tray.py::quit`); an exit the tray asked for spawns no replacement (`app.py::_exit_all_requested`). Off means no tray at all.
- **Coupling**: Self-contained; no upstream contact. Tests: `test_app_load.py`, `test_single_instance.py`, `test_autostart.py`, `test_tray_and_fullscreen.py`, `test_cli_entry.py`, `test_cli_help.py`, `test_cli_version.py`, `test_cli_debug.py`, `test_cli_debug_table.py`.

### Library browser + show-now
- **What it does**: `LibraryModel` rows = union of `tags.csv` ids and `WALLPAPERS_DIR` subdirs, with title/thumb/type from `discovery.project.read`; a sort/filter proxy drives search + favorites (`models.py::LibraryModel`). Commands list the library without Qt, the pool by title and then the Workshop downloads numbered after it, plus the trash list (`catalog.py::wallpaper_rows`, `catalog.py::trash_rows`), and pick wallpapers and playlists by the approved rules (`select.py::wallpapers`, `select.py::playlist`), while the grid keeps its own order. The list, workshop and scan commands print that catalog and write nothing (`library.py::VERBS`). The window's grid takes its membership from the same catalog: models.py re-exports `library_ids` from it (`catalog.py::library_ids`). `showNow(wid)` re-arms a stopped master service, then sends `show` with the fully resolved per-wallpaper args from `resolve_show_args` plus a follow-up `set-tuning`; a rejected or failed show is an honest False (`models.py::showNow`, the show and its `set-tuning` at `push.py::show`, resolver at `resolve.py::resolve_show_args`); the resolver chooses between the wallpaper's own value and the global setting by key presence (`wp.py::load_set`): a value the file sets is chosen, even one equal to the default, and a key the file leaves out takes the global (`SCALING`, `CLAMPING`, `VOLUME`, `SPEED`, `AUDIO_REACTIVE`, `MOUSE`, `AUTOMUTE`); a missing `CC` takes the color grade the wallpaper's own project file authors (`project.py::derive_cc`). A periodic status poll sends `ping` (the engine's dead-man heartbeat) + `status`; on FIRST sight of an engine pid per window process it runs one `sync_all`, which rebuilds the engine from the store (`push.py::sync_all`), and later polls drain the sync marker with 5, 15 and 45 s retries after failed drains of one generation, stopping when the retry after the 45 s wait fails until a writer raises the generation (`models.py::_drain`); while a bridge's debounced delivery is due (`models.py::delivery_due`) the poll does not drain, and the first-sight bundle, the readiness bundle after a service start or restart (`models.py::_ready_tick`), a backup import, `drain_now` and every window change but a trash leave the bundle's re-show out and CURRENT in the marker, so the burst's one re-show serves it and clears that marker (`push.py::_adopt_current`); a poll also drains, with no marker classes, when the engine it read is not the marker's served engine, so a replacement the panel did not start (a restart after a crash, a hand restart) or an engine that arrives after the marker was cleared gets one bundle and is then served (`models.py::_drain`). The grid binds to an order model over that proxy (library_order.py::LibraryOrderModel): playlist members first in stored order, padding cells to the column boundary when a pool follows, then the pool; a single card crossing the blocks is one row move. A filter change, a keystroke or a rail scope or a funnel, is applied by identity: the rows that left are removed and the rows that arrived are inserted as contiguous runs, so the tiles that survive keep their own delegates and never reload their image, and the padding band is re-cut around the new member block (`library_order.py::_apply_membership`). What makes remove-then-insert order-correct is that a filter changes membership only and never order, since members follow the stored order and the pool follows the source order; a source change that does reorder the survivors, or changes their kind, falls back to a reset and is counted (`library_order.py::_reset_fallbacks`). One `invalidateFilter()` emits a row signal per contiguous run, so the order model coalesces them: the runs only mark it dirty, and one update lands at the end of the batch, on the filter model's end-of-batch signal (`models.py::filterInvalidated`) which the filter model also emits after a source edit re-filtered rows dynamically, or on a zero-interval timer as the net for a source that announces nothing, never one update per run (`library_order.py::_flush`). It also holds the provisional order while a card is dragged (library_order.py::dragOver) and the backend stores the drop once and pushes once (models.py::endDrag). The column count is the one whose tiles land nearest a target width, 176 compact and a flagship target that ramps 216 to 260 with the window (Library.qml::targetTile). A column change is a relayout, not a reflow: it re-cuts the padding band, and that band reaches the view as row removals and insertions, so the grid applies it with the displaced, move and remove transitions disabled and the layout forced in the same turn (`Library.qml::setCols`, guard at `Library.qml::recutting`, re-cut at `library_order.py::setColumns`). The resize law: an item view never re-places an item whose transition is running, so a column change must leave nothing animating toward a width the resize has passed. Inside the reflow window of any row edit (a search, a scope, a trash, a membership move; `Motion.removeReflow` plus a margin, timed from the model's row signals at Library.qml::lastRowEdit) the change rebuilds the rows whole (`library_order.py::resetColumns`, counted); at rest it applies the re-cut padding with the transitions off and the layout forced in the same turn (Library.qml::setCols, Library.qml::recutting), keeping every delegate; an in-flight removal's fade is cut short by that, a relayout beating a reflowremoveReflow`, which is what a compositor sends while a neighbouring tile closes, used to leave the rows below the band laid out at a width the resize only passed through, drawn over the rows above them (`test_library_resize.py`). A card's rounded thumbnail is an effect sampling two per-card layer textures, the image and a rounded mask. After a column-change relayout some cards drew nothing while their image reported loaded and the effect reported visible; swapping the mask texture under the effect reproduced that on every card, and re-pointing the effect at its inputs cleared both the induced and the natural blank where redrawing the mask did not, so the card re-attaches the effect to both inputs one turn after its size changes and never toggles their layers (`WallpaperCard.qml::reattach`). The header's type filter offers Scenes, Videos, Web and Untyped so every wallpaper answers to exactly one entry, and the type is read in one lowercase spelling since project files carry both cases (`models.py::LibraryModel`); the header's search field lives inside its glyph until wanted, and a press anywhere in the window drops its cursor through an application-level event filter, since a press a Flickable or MouseArea accepts never reaches a QML listener (`app.py::install_search_focus_filter`). Wallpaper-supplied strings, titles, object names and property keys, are stripped of markup where they enter discovery (`project.py::strip_markup`) and every label that shows one renders plain text, so third-party markup never paints inside the panel. Per-wallpaper conf edits go through a presence-preserving writer that raises on an unreadable file instead of rewriting it from an empty parse (`wp.py::update_set_path`); the accent's ink is picked by measured WCAG contrast rather than a luminance threshold (`themes.py::_wcag_contrast`).
- **Surface**: verbs `show`, `set-tuning`, `ping`, `status`; test-only env `LWE_SANDBOX=1` makes every host-wide probe behind `status()` and `resources()` (the /proc engine scan, the pgrep of the engine family, the unit's MemoryHigh, nvidia-smi) report nothing found, so a sandboxed test reads the machine's live daemon through neither the socket nor the host (`models.py::_sandboxed`, set for every test by `tests/_sandbox.py`).
- **Coupling**: UI-side only; needs the fork engine's `show`-with-args semantics. Tests: `test_api_shownow.py`, `test_engine_sync.py`, `test_filter_model.py`, `test_harness_pin.py`, `test_library_resize.py`.

### Rotation playlists + deck transport
- **What it does**: Named playlists stored as shell-sourceable `playlists/<slug>.conf` (NAME/MODE/INTERVAL/UNIT/MEMBERS, schema at `constants.py::PLAYLIST_SCHEMA`). The active playlist is resolved into complete per-entry show-args in stored member order (`push.py::_playlist_payload`) and pushed by its slug in numbered parts under the engine's caps (`resolve.py::split_playlist_parts`), then the lane is bound to it with `lanes-set`; a refused part binds nothing (`push.py::_bundle`); a change sets the sync marker, saves, and sends its row's push with its own verb last (`push.py::run_change`, `marker.py::writing`), and `sync_all` rebuilds the engine from the store at start, restart, reload and marker drains, leaving speed out while the engine reports 0 (`push.py::sync_all`); every delivery compares the pid of the engine that answers inside sync with the marker's served pid, and an engine it does not name, or any engine when none is recorded, is owed BUNDLE and CURRENT and gets the whole bundle before the delivery's own action (`push.py::_unserved`); a bundle whose every request ended ok records that pid as served without raising the generation (`push.py::_record_served`, `marker.py::record_served`), so a delivery that reached an old engine leaves the new one to the first consumer that sees it; when the last two engines were each replaced within 60 s of being served (the engine's own BOOT_SURVIVED_SECONDS), the next one's owed bundle sends the settings but no re-show and no lanes-set that would start rotation, the engine is recorded as served with that count, and the reason goes to the log and, for a command, to stderr after its output (`push.py::_owe`, `push.py::brake_notes`); an engine replaced more than 60 s after being served resets the count, and an explicit show, playlist switch or next is never braked; a reload's run re-shows the wallpaper on screen whatever the marker holds, and sends that re-show even after the window's budget stops its bundle (`push.py::_bundle`); deck next/prev buttons send `next`/`prev` (`models.py::next_wallpaper`); shuffle and static toggles flank the transport, one mode at a time, writing the playlist's mode and poking a status read at once so the toggle shows the engine's word in the same frame (`models.py::setPlaylistMode`); while status reports the outputs released the deck replaces bar and readouts with a plain stopped label and stops its local tick. Deck animation freeze is `set-speed 0` - deliberately never the engine `pause` verb - and resume restores the resolved rate (`models.py::setAnimationFrozen`); session speed dial is `set-speed` 0..10 (`models.py::setEngineSpeed`). The deck's left block is three lines, Last, the current name and Next, from the engine's lane status (Deck.qml::nameOf); the back glyph follows the engine's `back_enabled` (Deck.qml::backEnabled). The centre progress bar is the bench presence cue's filament on the accent, one shared component with a colour input (GlowBar.qml::progress): a bed at 0.14 alpha, a fill lit to the elapsed portion breathing 0.85 to 1.0 on the motion tokens' inhale and exhale, a bloom behind the lit portion lagged by `Motion.bloomLag`, reaching half as far as the bench bar's (GlowBar.qml::bloomReach) and capped on the light theme by `Motion.bloomCeiling` (Motion.qml::bloomCeiling), and a 60 px shimmer at 0.30 clipped to the fill sweeping at the bench bar's speed (360 px, a 240 px bar plus its overshoot, per `Motion.shimmer`) then resting; paused rests the shimmer and doubles the breath (GlowBar.qml::paused), static is the flat bed (GlowBar.qml::flat), reduced motion is a steady fill with no breath or shimmer. The bench bar is the same component on the warning colour at full length (Deck.qml::deckBenchBar). The right cluster is a four-cell pill, schedule, playlist name, a reserved empty cell and a clock (PlaylistStrip.qml::cellClock); the clock opens a popover holding the mode and the interval with its unit, committing live, where an empty, zero or over-range interval re-reads the stored one (ClockPopover.qml::commitField). Static renders the bar flat with its times hidden (Deck.qml::isStatic). The schedule executes in the engine: the panel resolves `SCHEDULE`/`SCHEDULE_ENABLED` into `schedule-set` entries in stored order (`push.py::_schedule_entries`), pushes every scheduled playlist then the schedule then the lane in every `sync_all` (`push.py::sync_all`), follows the engine's bound playlist into `ACTIVE_PLAYLIST` while the schedule runs (`models.py::_follow_engine_playlist`), and marks the user's own switch `manual` while the engine reports its schedule on, so the engine holds it until the next boundary; with the schedule on and the engine not answering, a switch is refused and nothing is saved (`models.py::setActivePlaylist`). A playlist deleted from the window is decided and deleted under the settings and playlists locks, taken after the status read and held from the read of the active playlist and the schedule through the delete, so a switch or schedule edit made meanwhile waits and the delete's pending record names the switch or schedule drop it makes; a busy settings store refuses the delete with nothing deleted (`models.py::_delete_playlist`). The deck cell shows a sun by day and a moon by night from the day range (row 1 to row 2, `models.py::scheduleIsDay`), grey when off, amber or accent with a tint when on (PlaylistStrip.qml::cellSchedule); the modal's rows carry the same icons (ScheduleModal.qml::entrySun), and its day strip is a range bar: one handle per entry drags that entry's time in quarter-hour steps (ScheduleModal.qml::timeHandle), the labels sit under their own times with midnight at both ends, and the time fields select on a click-and-drag. The modal is the schedule's only editor: the General settings page carries no schedule block (it once duplicated the modal's two rows with a stricter time grammar, so the same entry passed on the deck and failed under Settings). The deck's four override icons (mute, audio reaction, mouse, parallax) save their settings keys through the window's own writer, and a key the window turns on is named in the same write on one settings.conf line whose key name is the one constant `OWNED_KEY` (`storage/settings.py::set_window_override`); every settings.conf write passes one function that decides that line and writes it in the same atomic text as the values, ending the window's ownership of a key the write changes or a command or import names, so the ownership and the values never part on a failed write (`storage/settings.py::_write`); every command write of a setting passes `cli/verbs/settings.py::_write`, which names its key even when the value does not change, and a backup import names the session keys it restores (`storage/settings.py::_backup_apply`); the line is this machine's own record, so an export never carries it and an archive's copy is neither applied nor kept aside with the preserved keys (`storage/settings.py::_backup_preflight`, `storage/settings.py::_backup_export`); and at quit the window returns at once, asking the engine nothing, when it owns nothing under the settings lock, and otherwise reads status, then holds the settings lock from a fresh read of the ownership through the sync marker and one write that turns off what it still owns (`storage/settings.py::clear_window_overrides`, called from `models.py::restoreSessionOverrides`): a window exit never undoes another door's choice.
- **Surface**: verbs `playlist-set`, `lanes-set`, `next`, `prev`, `set-speed`; settings `ROTATION_ENABLED`, `ORDER`, `INTERVAL` 60..7200, `ACTIVE_PLAYLIST` (`constants.py::ROTATION_ENABLED`); schedule keys `SCHEDULE_ENABLED` and `SCHEDULE` (`constants.py::SCHEDULE_ENABLED`), packed `HH:MM=slug;HH:MM=slug` and validated by the bridge (`settings_bridge.py::_validate_schedule`).
- **Coupling**: Self-contained UI + conf files. Tests: `test_playlists.py`, `test_playlist_bridge.py`, `test_playlist_strip_render.py`, `test_deck_states.py`, `test_popup_pointer.py`, `test_library_drop_scroll.py`, `test_refused_folder.py`.

### Per-wallpaper editor (realtime autosave)
- **What it does**: `EditorBridge` (QML `editor`) edits one wallpaper's `wp/<id>.conf` in place - no draft/Save; set-ness is key *presence* (choosing "Global" deletes the key) (`editor.py::EditorBridge`). Live-class keys push engine verbs immediately (SPEED->`set-speed`, VOLUME->`set-volume`, AUDIO_REACTIVE->`set-audio`, MOUSE->`set-mouse`, SKIP->`set-skip`, audio dials->`set-tuning`, FIT_*->`set-fit` with `layer: wallpaper`, all three fields resolved from the conf); the FPS dial here is the global `set-fps` cap, clamped 1..480, not a per-wallpaper key; build-consumed keys (SCALING, CLAMPING, AUTOMUTE, CC, PROP_*) trigger a debounced 600 ms re-show - and only when the edited wallpaper is the one on screen (`editor.py::AUTOMUTE`). The edits of one debounce, here and in the deck, are delivered as one burst with every edit's rows and the last edit's generation, and with the bundle first when the first edit found a marker or another writer raised the generation between two of its edits (`push.py::burst_existed`). Session marks/revert live in `wp_session.SESSION`. Generated Objects and Scene-properties panels come from the discovery indexes. Commands read the same parts and properties from the render folder without writing the editor's caches (`scene.py::tree`, `scene.py::knobs`). `lwe wallpaper <w> hide <id>` and `unhide <id>` write SKIP as the editor's part toggles do (`verbs/objects.py::_skip_edit`); `blink <id>` sends `set-skip` twice and writes nothing (`verbs/objects.py::_blink`). `lwe wallpaper <w>` reads and sets the same keys from the command line: a set writes one line through `wp.py::modify_set` inside the change runner, so a live key sends its verb and a build key re-shows the wallpaper when it is on screen, and the playlists the engine holds that list it are refreshed (`cli/verbs/wallpaper.py::_change`); the four color words seed from the file's CC or the authored look, read from the wallpaper's own folder as the show resolver reads it (a preset's own, not its base's), and write CC with CC_MODE=custom; unsetting one puts only that channel back to its authored value and keeps the other three as they were, removing CC and CC_MODE once all four equal the authored look, and leaves a conf the editor saved as None as it is; alias is saved with no engine side once its rules and the uniqueness scan pass under the overrides lock (`cli/verbs/wallpaper.py::_alias`).
- **Surface**: wp conf schema `WP_SCHEMA` (`constants.py::WP_SCHEMA`), `PROP_` prefix (`constants.py::PROP_`), and `ALIAS`, the wallpaper's alias, which the editor does not show and `Load defaults` keeps (`wp_session.py::IDENTITY_KEYS`; its rules at `alias.py::check`); dial ranges `editor.py::RESPONSE_THRESHOLD`. Fit rows in the Tuning cell (Zoom 1.00-2.00 left-anchored, Pan X and Pan Y centered at 0 with the chip reporting the offset in percent of the picture the scaling mode shows) read and write `FIT_ZOOM`/`FIT_PAN_X`/`FIT_PAN_Y` through `fitValue`/`setFit` (`editor.py::setFit`, field names `constants.py::FIT_FIELDS`); an empty entry deletes the key. The keys are live class: a commit pushes the wallpaper layer through `set-fit` when the edited wallpaper is on screen (no rebuild), and every show still carries the resolved fit (`resolve.py::resolve_fit`).
- **Coupling**: Self-contained; reads `project.json` via pure-Python discovery. Tests: `test_editor.py`, `test_editor_draft.py`, `test_editor_panels.py`, `test_editor_ui.py`, `test_draft.py`, `test_audio_dial_persistence.py`, `test_preset_identity.py`.

### Deck settings popup
- **What it does**: `DeckPopupBridge` (QML `deckPopup`) tracks whatever the engine is currently showing and offers global capsule controls (Speed/Volume/FPS saved first, then the change runner's resolved push) plus this-wallpaper Scaling and scene PROP_ properties (conf write + debounced re-show) and the same three fit rows the editor shows (`deck_popup.py::setFit`, one stored value set, pushed live through `set-fit`'s wallpaper layer by the change runner at `deck_popup.py::_commit_conf`, never a re-show) (`deck_popup.py::DeckPopupBridge`; the global doors save at `deck_popup.py::_save_global`). Marks, the revert snapshot and Load defaults go through the shared `wp_session.SESSION`, the same store the editor uses, so a seat that cannot read the conf is reported on sync and Revert refuses instead of deleting overrides (`deck_popup.py::revertChanges`). The quick panel, the clock popover and the playlist name menu are non-modal, and each takes the exclusive pointer grab on press both over its content and over the padding ring around it, the background outside the content that no control covers, so a drag that starts anywhere inside a popover never lifts a library tile beneath it (`DeckSettingsPopup.qml::ReleaseWithinBounds`, `ClockPopover.qml`, `PlaylistStrip.qml`); the content handler alone left the ring open, and `test_popup_pointer.py` presses both.
- **Coupling/Tests**: Self-contained apart from `wp_session`; `test_ab_gestures.py`, `test_combo_close.py`, `test_deck_popup_session.py` and deck tests cover it.

### Settings surface
- **What it does**: `SettingsBridge` (QML `settingsBridge`) is the store access for the Settings pages: schema-driven validation before write, then the save first and the key's row push resolved from the store (`push.py::run_change`), and a derived "reach" receipt per key (`settings_bridge.py`).
