Key classes declared in constants (`constants.py::REACH_NEXT_IMPORT`): live (the row's verb resolved for the wallpaper on screen, `push.py::SETTING_ROWS`), next-show, panel (CLOSE_TO_TRAY, STEAM_DIR), service-restart (the save rewrites engine-env only, never the unit (`daemon_unit.py::write_env`), and the bridge forgets its cached restart answers at `settings_bridge.py::_regenerate`; the restart itself is the user's: the header's master toggle, or the restart verb each restart-class Engine row shows (Wayland layer, Video decode, Texture detail, Resolution clamp, Effect clamp, Texture compression; `RestartVerb.qml`) while the running engine reports another value than it would read from the file on that row's own keys: the value its status config gives each knob against the file's line read by the engine's own rules, floats compared at float32, and only from the engine the service started, whose status pid is the unit's MainPID (`daemon_unit.py::restart_state`, `daemon_unit.py::_service_main_pid`); a knob given as a launch flag never lights the verb, since a restart applies the flag again, and the layer, which status does not report, is compared with the running engine's process environment as one token of the argument line, so a monitor change does not light it; one tap queues a restart of the unit and the bridge, which outlives any page, holds every verb until the engine is back up on the file or 20 s pass; `ASSETS_DIR` on Library is restart-class too and carries no verb (`daemon_unit.py::RESTART_ENV_KEYS`, `daemon_unit.py::restart_pending_keys`, `settings_bridge.py::takeRestart`, `models.py::restartMaster`)), boundary, next-scan, re-arm, next-import. Also owns autostart slot (`settings_bridge.py::autostart`) and saves the exceptions list through the change runner, whose verb row sends `set-fullscreen-ignore` (`settings_bridge.py::_write_exceptions`). The pages share one shell law (SettingsView.qml::colMax): in flagship the content column is 720 wide, centered between the rail and the window edge at every width, filling with 16 px side padding below 752; compact keeps the 640 column with 28 px padding. Section headers on General, Engine and Library are the settings tier (PSection.qml): 13 px labels in the text color at medium weight over a 0.08 hairline, 26 above and 6 below, the first sitting 14 under the tab strip; the 11 px rule label (PRule.qml) stays the instrument tier for the developer panel, gear popup, editor cells and deck.
- **Surface**: full `SETTINGS_SCHEMA` with types/defaults/ranges at `constants.py::SETTINGS_SCHEMA`. The resolution cap is two saved numbers, `SSFACTOR` for the scene and `CLAMPCOMPOSITES` for the effect buffers (service-restart class, 0 to 4 with decimals, a typed number at or below 0 saving 0, which is no cap, default 1), each read from the settings file's `RENDER_RESOLUTION` word while the file lacks it (`constants.py::resolution_word_numbers`: `screen` 1 and 1, `sharpfx` 1 and 0, `wallpaper` 0 and 0); a 1 writes no line and any other number writes `LWE_SSFACTOR` or `LWE_CLAMPCOMPOSITES`, and `resolve_show_args` sends each number a wallpaper sets, its own or its word's, as `ssfactor` or `clampcomposites` (`wp.py::clamp_values`); Engine > Advanced shows them as the `Resolution clamp` and `Effect clamp` rows, each a compact box offering 0, 1 and 2 and taking typed decimals, with its own restart verb (`daemon_unit.py::RESTART_ENV_KEYS`). Per wallpaper, the editor's `Quality and memory` rule (Tuning, above Metadata) stores its four rows sparsely in wp/<id>.conf (`constants.py::TEXCOMP`): `Resolution clamp` and `Effect clamp` save `SSFACTOR` and `CLAMPCOMPOSITES` as numbers picked from 0, 1 and 2 or typed as decimals (0 or below saves 0, above 4 or not a number is refused) and show the file's own number or the one its `RENDER_RESOLUTION` word stands for, and `Texture compression` and `Texture detail` save `TEXCOMP` and `TEXTURE_DETAIL`; each menu offers `Global (<value>)`, the settings file's value, which the engine reads at service start (`editor.py::globalDefaultFor`), and choosing it deletes the key, or for a clamp writes the `KEY=` marker where the file carries the word (`editor.py::setClampValue`); `resolve_show_args` sends only a set value, the clamps as `ssfactor` and `clampcomposites` (a word alone stands for `screen` 1 and 1, `sharpfx` 1 and 0, `wallpaper` 0 and 0) and the other two as `texcomp` / `texdetail` (`resolve.py::texdetail`), so an absent key inherits the engine's launch value (the flag when given, else the environment), and a change on the playing wallpaper re-shows it (`editor.py::_reshow`). `Interface scale` on General > App (`constants.py::INTERFACE_SCALE`, panel class, 75..150 percent, default 100) is a slider with detents at 75, 100 and 150 under a monitor glyph each (1080p, 1440p, 2160p); a release within three points of a detent settles on it (`settings_bridge.py::settleScale`), the chip reads the knob while held and the store otherwise. It applies after relaunch: the app bootstrap sets Qt's startup scale factor from the stored percent before the first window (`app.py::interface_scale_factor`), a hand-set `QT_SCALE_FACTOR` winning, so every logical-pixel law (deck clamps, tile floor, compact breakpoint, column cap) holds unchanged and hairlines snap to whole device pixels. The volume chips (Engine > Audio, the editor's two Volume rows, the deck popup) read the knob while held and the store otherwise, so a stored volume above 100 shows its number while the 0 to 100 slider sits at its end (`SettingsEngine.qml::settingsVolumeChip`).
- **Tests**: `test_settings_bridge.py`, `test_settings_ui.py`, `test_engine_sync.py`, `test_daemon_unit.py`.

### Configuration backup
- **What it does**: Export writes one `.lwebackup` file, a zip with a manifest (`storage/backup.py::FORMAT`), holding what the user authored and the app cannot regenerate: settings, theme, discovery, playlists, per-wallpaper overrides (a folder inside the library this machine's settings name travels as the bare id, a referenced folder anywhere else travels as it is, `wp.py::_portable_bg`), tags, favorites and the app rule files; never the engine env file, caches, state or logs. What travels is not a list the backup module keeps: each store declares a record naming the files it owns under the config dir and how it exports, plans and writes them (`store.py::Store`), and `storage/registry.py::STORES` is the one order the backup walks, settings first because every later store's plan is decided against the settings the import would leave. `config/foreign.json` holds the keys earlier imports kept aside and is no store and no member: its content travels inside the members it came from, which `storage/registry.py::CARRIED_INSIDE` says of it for the ownership law. Every other file under the config dir is named in `storage/registry.py::NOT_BACKED_UP` with the reason it stays behind, and `test_store_ownership.py` runs the writers in a sandbox and fails on a file neither table claims, on a store whose declared paths match nothing, and when any single store is dropped from the registry. That guard is what the hand list did not have: the theme member is the LIVE store, its active theme and its overlays (`themes.py::CONFIG_KEYS`, `themes.py::load_config`; the retired `theme_cfg.py` reader is no longer on the backup path), and `discover.json` travels with the API key included, since acquisition settings are user-authored and machine-independent (`discover_cfg.py::BACKUP`). Import never extracts a file: `preflight` (`storage/backup.py::preflight`) decides the whole plan before a write. Settings go through the same tables, coercion and validation a normal load applies (`storage/settings.py::migrate_raw`), playlists through their validator, overrides key by key against the override schema with their set-ness kept (an override travels as the keys its file carries and is written back as those keys, so an absent key keeps inheriting the global, `wp.py::_backup_text`), theme and discovery keys against the keys their store persists, tag rows against the tag states; the rule files are line lists with no schema and travel as lines, and a line the file cannot hold (not printable) is named in the receipt rather than vanishing (`rules.py::FILES`). Renames and retirements are DATA, not code: `constants.py::RENAMES` gives a store's old key names, its new ones and the function that carries the value across a meaning change, `constants.py::VALUE_ALIASES` the stored values a narrowed choice set maps onto, `constants.py::RETIRED` the keys that are gone and why, and one function applies a store's tables to a raw mapping (`migrate.py::apply_tables`) at BOTH doors, the normal load and the import, so a stored file and an archive member are read the same way; the five key-shaped stores (settings, theme, discovery, playlists, overrides) call it in their loaders, and a validator applies the alias table too so a legacy word given to a writer lands as this build's. Dropping a key is legal only where RETIRED names it. Everything else this build does not know is PRESERVED rather than dropped: the key and its raw value go to `config/foreign.json` under the store, the member it came from and its key (`foreign.py::record`), the receipt names each one, and the next export re-emits it into that same member minus anything RETIRED names (`foreign.py::extras`), so a key a newer build writes under a name this build lacks survives a round trip through an older one. At every panel start a kept-aside key this build now knows is written into its member through the store's own writer and removed from the file, and a scope whose playlist or override is gone is pruned (`foreign.py::promote`), so a kept-aside value is never re-emitted over one the user has since set or cleared; on export a store never lets a kept-aside key overwrite one its member already carries. A stored key whose new name the same file also carries is retired with that reason, the one drop RETIRED does not have to name. Every rename, alias, clamp and snap reaches the receipt through one function (`migrate.py::report`). The one downgrade loss is where a LIVE file is still written whole from its schema: a normal settings save changes only its own line and keeps unknown keys, comments and every other line as they were (`storage/settings.py::modify`, `tier_a.py::edit`), but reset and restore rewrite settings.conf whole (`storage/settings.py::replace`), as does the first write (`storage/settings.py::save`, reached through `storage/settings.py::ensure_exists` and the missing-file branch of `storage/settings.py::modify`), and theme.json is rewritten whole on every save, so a key a newer build wrote into this machine's own settings.conf is gone at the first reset or restore after a rollback, one in theme.json at the first theme save, and the pre-restore snapshot, being an export, does not hold it either. A value this build cannot hold is adjusted, never silently lost: a number outside the range is clamped and both numbers reach the receipt, a choice this build lacks is preserved in the sparse override store and snapped to the default with both values named in the dense stores (settings, playlists, the theme's active key, which goes through the same check the load door applies, `themes.py::active_keys`), text that is not a number at all is dropped with that reason in the sparse override store and snapped to the default and named in the dense stores; one function decides all of that for the override and playlist members from the spec and whether the file is dense (`migrate.py::coerce`), and the settings loader's own validator does the same for settings with its warning channel (`storage/settings.py::_coerce`), a tag state this build lacks snaps to review and is named, and the settings validator reports every clamp and snap to the importer through the same code path that warns on a normal load (`storage/settings.py::_validate`). The manifest carries each store's schema (types, defaults, ranges, choices) and this build's machine-key list for a LATER build to read (`storage/backup.py::schemas`, passed through JSON so a reader gets what the writer meant); nothing in this build reads either, and deliberately not the defaults: settings.conf is written dense, so a value equal to the archive's default cannot be told from a choice, and a rule that treated it as unset would discard what the user chose. The path keys re-resolved are this build's own machine keys, never a list read from the archive. Overrides and playlist members whose wallpaper is not in the library are held (written anyway, so a migration seeds the overrides before the wallpapers are re-imported), and user meta follows the same rule; a good or review tag row for an absent wallpaper waits instead, since a row would put a card with no folder in the grid, but a BAD row restores unconditionally, because a trashed verdict that waits lets the importer re-import what the user threw away (`tags.py::_backup_preflight`). Only what a person authored travels in meta (`meta.py::USER_META`); what a probe or the importer derived about this machine's copy stays behind and is dropped by name if an older archive carries it (`meta.py::MACHINE_META`). Machine paths that do not exist here are re-resolved (current values kept, an override's folder becomes its id), the follow-ups a changed setting implies are named (relaunch for the interface scale, engine restart for restart-class keys, rescan for a library change), and a last referential pass checks that the planned SCHEDULE and ACTIVE_PLAYLIST name a playlist the import leaves behind, dropping a dangling schedule entry, switching the schedule off as the panel does when it loses an entry, and naming all of it in the receipt (`storage/backup.py::_referential`). `apply` (`storage/backup.py::apply`) refuses a second restore through a lock under the state dir, which each door holds from apply through its later steps and its settle (`storage/backup.py::restoring`), so an import started while another is in its later steps is refused; a `state/backups/recovery.json` whose snapshot is missing, or that cannot be read, then refuses the import with nothing written and names the file whose deletion clears the block. Then it takes the lock of every store it writes in rank order and sets the sync marker to BUNDLE and CURRENT with a raised generation (`marker.py::writing`); a busy store lock or a marker that cannot be set refuses the import with nothing written, the snapshot included, so refused imports never rotate one out. Then, unless recovery.json names a snapshot, it exports the configuration as this build reads it to `state/backups/pre-restore-<ts>.lwebackup` before it writes any store (an export, so values pass the same validation as any export and a hand-set value outside its range is clamped there too), keeps the newest five by write time and the one recovery.json names, and abandons the whole import with the file named if any store cannot be read for that snapshot, the marker it set then costing one extra bundle (`storage/backup.py::snapshot`); then it writes the plan through the stores while holding the marker, releasing it before the store locks, so no drain can clear that intent before the stores hold the plan and a restore that dies before its push still leaves the engine side owed; a write that fails before anything was written refuses the receipt, so the row shows the error; after earlier stores wrote, the row reads Restore incomplete with the failure count and the first file, never a count of what was only planned, and every error is in the panel log. Each door ends its import with `storage/backup.py::settle`: an import that failed after taking its snapshot names that snapshot in recovery.json, so later imports take no new snapshot and the way back is kept, and any successful import removes recovery.json. A member one store cannot read at preflight holds that store with the reason and the others restore. The export itself is written beside its target and renamed into place. Nothing is refused whole any more except a file that is not an LWE backup: an archive whose format is newer than this build's is restored best effort with a note, and a backup with no settings member leaves the current settings to decide the rest of the plan. The importer keeps a pre-seeded override's user keys at the wallpaper's first arrival and sets only the folder and the type (`importer.py::_write_conf`); a preset wired through its base is rewritten whole. The bridge exposes the Save and Open dialog accepts (`settings_bridge.py::exportBackup`, `settings_bridge.py::importBackup`), logs every receipt item to the panel log, and shows the import receipt as the Configuration row's caption (`storage/backup.py::receipt_line`: the counts, what waits, what was dropped, adjusted or kept aside, and whether the backup had no settings or came from a newer version); export succeeds silently, per the surface's law. The command `lwe backup export|preview|import <file>` wraps the same `export_to`, `preflight` and `apply` (`cli/verbs/backup.py::run`, never the module's own `main`); an import then delivers the store like a reload, one sync with a re-show of the wallpaper on screen (`push.py::sync_all`), rebuilds engine-env from the store under the no-screens rule (`daemon_unit.py::write_env`) and names the restart-class settings the running engine still runs on other values (`daemon_unit.py::restart_state`); the window is not told.
- **Surface**: `.lwebackup` (zip, one member per store: `settings.conf`, `theme.json`, `discover.json`, `playlists/<slug>.conf`, `wp/<id>.conf`, `tags.csv`, `meta.json`, `rules/<name>.txt`, plus `manifest.json` with `format`, `app`, `created`, `host`, `counts` one key per store that counts (settings counts nothing; theme reports `theme` 1 when it traveled and `overlays` for the custom palettes), `machine_keys` from `storage/settings.py::MACHINE_KEYS`, and `schemas`, one per store, holding what that store's keys are, their types, defaults, ranges and choices (`storage/backup.py::schemas`); an archive with no `schemas` restores exactly as it did before, which is why the format is unchanged); the receipt object `{kind, path, counts, dropped, held, reresolved, adjusted, preserved, notes, followups, errors}`, where `adjusted` carries `{kind: clamp|snap|alias|rename, store, id, key, from, to}`, `preserved` `{kind, store, id, key}` and `notes` the snapshot, a newer format, a missing settings member and a dangling playlist slug (`settings_bridge.py::receipt`); the command line `python3 -m lwe_ui.storage.backup export|restore|preview <file>` (`storage/backup.py::main`) prints the receipt line, the counts and every receipt list as plain text and exits 1 on a refusal or a write failure; preview is the whole plan with nothing written and no snapshot taken, and imports no Qt, which is why the restart-class key tuple lives in constants rather than the Settings bridge (`constants.py::REACH_SERVICE_RESTART`), so a configuration can be restored the night the panel will not start.
- **Tests**: `test_backup.py`, `test_store_ownership.py`, `test_settings_bridge.py`, `test_backup_cli.py`, `test_backup_corpus.py`, `test_cli_backup.py`. The corpus: `tests/fixtures/backups/` holds archives built from the live schemas by `tests/corpus/make_corpus.py` (`make_corpus.py::build_variant`, run as `PYTHONPATH=src python3 tests/corpus/make_corpus.py`), each beside the JSON of the values that were written, which is the only expected file the corpus has and is produced by the generator, never by hand; a settings key holds one value per file, so one run writes a variant per pass over the ring of legal values (`make_corpus.py::ring`). `test_backup_corpus.py` restores every archive into a fresh sandbox whose library holds only some of its wallpapers, so the held paths are exercised, and asserts: no receipt error, no Python warning, no dropped entry that `constants.py::RETIRED` does not name, nothing adjusted, nothing preserved, no note but the pre-restore snapshot, every written value back verbatim or under its new name through `constants.py::RENAMES`, every override's set-ness kept key for key, and the newest generation carrying every key the current schemas define, so a key added without regenerating the corpus fails with the key's name. `test_backup.py::doors` is the other half: it exports a whole machine through the live stores, then edits the zip in memory into what an older or a newer build would have written (a minutes-era detect period, a vendor decoder token, a retired theme key, an override above its maximum with a key this build has never heard of, a manifest whose schema disagrees and whose format is one ahead, a backup with no settings at all) and asserts the restored STATE, not the receipt's shape. `test_backup_corpus.py::EXPECTED` names the losses this build still has, each with its reason; a case that starts passing fails the suite until its entry is deleted with the fix. Beside each plain archive the generator writes a faulted sibling (`make_corpus.py::fault_variant`): the same export as an older, careless build would have written it, with the record naming exactly what the restore must adjust and keep aside, so the adjusted and preserved gates have a non-empty expectation (`test_backup_corpus.py::_check_faults`). A schema change regenerates the corpus under a new hash and leaves the older archives in place: they must keep restoring. Archives without the clamp keys `SSFACTOR` and `CLAMPCOMPOSITES` restore through the old word: the settings preflight maps the archive's `RENDER_RESOLUTION` for each number it lacks (`storage/settings.py::_backup_preflight`).

### State tree and logs
- **What it does**: the state dir (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/lwe`) is a tree, not a drawer (`paths.py::LOG_SUBSYSTEMS`): `logs/<subsystem>/` holds one real-time log per subsystem, `panel/` the panel's own state (objindex, propindex, records, draft, dev-slots.json, the restart lock), `engine/` and `probes/` are reserved for the engine's state files and instrument dumps. `migrate_state_tree` (`paths.py::migrate_state_tree`) moves the panel's files from the flat layout once, idempotently, never touches an engine file, names a source it left beside a non-empty destination as skipped, and names the top-level files with no writer left (`paths.py::DEAD_STATE_FILES`) without deleting them; both panel processes run it at start (`app.py::_settle_state_tree`). Logs are written as they happen, not on request: `logs/panel/window.log` and `logs/panel/tray.log` take Python logging and every Qt message of their process, tagged and size-rotated, one file per process because a size rotation shared by two writers loses lines (`logbook.py::install`); `logs/developer/<side>.log` is truncated at every launch of a Developer slot and appended as its lines are drained (`dev.py::_slot_log_write`), kept after the exhibit exits; `logs/bench/bench.log` is the wizard bench's log (`wizard_bridge.py::bench_log_file`) and `logs/bench/command.log` the bench command's, written afresh at each run (`paths.py::command_bench_log_file`). The Settings `Open logs` verb opens `logs/` (`settings_bridge.py::openLogs`). The General page's System block is the memory caps readout, Logs and Configuration; the socket health light that read as an engine mode is gone, because the header's master toggle carries that state: an active unit whose socket stays silent past a 20 s start window turns the track red with "Engine running but not answering" (HeaderBar.qml::startWindowOver), the same red a crashed unit gets. The engine side resolves the same tree from one header (Logging/StatePaths.h::root): `engine/` holds engine-state.json, boot-history.json and the texcache; `logs/engine/engine.log` is the daemon's own log, every line stamped and flushed as it ends by a file sink beside stdout (main.cpp::attachFileLog), rotated at boot with five kept (StatePaths.h::rotate), and attached only in daemon mode so a windowed exhibit never rotates it; `logs/cef/cef.log` is CEF's file, rotated by the same helper; `probes/` takes the shader dumps and the pass probe image; `migrateEngineFiles` (StatePaths.h::migrateEngineFiles) moves a flat layout into place at every boot, idempotently, overwriting nothing.
- **Surface**: `paths.py::logs_dir`, `paths.py::log_dir`, `paths.py::panel_state_dir`, `paths.py::dev_slot_log_file`.
- **Tests**: `test_state_tree.py`.

### Engine daemon systemd unit management
- **What it does**: `engine/daemon_unit.py` generates `~/.config/systemd/user/lwe-engine.service` (Type=simple, `ExecStart="<bin>" --daemon $LWE_ENGINE_ARGS`, Restart=always, MemoryHigh=2G/MemoryMax=3G/MemorySwapMax=2G, WantedBy=graphical-session.target) and the env file `~/.config/lwe/engine-env` (`daemon_unit.py::LWE_ENGINE_ARGS`). One generator writes engine-env: `write_env` rewrites that file alone, under the env lock and only when its text changed, and never writes the unit or runs systemctl; its `lines` argument sets or removes a line the generator does not own; when no screen is found (no output enumerates) it leaves the file exactly as it was, named lines included, and returns "no screens" (`daemon_unit.py::write_env`). `write_files` is the service operations' rebuild: engine-env through `write_env` under the same rule, then the unit only when its text changed, then `systemctl --user daemon-reload`, whose failure fails it with that command named as the fix (`daemon_unit.py::write_files`). Launch shape (assets dir, `--screen-root` per detected monitor, `--layer`, hwdec, texcomp, audio dials) is resolved from settings into the env file, with the dead-man interval kept: a valid existing `LWE_DEADMAN` (whole seconds 0-86400, the check Config applies) is written again, else 300; monitor enumeration tries hyprctl -> kscreen-doctor -> wlr-randr (`daemon_unit.py::_outputs_kscreen`). Hand-edited env lines are preserved across regenerates only when their key carries the engine's `LWE_` prefix or NVIDIA's `__NV_` driver prefix (`daemon_unit.py::_FOREIGN_PREFIXES`); anything else is dropped as drift; a panel start rewrites engine-env only, through `write_env`, without touching the running engine (`daemon_unit.py::write_env`). Enable/disable is the master switch's job: `Backend.setMaster` runs `systemctl --user enable/disable --now` on `lwe-engine.service`; the switch's start and the restart row first rebuild engine-env, write the unit and run daemon-reload, and a failure there runs no systemctl and shows its notice; a start that launches the engine, and every restart, record BUNDLE in the sync marker before the launch and launch nothing when it cannot be written, busy lock included (`models.py::_record_bundle`); a start then bundles once the engine answers (`models.py::setMaster`), and a restart runs on a worker thread that holds the sync lock from the record through the queued restart, the wait for the new engine and its sync (`models.py::_restart_worker`): it reads the service's MainPID before queueing the restart, the new engine is the one whose status pid is a new, non-zero MainPID (`push.py::wait_ready`), and when that 20 s wait ends while systemd reports the unit deactivating or activating, the worker keeps the lock until the unit settles, at most 120 s after the restart was queued (`models.py::_RESTART_CAP_S`). The window waits only for the restart to be queued, and the worker's hold is a long hold: meanwhile no other sync attempt in the window waits for it, one already waiting included, so a delivery, the poll drain included, ends pending and an engine-only action or a second restart is refused as busy, at once (`push.py::restart_hold`, `lock.py::held`). The hold and the MainPID wait are timing aids: when either lets the old engine take the bundle, the new engine is not the served one and the next poll bundles it (`models.py::_drain`).
- **Surface**: managed env keys `LWE_ENGINE_ARGS, LWE_HWDEC, LWE_TEXCOMP, LWE_DEADMAN, LWE_NOPAUSEVRAM, LWE_TEXDETAIL, LWE_SSFACTOR, LWE_CLAMPCOMPOSITES, LWE_AUDIOGAIN, LWE_CLASSICK, LWE_CLASSICEXP` (`daemon_unit.py::LWE_ENGINE_ARGS`; `LWE_NOPAUSEVRAM` is owned but never emitted; regeneration drops stale lines); `LWE_DEADMAN` keeps a valid existing value, else 300 (`daemon_unit.py::_deadman_ok`); `LWE_TEXCAP`, the engine's testtexturelimit switch, is not managed and is carried like any hand-added line; engine binary lookup ENGINE_BIN -> PATH -> `~/.local/bin` -> the installer's engine home under `~/.local/lib` (`daemon_unit.py::ENGINE_BIN`). Unit name `constants.py::ENGINE_SERVICE`.
- **Coupling**: Pure file generation + systemctl; lifts alone but is meaningless without the fork engine's `--daemon` mode. Tests: `test_daemon_unit.py`.

### Bench (test-render an item before approving it)
- **What it does**: The bench launches a *separate* non-detached test engine process rendering the item's own conf through the same argv builder live launches use (`bench.py`, `engine/invocation.py`), while the desktop engine is stood down over the socket: `release-outputs` frees the surfaces + scene VRAM with the daemon still serving, `acquire-outputs` hands them back (`bench_courier.py::standdown`). A release is STATE - it holds until acquire, with the engine's own dead-man reflex as the abnormal-exit backstop - so there is no lease and nothing renews. `BenchBridge` owns the QProcess lifecycle and routes approve/reject through `commit.py` (`bench_bridge.py::BenchBridge`). Test engines are reaped before commit/reject; commit off the GUI thread.
- **Coupling**: UI + `commit.py` gate; depends on fork's `release/acquire-outputs` verbs. Tests: `test_bench.py`, `test_bench_bridge.py`, `test_bench_standdown.py`, `test_bench_fixes.py`, `test_commit.py`, `test_commit_packed_scene.py`.

### Import wizard (human-verdict approval of new items)
- **What it does**: `WizardBridge` (QML `wizardBridge`) runs a phase machine for each pending Workshop item: static census -> launch a *windowed* test engine in the top-left cell of the focused output while the desktop engine keeps running (`wizard_bridge.py::_launch_bench`, placed by pid through `placement.py::place`, the same cell the Developer view's slot A uses) -> watch for `LWE-PRESENT`/fatal lines via a `BenchSession` -> human clicks pass/fixable/fail; terminal actions write records events and graduate or trash the item (`wizard_bridge.py::wizardBridge`). Crash-vs-close keys off "did it present a frame", not exit signal. The bench engine listens on its own socket (`wizard_bridge.py::bench_sock_path`); once a second the bridge samples it and pushes the item's title, its stats block and a countdown through `set-overlay` (`wizard_bridge.py::bench_overlay_text`): the 15 s run window after the first frame, the 30 s load window before it. One argv builder serves the wizard's bench and the bench command, which runs without a socket (`benchrun.py::bench_argv`). Uses `texcomp` scanning/encoding. The encoder can report the bytes it read and wrote (`texcomp.py::encode_scene`), and the compress command is the command door to it (`compress.py::compress_one`).
- **Tests**: `test_wizard.py`, `test_wizard_bridge.py`, `test_bench_verdict.py`.

### Workshop scope + import pipeline
- **What it does**: `WorkshopBridge` (QML `workshop`) handles steam:// handler detection, Workshop deep links, pending-item tiles, and the trash chain (deletion record + copy-mode delete + unsubscribe link) (`workshop.py`). Approving a wallpaper into the pool and lifting a trash block live in one place, shared by the window's approve, the trash manager's import and the add and untrash commands (`actions.py::approve`, `actions.py::untrash`). The trash chain lives there too (`actions.py::trash`): one change takes the wallpaper out of every playlist that holds it, from the window and the command alike, and sends every engine-held playlist that changed. It and the two membership changes decide the playlists they alter under the playlists store lock, held through the write, and set the sync marker for those playlists before any of them changes (`actions.py::_members_change`). The mechanical import (`storage/importer.py`) scans `WORKSHOP_DIR` (native or Flatpak Steam, auto-detected `paths.py::XDG_DATA_HOME`), type-classifies, derives color correction, dedups; `REVIEW_REQUIRED` decides review-vs-good landing tag, `STORAGE_POLICY` copy-vs-reference (`importer.py::STORAGE_POLICY`). An override is sparse by law: at a wallpaper's first arrival the importer writes its identity and the facts it declares (a colour grade that is not identity, audio support when declared, preset properties) and never a default (`wp.py::facts_to_keys`, `importer.py::_write_conf`); a present key is a pin and an absent key inherits the global, so the editor's `Global` entry deletes the key. A conf that already exists at arrival (restored from a backup) keeps every key the user set and gains only the facts the user has not set; a preset wired through its base is rewritten whole, sparsely. The panel's first start on this build strips every materialised default from the existing files once, after a snapshot to `state/backups/pre-sparsify-<ts>.lwebackup`, logging each removal (`wp.py::sparsify_overrides`, `app.py::_sparsify_overrides_once`). Hand-added folders go to `~/.local/share/lwe/manual` as a second pending source (`paths.py::pending_root_for`). Steam Web API browse constants exist (`constants.py::STEAM_QUERYFILES_URL`).
- **Surface**: settings `WORKSHOP_DIR`, `STEAM_DIR`, `REVIEW_REQUIRED`, `STORAGE_POLICY`, `DETECT_MODE`/`DETECT_INTERVAL_SEC` (`constants.py::DETECT_MODE`); `discover.json` holds apiKey/acquireMethod defaults (`constants.py::DISCOVER_DEFAULTS`).
- **Tests**: `test_importer.py`, `test_discovery.py`, `test_workshop_*.py`, `test_tombstone_manager.py`.

### Commit gate + records
- **What it does**: `commit.commit` is the single lifecycle-advance function: first-commit copies `{project.json, payload, preview.*}` (never `shaders/`) atomically into the library, rewrites conf BG, tags good, builds indexes; re-commit is a no-op promote; `reject` tags bad (`commit.py::reject`). Per-wallpaper lifecycle events live in append-only `state/records/<wid>.jsonl` (`paths.py::records_dir`), migrated from a legacy tombstones.json (`workshop.py::__init__`). The add, untrash and trash commands write `approved`, `bypassed` and `deleted` events through the same append (`records.py::append`).
- **Tests**: `test_commit.py`, `test_records.py`, `test_records_view.py`, `test_journal_and_toggles.py`.

### Fullscreen + running-apps policy (engine-owned; the panel only pushes it)
- **What it does**: Both display policies live IN the engine now - see the engine
  chapters ("Fullscreen policy" and "Running-apps condition (engine-side)"). The
  panel's whole role is configuration: it pushes `set-fullscreen` /
  `set-fullscreen-ignore` / `set-app-conditions` on settings commits, and the
  engine detects, acts, and persists on its own. The old UI-side watcher
  processes are gone.
- **Surface**: settings `FULLSCREEN_BEHAVIOR` (off/pause/stop), `APP_CONDITION_BEHAVIOR` (`constants.py`); config file `app-condition.txt` holds process comm names for the app-condition policy, and `pause-blacklist.txt` holds fullscreen-ignore app_ids, mtime-watched and pushed via `set-fullscreen-ignore` (`push.py::_fullscreen_ignore_ids`).
- **Tests**: `test_tray_and_fullscreen.py`.

### Developer view (advanced scene control)
- **What it does**: `DevBridge` (QML `dev`, `DeveloperView.qml`) is one embedded view with two persistent exhibit slots (A and B) and three launch verbs. Every verb runs windowed while the daemon keeps the desktop: Launch A / Launch B open that slot alone in the top-left / top-right quadrant of the focused output (`dev.py::launch`; the quadrants are laid out like a two by two tiling, so the compositor's reserved edges and outer gap stay clear and a bar keeps its room, `placement.py::quadrant`, `placement.py::layout`, shared with the Workshop bench), Launch both opens A then B (`dev.py::launchBoth`), each window placed by pid once it maps (`dev.py::_place_tick`). Each verb is a toggle: it reads Stop while its exhibit runs and stops that side alone (`dev.py::stopSide`), Launch both while both run stops both (`dev.py::stop`). One argv builder serves all three (`dev.py::compose_argv`). Every knob is per slot: scene (library or `~/.local/share/lwe/probes/`; the scene popup's rows and its background take the exclusive pointer grab on press, since a passive grab travels through the popup to the Binary field it covers, `DevSlotCard.qml::ReleaseWithinBounds`), binary (the daemon's own, anything in the dev-binaries dir, or a browsed path; a build whose help lacks `--api-socket` launches on the old command line with live controls disabled, `dev.py::_binary_is_api`), overlay label (`LWE_OVERLAY_TEXT`), the stats overlay switch and its corner (`dev.py::setOverlayStats`, `dev.py::setOverlayCorner`: once a second a worker thread samples the exhibit's pid and socket for CPU as a share of the whole machine, whole-GPU load, resident plus swapped memory, VRAM and FPS, `dev.py::sample_exhibit`, and pushes them through `set-overlay` as a heading line and one figure per line (`dev.py::overlay_text`); the switch and corner apply live, a legacy build gets neither), the vetted feature toggles (`dev.py::FEATURE_TOGGLES`; the two resolution-cap rows are one choice per side, `Resolution clamp` and `Resolution clamp + effects`, each switching the other off through the table's `excludes` entry at `dev.py::setToggle`, and both off leaves nothing clamped), trail mode, render-debug flags (`dev.py::RENDER_DEBUG_FLAGS`), env-class instruments (`dev.py::INSTRUMENTS`), raw env lines and the `--set-property` queue behind the Raw env door, plus a reference of value-class knobs (`dev.py::RAW_ENV_REFERENCE`). Each API exhibit gets its own command socket via `LWE_SOCKET` + `--api-socket`; live-class knobs (scene switch, isolator, the three registry instruments) reach it there, everything else relaunches only the affected slot, debounced (`dev.py::compose_env`, `dev.py::_schedule_relaunch`). The isolator set is session state: edited any time a slot has a scene, pushed over the exhibit's socket once it answers (`dev.py::_push_skip_when_ready`; a legacy build takes it as skip-object launch flags), kept across a stop, cleared on a scene change (`dev.py::setObjectsOn`). The console is one read-only text surface over a session buffer the bridge holds (5,000 lines, oldest dropped first, `dev.py::_push`): both exhibits and the daemon journal land in it tagged by source, every line stamped with its receipt time, and a read of any size arrives whole, the read is a batch and never a discard. The journal is followed as JSON records (`dev.py::_journal_fields`): the surface shows the tag, the time and the message, the stamp, host and unit are kept as the raw line a copy yields, and priority warning and above carries the same mark stderr does; the first open pulls a backlog that fills the buffer. Lines never wrap or truncate, the surface scrolls both ways, and the substring filter searches the whole buffer. Crash residue replaces run history: the last exit code and last 2,000 lines per slot, each with its time, persist beside the slot config (`dev.py::_retire`); the console keeps a side's lines until that side launches again, prints a dated delineation at every launch (`dev.py::runStarted`), replays the persisted tail once at startup under a `Last run` header, and the slot card's Tail puts that block in view again, pinned to its side, without duplicating it (`dev.py::showTail`). Empty slots take the wallpaper the daemon is showing (`dev.py::_seed_scenes`). Any exhibit alive puts the deck in its Developer Bench state, subtitled with the mode. Participates in the one-engine-at-a-time conflict gate with bench/wizard (`app.py::DevBridge`).
- **Tests**: `test_dev_bridge.py`, `test_developer_view_ui.py`, `test_journal_and_toggles.py`, `test_engine_sync.py`, `test_deck_states.py`.

### Engine invocation builder + discovery utilities
- **What it does**: `engine/invocation.py` is the single definition of engine argv/env for mirror rendering and `--window` preview: flag order documented and built from `constants.ENGINE_FLAGS` (`--scaling` before `--screen-root` list, `--clamp` not `--clamping`, `--bg` last, env `LWE_CC`/`LWE_TIMESCALE`) (`invocation.py::LWE_CC`; flags table `constants.py::ENGINE_FLAGS`). `discover_cli.py` (`lwe-discover`) rebuilds per-wallpaper object/property indexes in pure Python (state/objindex, state/propindex).
- **Tests**: `test_invocation.py`, `test_discovery.py`, `test_texcomp.py`, `test_storage.py`, `test_light_mode.py`, `test_theme_bridge.py`, `test_themes.py`.

---

### Panel-read environment variables

Three `LWE_*` variables are read by the panel itself (not the engine): `LWE_SOCKET`, `LWE_BC7ENC`
(`texcomp.py::LWE_BC7ENC`) overrides the path to the `lwe_bc7enc` encoder shim. The panel also *writes* engine-side vars into the generated
`engine-env` file - see "Engine daemon systemd unit management" above.

### On-disk layout (all from `storage/paths.py` unless noted)
- `~/.config/lwe/` (XDG_CONFIG_HOME): `settings.conf` (shell-sourceable Tier A, schema `constants.py::SETTINGS_SCHEMA`), `tags.csv`, `wp/<id>.conf` (per-wallpaper, schema `constants.py::WP_SCHEMA`, the wallpaper's alias as `ALIAS`), `playlists/<slug>.conf`, `meta.json`, `discover.json`, `theme.json`, `engine-env` (generated, `daemon_unit.py::write_files`), `pause-blacklist.txt`, `app-condition.txt`, `legacy/` (deleted-playlist backups), `locks/` (one lock file per store that its writers hold while they read, change and write it, plus `sync.lock`, which guards pushes to the engine, and `marker.lock`, which guards the sync marker; never deleted or replaced, `lock.py::held`).
- `~/.local/state/lwe/panel/`: `objindex/`, `propindex/`, `records/<wid>.jsonl`, `draft/`, `dev-slots.json` (exhibit slot config + crash residue, `dev.py::_slots_file`), `sync-pending` (the sync marker: BUNDLE while the engine needs a full sync from the store, CURRENT while the wallpaper on screen also needs a re-show, with the generation a clear must match, `engine/marker.py::writing`, and the pid of the engine that last took a whole bundle, `engine/marker.py::record_served`); `~/.local/state/lwe/logs/`: `panel/window.log`, `panel/tray.log`, `developer/<side>.log`, `bench/bench.log` (`paths.py::log_dir`).
- `~/.local/share/lwe/`: `assets/`, `wallpapers/` (library), `manual/` (hand-added pending) (`paths.py::default_wallpapers_dir`), `probes/` (user-supplied probe scenes, `dev.py::_probes_dir`), `dev-binaries/` (alternative engine builds for the Developer view, `dev.py::_binaries_dir`).
- `~/.config/systemd/user/lwe-engine.service` (`daemon_unit.py::write_files`); `~/.config/autostart/lwe-ui.desktop` (`models.py::XDG_CONFIG_HOME`).
- Sockets: `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/engine.sock` (engine API, `LWE_SOCKET` override, `/tmp/lwe-<euid>/engine.sock` when no runtime dir is set, found as the engine finds it, `api_client.py::socket_path`), `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/exhibit-a.sock` / `exhibit-b.sock` (Developer view exhibits, `dev.py::sock_path`) and `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/ui.sock` (single-instance).
- systemd user units touched: **`lwe-engine.service`** (written, enabled/disabled, restarted, stopped/started).

### Tests note
The panel has no C++ coverage under `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Cases/` (those cases are engine-side). Its coverage is the 56 script suites in `lwe-ui/tests/test_*.py`, run offscreen per `README.md` (README usage confirmed by test filenames; harness claim itself is README prose, not verified by me).

### 5-line summary
`lwe-ui/` is an entirely fork-added PySide6+QML control panel (`python -m lwe_ui`) that manages a Wallpaper Engine library: browsing, playlists/rotation, per-wallpaper settings with realtime autosave, a test-bench + import wizard for new Workshop items, and a Developer view for benching and comparing engine builds. It runs as two processes through one entry point: `--tray` is a small resident tray (icon, quick actions, launcher; no QML - what autostart runs), and the bare command is the full window, which exits on close and returns its memory; the repurposed "minimize to tray when closed" setting decides whether a window close leaves the tray running. It drives the fork engine live over a JSON-lines Unix socket (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/lwe/engine.sock`: `show`, `rotate-set`, `set-speed/fps/volume/skip/tuning/instrument/fullscreen/app-conditions...`). It owns the `lwe-engine.service` systemd user unit and its generated env file, and persists everything under `~/.config/lwe/` (shell-sourceable confs) plus state/data dirs. Coupling to upstream code is zero - it is a separate package whose only dependency is the fork's engine-side daemon API and flags, so cherry-picking it requires the engine daemon work first. Coverage is via ~60 Python test scripts in `lwe-ui/tests/`, not the C++ test tree.

---

# 10. Cherry-pick guide

Ranked by what a porter would actually have to take, using the coupling assessments from
each chapter. "Self-contained" means new files plus at most a few call-site lines.

### Install route
- **What it does**: `install.sh` installs through `cmake --install`, which copies the fork's binaries with their permissions from `install(TARGETS)`, and links the engine into `~/.local/bin` both as `linux-wallpaperengine` and as `lwe`, the name under which it is the shell client (chapter 1, "The `lwe` name"). Every binary carries the run path `$ORIGIN:$ORIGIN/lib:$ORIGIN/lib64` in the build tree as well as installed (CMakeLists.txt::CMAKE_BUILD_WITH_INSTALL_RPATH), so it finds its libraries beside itself and never in the working directory, and nothing is rewritten at install time; a separate build run path would end in an empty entry, which the loader reads as the working directory. Every rule of the fork's own sits in one install component and only that component installs, which skips the vendored projects' rules (kissfft benchmarks and tests, the quickjs and glslang tools, the SPIRV-Cross CLI) (CMakeLists.txt::install). The Arch package list carries glm, which the engine includes from more than fifty files (`packaging/archlinux/PKGBUILD`). The package links `/usr/bin/lwe` straight to `/opt/linux-wallpaperengine/linux-wallpaperengine`, not through the `/usr/bin/linux-wallpaperengine` wrapper, so `lwe` keeps its own name and the caller's working directory.
- **Tests**: `src/WallpaperEngine/Testing/Cases/RunPath.cpp` reads the run path of the built engine and its library beside the tests binary (DT_RUNPATH, else DT_RPATH) and refuses any entry that is not an absolute folder, `$ORIGIN` or `$ORIGIN/` followed by a folder.

## Tier 1 - clean lifts (self-contained; hours, not days)

| Capability | Take | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| builtins.js script library (Vec2-4/Mat3/Mat4, guarded fallbacks) | `Scripting/resources/builtins.js` | zero native coupling; build machinery already upstream |
| BinaryParser hardening (fail-fast reads, seek bounds, package/texture caps) | `BinaryReader.*`, `MemoryStream.h`, `PackageParser` + `TextureParser` hunks, `Testing/Cases/BinaryParserHardening.cpp` | same public API; test suite included |
| Lenient JSON (`parseLenient`, comment/trailing-comma tolerance, numeric-string coercion, exception-safe optionals) | `Data/JSON.{cpp,h}` + one-line call-site swaps | changes parse semantics engine-wide (silent defaulting) - port deliberately |
| Directory-adapter containment fix | `FileSystem/Adapters/Directory.cpp` `within()` | security fix; upstream's prefix check matches sibling dirs |
| Case-insensitive package index | `FileSystem/Adapters/Package.{cpp,h}` | fixes Windows-authored case drift |
| AlbumTexture readback crash fix (glGetnTexImage -> glGetTexImage) | `Render/AlbumTexture.cpp::copyContents` | one hunk; fixes a NULL-function-pointer segfault |
| Per-object sound volume | `CSound.cpp::LWE_NOOBJVOL`, `AudioStream` volume member, `SDLAudioDriver.cpp::SDL_MixAudioFormat` | 4 files, one call chain |
| MPV resource caps (demuxer/thread/hwdec defaults) | `VideoPlayback/MPV/GLPlayer.cpp::LWE_MPV_DEMUX_MB` | self-contained; cuts VRAM with NVDEC |
| PointerMoveGate (dedup/unknown-pointer gate) | `Input/PointerMoveGate.*` + its test | class lifts alone; wiring needs the normalized-pointer interface |
| NullAudioDriver + lazy audio bring-up | `Audio/Drivers/NullAudioDriver.*`, `AudioContext` reference->pointer diff, `WallpaperApplication::setupAudio/ensureAudioForProject` | no SDL device until a shown wallpaper has sound |
| InstrumentRegistry (runtime-toggleable log gates) | `Logging/InstrumentRegistry.*` + test | consumers opt in per call site |
| OverlayLabel debug overlay | `Render/OverlayLabel.*` + one call line in `CWallpaper::render` | self-contained (FreeType is already linked upstream) |
| Steam workshop multi-root discovery | `FileSystem.cpp::workshopContentRoots` | one function, one call site |
| Texture-cache sole-owner eviction | `TextureCache::evictUnused` + its wrapper's two call sites | trivial |
| Sha256 utility | `Data/Utils/Sha256.h` | header-only, stated to match `hashlib.sha256` |
| Script-engine correctness fixes (dead `setInterval`/`setTimeout`, unwired layer `get_property`, ScriptPropertiesObject ref-count/exception shapes) | `EngineObject`, `ScriptableObjectAdapter`, `VectorAdapter`, `ScriptPropertiesObject` - per-file hunks | arguably pure upstream bug fixes; each file's hunks lift independently |
| Wayland fullscreen-detector live recount | `WaylandFullScreenDetector::recomputeRelevance` + base virtual + two call sites | makes ignore-list edits immediate |
| `web-frame-probe` verification tool | `tools/web-frame-probe.cpp` | links only the engine lib; needs the WebHelper stack to probe |

## Tier 2 - a subsystem at a time (one coherent bundle each)

| Bundle | Contents | Why it's a bundle |
|---|---|---|
| Command socket pair | `Api/CommandServer.*` + `Api/CommandDispatcher.*` | fully self-contained transport+validation - but useless without the WallpaperApplication handlers (Tier 3) |
| Wayland poll loop + keepalive + DPMS safety | `WaylandOpenGLDriver::dispatchEventQueue`, `WaylandOutputViewport` framePending/swapInterval, `WallpaperApplication` render-stamp | replaces upstream's blocking dispatch; needs `getApiWakeFds` hook or a stub |
| Output hotplug/hot-unplug | `handleGlobalRemoved`/`onLayerClose`/`onOutputAnnounced` blocks | moderate; independent of the socket |
| Camera corrections (perspective projection, zoom-corrected ortho, scripted view, screen projection) | `Render/Camera.*` + consumer hunks in CImage/CText/CModel | changes every 2D scene's framing; not drop-in without the canvas/view split |
| FBO pooling + coverage sizing + clampToCap | CScene pool methods, `clampToCap`/`largestOutputSize`, CWallpaper virtual, CImage coverage block | safe to drop pooling alone (legacy fallback branch), but coverage sizing is woven through FBO creation |
| HDR bloom ladder | CScene ctor block, CFBO RGBA16F, CWallpaper `m_sceneFormat`, AssetLocator shader injection, Wallpaper data fields | the ladder excises as a unit if the data fields come along |
| Mip-residency cap + demand expansion | `Render/MipResidency.*`, TextureCache 4-line block, CTexture startLevel/rebase (both upload paths), one CImage hook | needs retained LZ4 mip payloads (data-layer change) |
| BC7 texcache ingest | `tools/texcomp/lwe_bc7enc.cpp`, `CTexture.cpp::uploadFromTexcache`, CMake block; vendored ISPCTextureCompressor (MIT) | x86-64 only (ispc); cache producer lives in lwe-ui |
| Puppet skeletal animation | `PuppetModel.*` + the CImage puppet block | replaces upstream's static puppet loader |
| 3D model objects | `CModel.*`, `ModelObject` data struct + parser entry, CScene factory branch | lit models also need the lighting stack (Tier 3) |
| Text object fixes (UTF-8, alignment, width limits, placement) | `CText.*` + `Camera::getScreenProjection` | self-contained otherwise |
| Script cursor events | ScriptEngine hook scan + dispatch + `CImage::cursorLocalPosition` | only image layers report hits |
| Persistent localStorage | `Scripting/LocalStorageObject.*` + three ScriptEngine wiring points | keep install order: builtins first, native overwrite |
| Web stack (three-binary CEF split) | all of `WebHelper/`, the two new mains, rewritten `WebBrowser/` classes, `CWeb.*`, `CWallpaper::fromWallpaper` signature change, CMake targets | coherent module; the factory signature is the invasive part |
| AnimationTimeline (keyframed property animations) | `Data/Model/AnimationTimeline.*`, parser hook, CScene ticker | data half lifts alone; needs CScene ticker to run |

## Tier 3 - deeply woven (take the subsystem or nothing)

- **The daemon command handlers** (`WallpaperApplication.cpp` `handleApiCommand` and
  everything below it: `applyShowCore`, `rebuildForCurrentBackgrounds`, rotation engine,
  release/acquire state machine, deadman, status aggregation - ~1,844 new lines). Every
  handler reads/writes settings, render context, detector, and instrument state. This is
  the single largest porting cost in the fork - but it is also the daemon itself.
- **The lighting stack** (CLight + CScene light/shadow stage + CPass light staging +
  `ShaderUnit::generateLightingV1` + CRenderable virtuals). The GLSL module calls WE's
  own `ComputePBRLightShadow*` from wallpaper shader includes, so it only links in
  shaders that carry WE's lighting headers.
- **The particle rewrite** (`CParticle.{h,cpp}`, ~1,900 diff lines: child systems,
  events, instance overrides, trail ribbons, buffer pooling). `EmitterFunc` signature
  changed; not liftable piecemeal except the small rate/startTime/prewarm gates.
- **Composition-layer subtree rendering** (CScene `renderFrame` restructure + CImage
  composition FBO/alias logic). Must move as one piece.

## What a porter should NOT take

The ~40 pure log-probe env vars (`LWE_*DUMP`, `LWE_*PROBE`, ...) are development
instrumentation; take the 5-6 you need while debugging a port, leave the rest. The
handful of unconditional diagnostics listed in "Known rough edges" should be dropped or
gated in any port.

---

# 11. Known rough edges

Listed because this document is meant to be honest, and because upstream readers will
find them anyway:

- **Unconditional diagnostics that fire on normal operation** - these look
  like leftover development instrumentation and are candidates for env-gating:
  the `LWE-DEBUG` projection dump in the CScene ctor (`CScene.cpp::DEBUG`, error level),
  `LWE-SCENEFB` for the first 3 frames (`CScene.cpp::SCENEFB`, error level),
  and `LWE-TIMELINE` logged per animated property (`CScene.cpp::TIMELINE`, out level).
  (`LWE-PRESENT` used to be on this list; it is now env-gated behind `LWE_PRESENTTRACE`
  and demoted to out level.)
- **`LWE_LIGHTDUMP` contains a hardcoded wallpaper-specific trace** (`angles_112`,
  `ScriptEngine.cpp::LWE_LIGHTDUMP`).
- **Test coverage is concentrated in the new infrastructure** (command socket,
  dispatcher, binary parsers, instrument registry, pointer gate, web-helper startup).
  The render, scripting, audio, and video chapters have no `Testing/Cases/` coverage;
  behavioral confidence there comes from live burn-in, not the suite.
- **Observation-derived formats**: the MDLV submesh layouts, puppet clip padding, and
  point-light shadow face order are reconstructed from observation, not specs - each is
  marked Uncertain in its chapter. They are guarded against malformed input but their
  native-parity claims rest on measurement, not documentation.
- **`VirtualAdapter::open` returns a shared stream with a shared cursor** - safe only
  because current usage is strictly sequential on one thread.
